# Decisions

Choices made while building, each with its context, two real options, the choice
and what would change it. "Plan" is the build plan, cited by section. Architecture
decisions the plan itself records (its Section 8, Decisions 1 to 30) are not
repeated here; an entry here settles something the plan leaves to the builder
(plan 5.8) or a choice the code needed.

## D-01 Language and dependencies (plan 5.8, B-7)
- Context: the tool wraps Python pipelines, reads numpy index files and needs SQLite.
- Options: (a) Python 3.12, SQLite through the standard library, numpy as the one
  runtime dependency; (b) a compiled single binary (Rust or Go) with bindings.
- Choice: (a).
- Why: a team's capture points are Python call sites (the tokenizer, the training
  launch), so wrappers in the same language are one import; (b) would need a second
  language at every capture point.
- What would change it: a measured query or walk time (plan 5.6) beyond what a team
  can use on its corpus would move the proof walk, not the wrappers, to compiled code.

## D-02 Content digest (plan 3.1)
- Context: the digest conditions compare a stored digest with a file (plan 2.2(d), 3.4).
- Options: (a) SHA-256; (b) BLAKE2b for everything.
- Choice: (a) for content digests, BLAKE2b for fingerprints (plan Decision 11) and ids.
- Why: a team can check a file digest with the tools it already has, and a run
  record written by another program can carry it.
- What would change it: digest time dominating ingest in the measured cells (plan 5.6).

## D-03 Hash and width of the instance id (plan 5.8, B-2a)
- Context: an instance id names an artifact's content and history (plan 3.1, Decision 29).
- Options: (a) BLAKE2b, 32 bytes; (b) BLAKE2b, 16 bytes, the fingerprint's width.
- Choice: (a).
- Why: an instance id is stored once per artifact, not once per record (occurrences
  refer to their artifact by row), so the wider id costs nothing per record, and a
  collision between two histories would merge their attributions.
- What would change it: ids that must be typed or read aloud by people; then a
  shorter printed prefix, not a shorter id.

## D-04 How a file, a snapshot entry or a query is tied to one instance (plan 5.8, B-2b)
- Context: two instances can share their bytes (plan Decision 29), and the digest
  conditions stay on the content digest.
- Options: (a) every artifact is registered under a name; a snapshot entry stores
  the instance id beside the path and digest; a query names a name or an instance
  id, and a query by path or digest that matches several instances is refused with
  "ambiguous artifact"; (b) the path is the binding: one path, one instance.
- Choice: (a).
- Why: under (b) a file rebuilt at the same path would silently take over the old
  instance's history, the error plan Decision 27 names for shard lists.
- What would change it: teams that never register names; then the instance id
  printed at registration is the only handle and (a) still holds.

## D-05 Tokenization-map links are not part of a shard's identity (plan 5.8, B-2c)
- Context: a shard's map can be recovered or verified after registration; if its
  links counted among the shard's edge records, a later map would make a new
  instance and orphan the step traces and snapshots keyed to the old one.
- Options: (a) identity covers only what the producing step stated as it ran (its
  inputs and its stated edges); a link derived afterwards (a recovered edge, a
  position or token-matching map) is evidence about the instance, stored in its own
  namespace, and never changes the id; (b) every link counts, and a later map makes
  a new instance to which traces and snapshots are re-keyed.
- Choice: (a).
- Why: under (b) a recovery, which adds evidence, would change which artifact the
  run is said to have read. Immutable provenance ends at what the step stated;
  everything derived later is a correction of evidence and is versioned by namespace.
- What would change it: a tokenizer that writes ids itself. Its links would be
  stated by the step and would belong to the identity.

## D-06 Release rows carry no source id (plan 5.8, B-3; Check 7)
- Context: Check 7 asks whether two releases with identical bytes and different
  histories are two instances. If a release row carried an id that marks its
  source, swapping the dedup winner would change the bytes and the question could
  not fail for the reason it was added.
- Options: (a) a release row is `{"text": ...}` only, and which input each row came
  from lives in the store's edges and in the replica's manifest; (b) rows carry a
  source-marking id.
- Choice: (a) for the fixture's releases. The generated sets' chunk files carry ids
  up to the tokenizer input, as plan Check 3 needs; the tool is never given them.
- What would change it: a pipeline whose releases must carry ids; the instance
  question would then be asked on a release whose ids are content-derived.

## D-07 A refused read does not break a generation edge's bound (plan 2.2(g), F10)
- Context: the harness logs every read; a job can ask for a record outside its
  hand-off and be refused.
- Options: (a) only a read outside the hand-off that the harness let through breaks
  the bound; a refused attempt is logged and listed with the proof; (b) any entry
  outside the hand-off breaks it.
- Choice: (a).
- Why: the bound says what the job read. A refused attempt delivered nothing, so
  under (b) one stray request would cost every output of the job its negative
  answers without the job having read anything.
- What would change it: a harness that cannot show a refusal delivered nothing.

## D-08 What a cache digest is taken over (plan 3.5, F3)
- Context: a replay counts only when its digests equal the ones recorded at
  training time; the cache file names and `description.txt` carry paths.
- Options: (a) the array: dtype, shape and bytes; (b) the `.npy` file's bytes.
- Choice: (a).
- Why: the `.npy` header can change with the numpy version while the array does
  not; (b) would reject a correct replay made with another numpy.
- What would change it: nothing found; (b) is strictly narrower.

## D-09 The fixture's tokenizer (plan 5.8, B-12)
- Context: the fixture holds English and German passages, so it needs a real
  tokenizer; Megatron's preprocessing must run it unchanged.
- Options: (a) GPT-2 BPE through Megatron's own tokenizer class; (b) Megatron's
  NullTokenizer over lines of integers.
- Choice: (a), with the tokenizer files fetched once into the environment and the
  run offline afterwards; the files are not committed.
- Why: (b) cannot tokenize prose, so the fixture's texts would have to be integers
  and the paraphrase and the translation would be labels, not texts.
- What would change it: a team tokenizer the fixture should mirror.

## D-10 The form of the hand-written table (plan 5.1, 5.8 B-7)
- Options: (a) TOML files, one per check, read by the tests with the standard
  library; (b) Markdown tables with a parser in the tests.
- Choice: (a).
- Why: a person can write and review TOML by hand, each row can carry its question
  and its source in words, and no parser of our own stands between the table and
  the test.
- What would change it: reviewers who cannot read it.

## D-11 Where an extension's switch lives (plan 1.4, Section 7)
- Options: (a) a setting in the store, off when absent; (b) a flag on each command.
- Choice: (a).
- Why: an answer depends on the generating-model switch, so two people who query
  one store must get one answer; under (b) the same store would answer differently
  by who typed what.
- What would change it: a need to compare on and off on one store without writing
  to it; then a read-only override that the answer prints.

## D-12 Two link tables: edges and map rows (plan 3.4)
- Context: a JSONL record links to parent records through a step's edge; a shard's
  document links to the line it was tokenized from through the shard's map.
- Options: (a) `edge` rows for JSONL children and `map_row` rows for shard
  documents, the map bound to its shard by count and digest in one transaction;
  (b) one edge table for both.
- Choice: (a).
- Why: a map is whole or absent and exists in two namespaces (written at capture,
  recovered later), while an edge is per record; under (b) the count-and-digest
  binding and the namespace would have to be carried on every row.
- What would change it: a measured walk time that needs one index over both.

## D-13 A checkpoint answer for a document read only through a shared token (plan ledger id M9; Check 8)
- Context: a sample spans sequence_length + 1 tokens, so a step can read a document
  through one token only, the last token of a sample (plan 3.5, Decision 18). The
  fixture holds such a document and Check 1 compares its row with the oracle's log.
  Whether a checkpoint answer is also asked for it was left to the builder: run E's
  only affected record under "withdraw A" is T, so a row needs either a second
  affected record in run E, which can move the answers of checkpoints 6 and 9, or
  a new request.
- Options: (a) no Check 8 row; the document stays in the fixture, the table and
  Check 1 only; (b) a new request, a withdrawal narrowed to that one record (plan
  2.3 allows narrowing to a list of records), with the checkpoint at or after the
  step that read its token expected to answer "contains" with its basis, and an
  earlier checkpoint "does not contain".
- Choice: (b), on a record of source B or Z in shards 1 or 2, whichever the seed
  search fixes; the request names that record alone, so no answer under
  "withdraw A" moves.
- Why: under (a) a join that keeps the one-token row and an answer rule that drops
  it would pass every check; (b) asks the question at the answer, where Decision 18
  is promised.
- What would change it: a ruling that a one-token read is no exposure (Decision 18's
  own "what would change it"); the row's expected answer would then follow that rule.

## D-14 A second registration that disagrees with a stored instance is refused (plan 3.1, Decision 29)
- Context: the instance id covers the content, the step, the inputs, the parents and the selected parent. Two registrations can agree on all of these and differ elsewhere (which handed records were in scope only, a label, a harness log).
- Options: (a) return the stored instance and refuse, with a named error, any difference in the fields the id does not cover; (b) put every field into the id.
- Choice: (a).
- Why: the plan fixes the id's inputs (3.1); under (b) a corrected label would make a new artifact. Under (a) nothing is dropped silently.
- What would change it: a ruling that in-scope-only records are part of an artifact's history.

## D-15 A stated edge's parent lies in a declared input (plan 3.1)
- Options: (a) the store refuses a stated edge whose parent is in no input of the step and not in the artifact itself; (b) any registered record may be a parent.
- Choice: (a). A recovered edge, written after registration, may name any registered record.
- Why: the inputs are part of the instance id; a parent outside them would be history the id does not cover.
- What would change it: a step whose inputs cannot be listed when it runs.

## D-16 What a shuffle digest covers (plan 3.5, the ruling on the cyclic order)
- Options: (a) per launch, epoch and rank, the whole epoch's order for that rank, whatever part the launch read; (b) only the indices the launch drew.
- Choice: (a).
- Why: the plan records "each epoch's shuffle"; (a) is one value per epoch and rank that a resume inside the epoch does not change, so the join compares the shuffle and not a slice of it.
- What would change it: an environment where the wrapper cannot drive the sampler past what the launch read.

## D-17 One definition of the normaliser (plan 3.1, F8)
- Options: (a) `control-chars/1` is defined once, in `waterwhite.normalise`, and the replica's sanitizer imports it in every mode; (b) the replica carries its own copy and a test compares the two.
- Choice: (a). It removes Unicode category Cc except tab and line feed, keeps category Cf, and strips a fixed list of white-space code points from the ends.
- Why: the tool re-runs the pipeline's sanitizer to match fingerprints (F8); two copies can drift, and a drift would show as lost links, not as a failed test.
- What would change it: a team sanitizer the tool must load by name; the registry already takes one.

## D-18 What each loud check compares (plan 3.4)
- Choice: the map check compares a binding with the store's own records (its declared digest with the artifact's registered digest, its declared count with its stored rows); the artifact check compares the registered digest with the file on disk, and a snapshot entry's digest with its shard's registered digest. An artifact whose file is gone is not a fault: the records still answer.
- Other option: compare the binding with the file on disk as well; then one changed file would raise the map fault first and the two faults could not be told apart.
- What would change it: a team that needs a gone file to stop a query; that would be a setting, not a default.

## D-19 A release row that copies a withdrawn source's own record (plan 4.1)
- Context: with dedup off, a release row made from A's record has no equivalent copy to take its place; the plan's actions name no separate word for it.
- Options: (a) "retire": the row is a descendant of the withdrawn record through a single-parent copy edge; (b) a sixth word, "remove".
- Choice: (a). The report's count of records removed is the sum of what leaves the successor.
- What would change it: a ruling that a source's own rows need their own action word.

## D-20 A shard can hold one recovery map per derivation mode (plan 3.3, Check 3)
- Context: plan Check 3 asks questions of the position-mode recovered map and of the token-matching recovered map of the same shard.
- Options: (a) a binding is unique per shard, namespace and derivation, so both recovery maps can be stored and compared; (b) one recovery map per shard, the later replacing the earlier.
- Choice: (a). The order in which a reader takes the maps is in D-24.
- What would change it: a Check 3 that asks only one recovery map of a shard.

## D-21 A job's sole handed record is a parent (plan 3.1, 3.3, Decision 25)
- Context: plan 3.3 names as parents "the sole input or each input the output states it used".
- Options: (a) when a job is handed exactly one record, that record is a parent of each output even when the output states nothing, and with two or more handed records only the stated ones are parents; (b) stated records only, so that a job with one handed record and silent outputs gives "in scope only".
- Choice: (a), the plan's own wording.
- What would change it: a ruling that an output's own statement is needed even for one handed record.

## D-22 Readings of the plan the hand-written table rests on (plan 2.2, 2.4, 3.2, 3.4, Check 5)
Each reading decides rows of the table; fixture/table/README.md section 9 names them.
- Reading 1 (plan 2.2(a), 2.4, 4.5): a checkpoint is answered from the records its steps consumed and their ancestry, so a fault on a record no step of it read leaves its answer. Other reading: a checkpoint answers for every record of its mixtures; a fault on an unread record would then lower its answer. What would change it: a ruling for the other reading; the rows of Y@6 and K@3 under seven fault states move.
- Reading 2 (plan 2.2 closing paragraph, Decision 20): a recorded chain that shows "contains" wins over a failed condition, for a mixture as for a shard or a checkpoint. Other reading: the parenthesis of plan Check 5 to the letter, everything downstream of a deleted map "may contain" except consumed evidence. What would change it: a ruling for the parenthesis; five mixture rows move.
- Reading 3 (plan 3.4, Check 5): a loud fault stops the queries whose artifact has the faulted record in its ancestor closure, and the listing; other artifacts answer. Other reading: a loud fault anywhere stops every query of the store. What would change it: a ruling that "whatever the answer would have been" (plan 2.2) means every query; 14 rows under the corrupted digest move to stops.
- Reading 4 (plan 3.2, L1): a file-level link decides a request about the whole source: the file answers "contains"; each of its records answers "may contain" when the request is narrowed to it, and the rebuild report lists it as unresolved (plan 4.1). Other reading: the file answers "may contain" until a record-level link exists. What would change it: a ruling that file-family evidence is not enough for "contains".

## D-23 The generated sets also hold paraphrases of unrelated records (a marked addition to plan 5.1, ledger id BF-1)
- Context: found during the build. In the generated sets every generated record derived from A, as in the hand-checked fixture, so the rebuild check (plan Check 9) could not tell a report that retires by lineage from one that retires every generated record.
- Options: (a) the 10k and 100k sets also hold a few paraphrases made from unrelated records of Z, inside the totals and in the set's R1, each made like P (closed-input harness, generating model "none") so its parent link is established and none is unresolved; under "withdraw A" each stays in the successor on the repaired side and on the reference side, the plant list names them and the counting script counts them as kept; (b) the sets repeat the fixture's structure to the letter.
- Choice: (a), by a ruling on the plan, which now carries it at 5.1 "Sizes". It is an addition to "repeat the fixture's structure"; the hand-checked fixture, its spec and its table are unchanged, and nothing else depends on these records, so a reversal only regenerates the two sets.
- The test: one red run in which a report that retires one of them makes the two sides differ.
- Order (plan W5): the plant generator and the counting script change first, written by someone who writes no tool code, before any code that computes actions on those sets.
- What would change it: the owner reversing the ruling.

## D-24 Amendments after the second integration
- D-13 amended (plan Check 8, 3.5; ledger id M9): the record read only through a shared token is an unrelated record of Z in shard 3 of run E. In shards 1 and 2 it cannot exist: every sample of those shards that segment 2 reads in steps 7 to 9 was already read in segment 1 with the same seed, so no record there is read for the first time between checkpoints 6 and 9. The request stays a withdrawal narrowed to that one record. Other option: keep shards 1 and 2 and ask only for a "contains" at checkpoint 6, with no checkpoint before the read; that loses the "does not contain" before it. What would change it: a fixture whose first segment does not read shards 1 and 2 whole before checkpoint 6.
- D-20 amended (plan 3.3, 2.2(f)): across namespaces a verified map comes before an unverified one; among verified maps the order is capture, position-mode recovery, token-matching recovery. An unverified map is still the shard's own record, and its links are "unknown". Other option: the capture map first whatever its verification; a shard with a failed capture map and a verified recovery map would then answer from "unknown" links. What would change it: a ruling that the capture namespace always outranks recovery.
- The fixture's X, P and T are short enough that T fits whole inside one packed sample as its second document (plan 5.1, Check 1: "the sample that holds T"). Other option: a long T spread over several samples, with the plan's sentence read as "T's first tokens"; that mixes the split-document case into every statement about T. What would change it: a reading of plan 5.1 in which "the sample that holds T" means the first of several.
- An edge keeps the name of its job (plan 3.3, Check 5, which asks that an answer name the job when the job's log is gone). Other option: the name only in the log; the answer could then name only the step. What would change it: plan Check 5 dropping its question about a removed log.
- The order of causes in a checkpoint's "may contain" is the order of its history: the failing link at the earliest step is named, and the list order of plan 2.4 decides only inside one step (plan 2.2: "the first failing link"). Other option: the list order of plan 2.4 over the whole history; the faulted link of an early step would then be hidden behind a later untraced step. What would change it: a ruling for the other order; four rows of Check 5 (the deleted edge and the file-level edge, asked of E@9 and of D@3) would then name the untraced step 8.
- A file-level link never supports "does not contain" for another source: a record whose only evidence is a file-level link has no recorded origin for any source the link does not name (plan 2.2(a), 3.2). Other option: read "decides requests about the whole file or source" as deciding the negative too; a file family would then vouch for what else the file holds. What would change it: a source format in which a file family is a closed statement of everything the file holds.
- A listing prints an answer for every registered artifact, a "not covered" one included, and names in its not-covered list every artifact that has no record of its own (plan 2.1, Decision 26). Other option: answers for releases, shards, mixtures and checkpoints only; a source file with no record of its own would then be missing from the list that is meant to show it. What would change it: a store so large that a listing must be asked by kind; that would be a filter, not a narrower default.
- The round-robin variant's second segment resumes from a registered checkpoint, E-rr@6; no answer is tabulated for it (plan 3.1, 4.5). Other option: register the second segment with no parent checkpoint; the store would then hold a resume whose start nothing registers. What would change it: the plan tabulating a checkpoint answer on the round-robin layout; its rows are then written first.

## D-25 Amendments after the third integration
- D-24 amended (plan 5.1, Check 1; D-13): the sample that shows the shared-token record is not the sample that holds T. Without this the smallest seed puts the record's one token as the third row of T's own sample, and the two rows of Check 1 that show T and the shared token compare the same log lines, so a fault in one-token rows fails both and neither tells the cause. Other option: D-24 to the letter, one sample showing both; a smaller seed, a weaker pair of rows. What would change it: a ruling that one sample should show both cases.
- "Read only through the shared token" is asked up to run E's last checkpoint, step 9 (plan 5.1, Check 8; D-13). The record is read again, whole, at step 10, after that checkpoint; no answer of the table is asked about a state after step 9, so the Check 8 rows stand. Other option: ask it of all 12 steps; that changes run E's seed and every position, and decides no answer. What would change it: a ruling that the sentence of plan 5.1 must hold for the whole run.
- The log shows that T is whole inside one sample by three things: T's row runs from token 0 to its token count, a third row follows it, and run E reads T in exactly one row (plan 5.1, 3.5, F9). The third row rules out that T's last token is the sample's shared last token. Other option: carry every document's length from the shard index into the search evidence and compare; the sentence then carries no number, and the search reads one more file. What would change it: a fixture whose T changes often.
- The spec reader refuses a resume inside a run whose named checkpoint the segment before does not register at its last step (plan 3.1, F4). Other option: take the name as stated; a misspelt name would then pass as a checkpoint from outside the run and the start would go unchecked. What would change it: a fixture run that resumes from a checkpoint registered elsewhere.
- The generated sets hold max(2, records // 1000) paraphrases of unrelated records (plan 5.1 "Sizes", ledger id BF-1; Check 9; D-23): 10 in the 10k set, 100 in the 100k set, the same share in both, and never fewer than two, so a wrong report can retire one while another stays. Each replaces one unrelated line of Z, and the rule "the first half of Z is R1's" stays, so R1 gains the new rows and each release loses half as many Z lines. A set with 0 derivations (the measured cells of plan 5.6) holds none: it has no generation job. Other options: one fixed number at every size (the 100k set would hold them at a tenth of the share); taking every replaced line from R1's half (the releases' shares of Z would no longer be halves); planting them in the 0-derivation cells (those cells would then carry generation edges). What would change it: "a few" read as the same number in both sets, or the owner wanting the measured cells to hold them.
- A mixture whose snapshot holds no entry answers "not covered" with "record missing": the snapshot is the mixture's own record (plan 2.1, Decision 27). Other option: a fifth loud fault; plan 3.4 fixes the loud faults at four. What would change it: a change of the plan that names a fifth loud fault.
- A source link whose label is stronger than its derivation and verification allow fails condition (f) of plan 2.2 at read time, as a map's own label does (plan 3.4); a recorded "contains" through another chain still wins. Other option: trust the stored label; an edited label then gave "contains" or supported a negative. What would change it: nothing found; the write path already refuses such a label.
- An extension switch holds "on" or "off" and nothing else: any other value is refused when written and when read (plan 1.4, L7). Other option: read every other value as off; a store set to "ON" then answered as if the extension were off. What would change it: an extension that needs more states than two.
- A version-1 store file written before an edge kept its job name is refused at open as "not a store" (plan 3.4). The schema stays at version 1 until the first milestone that hands a store to someone; from then on a change of the schema raises the version and adds a migration step. Other option: raise the version now; no store written by an earlier build exists outside test folders. What would change it: a store handed out before the schema is final. Amended by D-30: from version 2 on, every change of the schema raises the version and adds a migration step (plan 3.4: versioned and migrated stepwise).
- A row of the table with one expected field that cannot be compared yet is not shown as a plain pass: the fields that can be compared are compared, and a skipped test of its own names the missing field. A row whose target is not built yet is skipped with the reason. Neither is a pass (plan 5.5, W5). Other option: skip the whole row; the milestone's own exit answer (R2 under "withdraw A") would then not run. What would change it: nothing found; once the rebuild report exists the field is compared and the rule has no case left.
- A run segment whose run record holds no launch supports no "does not contain": the proof cannot tell whether steps come before the first registered one (plan 2.2(e), 3.1, 3.5). The tool does not do this yet; it is built with the registration of runs, with a test written first. Other option: take the first step from the start kind (a resume: the parent checkpoint's step plus 1); that trusts a step count nothing recorded. What would change it: a trainer whose checkpoints record the first step of each launch (plan F4).
- A segment's trace cause is one of the three causes of plan 3.5 and nothing else; the store refuses another (plan 2.4, 3.5). Built with the trace join. Other option: any named cause, as now; a segment could then carry a cause its route cannot have. What would change it: a change of the plan that adds a cause.

## D-26 After the first milestone's exit checks
- D-18 amended (plan 2.2(d), 2.3, 3.4): a binding's declared count is also compared with the number of records the artifact's file holds, in the read that digests the file, when the file's digest is the registered one. A file whose digest differs is the artifact fault, as before, so the two faults stay apart. Other option: compare it with the store's own rows only, as D-18 had it; a record removed from the store together with a lowered count was then invisible to the proof, and the artifact answered "does not contain". For a file that is gone the count is compared with the store's rows only, and the answer's scope names the file as not checked. What would change it: a file format whose records cannot be counted in the read that digests it.
- A shard whose index file has its registered digest and cannot be read as an index gives the count fault, with no count on disk in its context (plan 3.4): only a store edited to match a broken file can reach this. Other option: the refusal "unsupported format"; an edited store would then be refused, not reported as a fault. What would change it: nothing found.
- A file that is gone stays no fault (D-18): its count is compared with the store's rows only, so a record removed from the store with a lowered count, with the file then moved away as well, is not found, and the artifact can answer "does not contain" with the file named in the answer's scope as not checked. Other options: a gone file in the closure stops the query; or the answer stands but no "does not contain" is given while a file of the closure is not checked. Under either, an artifact downstream of a source whose file was removed after its withdrawal could no longer answer "does not contain" for any other source. Choice: the first, as built: the answer stands and its scope names the file as not checked (`files_not_checked`); the test `test_a_negative_names_the_files_whose_digest_could_not_be_checked` in tests/coverage/test_coverage_proof.py shows it. What would change it: a team that must not rely on a store whose files are not all present; then the second or the third, as a setting.
- The process-evidence note (plan 2.1, 3.3): an artifact's answer carries the note when any chain that puts one of its records in scope passes a generation edge; the answer prints the first chain only. Other option: the note only when the printed chain passes a generation edge; R1's answer under "withdraw A" would then carry no note, although three of its four affected rows are in scope through generation edges alone. What would change it: an answer that prints every chain; the note then goes on each chain it is about.
- Two refusals beyond the one the plan names for a withdrawal (plan 2.3; plan 5.8, B-7, leaves further error names to the builder): "member not registered", for a withdrawal narrowed to a member its source does not have, and "record not in source", for one narrowed to a record that is no record of the source. Other option: answer as if the narrowing were empty; a mistyped member would then read as a clean "does not contain". What would change it: nothing found.
- A segment read with the cyclic sampler whose run record holds no shuffle digest supports no "does not contain" (plan 3.5, 2.4: the cause "cyclic order not recorded"), and a segment's trace route follows from its run record at registration and is not taken from the caller. Not built yet: built with the trace join, with a test written first. Other option: the caller states the route, as now; a store edited by hand, or a caller's mistake, could then give a cyclic segment a route its record does not support. What would change it: nothing found.
- The list of joins that plan Check 1 verified (plan 3.5, F6) is shipped with the tool once Check 1 has passed for a release, and a store takes it from the tool. Until then `init --verified-join` exists for tests, and docs/cli.md says so (amended by D-51: it now ships, and the flag accepts only joins of the shipped list). Other option: leave the list to whoever creates a store; a store could then name a join nobody verified. What would change it: a team that verifies a release itself; that would be a command that runs Check 1 and records its result, not a free-text flag. Built in D-51: the list ships, and a store made by `init` takes it from the tool.
- Where the "verify first" items of the build's start stand at this milestone: F1 is shown on the fixture (one document per line that gives tokens, the end-of-document token inside the span; the spec records it). F5 is shown: Megatron's tokenizer, dataset and sampler run on the fixture with no GPU (the tests marked megatron). F6: the release is pinned to core_v0.20.0 and its commit is recorded by the environment's setup script; the join's version gate is built with the join. F8: the sanitizer is the tool's named normaliser (D-17) and can be re-run. Megatron-LM's licence files were read at the pinned release: the LICENSE file gives a three-clause BSD text for the project's own files and lists third-party parts, while the package metadata names "Apache 2.0" in one field and a BSD classifier in another; the record is inconsistent, as the plan says (plan Section 7), so no Megatron code ships: the tool imports none, and the oracle, which is test infrastructure, imports the installed package. F2, F3, F4, F7, F9, F10, the index-file reading of 3.5, the round-robin answers of 4.5 and the two existing tools of Check 10 are checked in the milestones that build them.

## D-27 The harness's confinement (plan 5.8, B-1; F10, 3.3 "Generation", 2.2(g))
- Context: F10 names what the closed-input harness must give a job (a read-only hand-off of the handed records, no other record path, no record-bearing network source, the model client allowed). The plan leaves how to the builder (B-1); 2.2(g) and the core rows of Check 5 rest on it.
- Options: (a) the in-process harness alone (`waterwhite.harness`): it logs every read through its context but cannot say what else the job opened; (b) a job run out of process under bubblewrap (`waterwhite.confine`): every namespace new and required, no network at all, only the system library folders, the interpreter, the job's script and the client mounted read-only, the handed records served over a pipe and logged before they are answered, the model called by the harness on the job's behalf.
- Choice: (b) is built beside (a); both write the same log format with the same code, and a test runs one job both ways and compares the two logs byte for byte. Where `bwrap` is missing or cannot build the sandbox, the run is refused with "sandbox unavailable" and nothing starts; there is no fallback to (a). `bwrap` is a system program, not a Python dependency: numpy stays the one runtime dependency. Not wired into the replica's build yet: its generation jobs still run in process. F10 stays unverified (B-1): the sandbox shows what this laptop's kernel allows, not a team's setup.
- What would change it: a platform with no user namespaces (the tool would then need another confinement there, or answer as with no bound); a model client that must run inside the job.
- Only a log the confined harness wrote establishes a bound (Decision 13: a bound is established only by a closed-input harness whose access log lists every record the job read; plan 3.3: the job can reach no other record or record source). The hand-off over a pipe is read as giving F10's first condition; F10 stays unverified. Other option: a read-only mount of the handed records beside the pipe, for jobs that need files; the job would open the mount itself, and its log could say what was handed, not what was read. Not built. What would change it: a team's jobs that must read their records as files and a way to log every open of the mount.
- AMENDED (the "Choice" paragraph, which says a test compares the two logs "byte for byte"): the two logs are equal byte for byte except the word the first entry carries for the harness, "in-process" or "confined", which D-30 records and which tests/confine/test_confine_job.py::test_the_log_is_the_in_process_harness_log_but_for_the_harness_it_names asserts (the confined bytes equal the in-process bytes with that one word replaced). Other option: logs equal in every byte across harnesses (the option D-30 names). Result of the other option (D-30's own statement, traced): a stored bound would look the same whether the job could open other files or not. What would change it: a confined harness whose log must record something the in-process harness cannot (for example the mounts it gave the job), so that the two logs differ in more than the harness word and the test compares only the part both write. [written at the M4 check]

## D-28 Tokenization maps: capture and recovery (plan 3.3 "Tokenization", Decision 4, F1, 3.4)
- When position mode fails, every mode that ran leaves its map: the failed position map ("unknown") stands beside the token-matching map, and a reader takes the verified one (D-20, D-24; `c03.verification-failed` expects both failed recovery maps). Other option: only the map of the mode that finally built it; the store would then not say what was tried. Amended (D-30): a map that did not verify no longer holds its namespace and derivation; a later map of the same namespace and derivation is written beside it, at most one map per namespace and derivation is verified, and a verified map is final. What would change it: a rule that a failed verification must block its slot as evidence against retrying.
- A team's tokenizer function returns a text's tokens without the end-of-document token; the tool appends it where the recorded convention puts it in the span. Other option: the document exactly as the preprocessing writes it; the convention would then not be held against every document by the tool. What would change it: a framing other than one appended id (a start token, two end tokens).
- A span is always the document's whole sequence in the shard's index, and the recorded flag says whether that sequence ends with the end-of-document token (as the oracle's F1 probe defines it). Other option: a span shortened by one token when the flag is off; span sizes would then not equal the index, which Check 3 asks. What would change it: a convention that puts the token in a sequence of its own.
- In position mode a line that gives no tokens is read from the index (a repeated document boundary with no sequence), and the verification confirms that the line gives no tokens again. Other option: tokenize every line again while the map is built. What would change it: a tokenizer that leaves no boundary for an empty line; position mode then fails its count and token matching takes over.
- Token-matching verification requires the lines with tokens and the documents to be the same collection of token sequences (no document without a line, no line without a document, equal numbers for identical texts). Other option: only that every document has a line; an identical pair with one line could then not carry its candidate set of two. What would change it: a recovery input known to be a superset of the tokenizer's input.
- Drops are written only when one of the maps verified, since the no-tokens drops and the fit of the sanitizer's log rest on the count the verification checks. Other option: always, as the step's own record. What would change it: a ruling that the sanitizer's log stands on its own.
- The wrapper refuses a shard that already lies at its prefix before the tokenizer runs; `map_tokenized_shard` is the entry point for a shard that exists (a pipeline that tokenizes before its capture point). Other option: overwrite in place. What would change it: pipelines that rerun in place.
- A recovery reads either the file the tokenizer read, or the file the sanitizer read with the sanitizer's log (logged lines become drops; the others pass through the normaliser again before they are tokenized, F8). Other option: only the tokenizer's input. What would change it: the copy-step recovery always registering the tokenizer's input and owning the sanitizer's drops.
- With F1 said not to hold and no tokenizer function, the recovery is refused (token matching cannot be derived); position mode with no tokenizer writes a map whose verification is "not run". Other option: a token-matching map marked "not run". What would change it: a wish for a spans-only record in that case.

## D-29 Recovery of copy steps and of the sanitizer without capture (plan 3.3, 5.4, Check 6, L3)
- A found file is registered with its pipeline role ("intermediate" by default, or "release") and no producing step; its records have no recorded origin until a recovery links them, so a query answers "may contain", "missing origin" (2.2(a)). "import" stays for a text-only import. Other options: the role "import" for every found file (the first build of this package; a release found afterwards would then be missing from a listing's releases), or a new word "found" (a listing could still not say whether it is a release). Amended by D-30. What would change it: a listing or report rule that must treat found files differently from captured ones beyond having no step.
- A "candidates" edge has the parent mode "any" with every candidate a parent and none selected. Other option: mode "all" with the label alone marking it; "all" would say every candidate was used, which is false. What would change it: rebuild actions that read an "any" edge with no selected parent differently from a candidate set.
- The sanitizer's recovery takes its candidates from the store's fingerprints (taken after the normaliser), then runs the normaliser on both texts and compares them (derivation "function"; plan 3.1 defines identity as equal after normalisation). Other option: the normaliser on the earlier text only, compared with the later raw line; a later file written by a slightly different sanitizer build would then get no links although its fingerprints match. What would change it: a team sanitizer that is not idempotent.
- A line the sanitizer's log names as skipped is no candidate parent. Other option: keep it as a candidate; the store would then hold a drop for a line and name it as a possible parent at once. What would change it: evidence that a team's log line numbers are unreliable, since a wrong "recovered" edge can support "does not contain".
- Without the normaliser the sanitizer's recovery writes no record-level link. It writes a file-level link from the later file to the members the earlier file is tied to as a whole only where a file family of the later file, or the sanitizer's own log (the earlier file's lines less the logged ones equal the later file's lines), supports it; the link's evidence names the log's count. With no log, a log whose count does not fit, or an earlier file tied to no member, the outcome is "unknown" and the file answers "may contain", "missing origin". The log's drops are recorded either way. Other option: the link on the caller's word alone that the two files belong together (the first build of this package); a wrong pair would then write a "contains" that cannot be removed. Amended by D-30. What would change it: a sanitizer log that names its output file.
- A surplus copy (the later file holds a text more often than the named inputs do) gets no edge: each copy answers "may contain", "missing origin" for every source the file does not hold through another record, and the result counts the copies. Other options: a "recovered" or "candidates" edge to the found parents (the first build of this package), which lets a copy that came from an unnamed file support "does not contain" for that file's source; or an edge labelled "unknown", which a later, correct run could not replace. Added by D-30. What would change it: a store that lets an unverified edge be followed by a verified one, as maps now can.
- A record that already has another edge: every derived edge goes to the store, an equal edge is passed over and a different one refuses the whole call. Other option: skip such records and count them; an older "recovered" edge from a shorter input list would then stay while the tool knows of two candidates. What would change it: a store that can supersede a recovered edge.
- A copy step with no normaliser available is refused with "unknown normaliser", nothing written (plan 3.3 gives the degraded form only for the deterministic rewrite). Other option: confirm on raw texts. What would change it: a ruling that a copy step may be confirmed on raw texts.
- The direct comparison reads the candidate texts themselves (one pass per file for its digest and line offsets, then a seek per candidate), and every text read is fingerprinted again against the stored fingerprint. Other option: compare digests of the normalised texts, which is the hash comparison the direct one exists to get behind. What would change it: a measured cost of random reads at corpus scale.

## D-30 The tool's packages joined: one store call per run, schema version 2 (plan 3.3, 3.4, F10, 2.2(d))
- The schema is at version 2, reached from version 1 by a migration step (plan 3.4: versioned and migrated stepwise): a harness log gains the column `harness`, empty for every earlier log, and the unique index over a shard's maps keeps only the verified ones (D-28 as amended). A new store runs every step, so it holds the same schema as a migrated one; a test migrates a version-1 store and compares the two. Other option: revise version 1 in place again and refuse an older file at open, as D-25 did once before any store left the test folders. What would change it: nothing found; every later change adds a step.
- A harness log says which harness wrote it ("in-process" or "confined"), and the store and every negative's scope show it. Other option: logs byte-identical across harnesses; a stored bound would then look the same whether the job could open other files or not. What would change it: nothing found. Which harness establishes a bound under 2.2(g) follows from Decision 13 (a bound is established only by a closed-input harness whose access log lists every record the job read): only a confined log does (D-27).
- The model a log names is the one the model client reports where it reports one (`harness.ModelReply`), else the one the job asked for, written before the call. Other option: only the asked identifier, a text of the job's choosing. What would change it: clients that cannot say which model answered.
- A skipped line is recorded once: a recovery that would record a sanitizer drop for a line that already has one under another output of the other kind is refused with "drop already recorded" (exit 4) before anything is written. Other option: refuse only the literal case of a log read twice; the double count of a line under the tokenizer input and again under the shard would then be written. What would change it: a pipeline that sanitizes one input twice with one output registered (a limit in docs/tokenmap.md).
- All maps of one derivation run and their drops are one store transaction (`Store.write_maps`); the tokenization package no longer checks beforehand what the store would refuse. Other option: one transaction per map. What would change it: nothing found.
- A store made by an earlier build is brought forward by `waterwhite migrate --store FILE`; every command that only reads a store opens it read-only, so it refuses a store that needs a step and names that command. Other option: a reading command that migrates on the way; it would then write to a store the person only asked to read. What would change it: nothing found.
- The `recover` command reaches a team's tokenizer by `--tokenizer-command` (an external process speaking a line protocol) or `--tokenizer-entry MODULE:FUNCTION`; the tool imports no tokenizer library, and a test walks every import of the package. A recovery that ran exits 0 whatever it found; `--append-eod` and `--eod-in-span` have no default. Other option: one flag that imports a named tokenizer library. What would change it: scripts that need a failed verification as an exit code.
- The words shared by the packages (sanitizer drop reasons, the step names "sanitize" and "tokenize", the harness and outcome words) are defined once in `waterwhite.vocabulary`, as are the line reader, the path rule (`Store.disk_path`) and the shard reader (`artifact_files`). Other option: each package keeps its own; two copies can drift. What would change it: nothing found.
- `show SHARD --map NAMESPACE/DERIVATION` prints the rows of one named map, so a recovery map beside a capture map can be read (plan Check 3 asks both). Where a namespace and derivation hold a map that did not verify beside a verified one, it prints the verified one, says so in the map's verification, and adds the key `other_maps` naming the others with the flags (`--verification`) that read each; the key is absent otherwise. Other option: no such key, and the offer only in the flag's help; the output would then never say that another map exists. What would change it: a rule that the output holds exactly the listed keys.
- A `--map` naming a map the shard does not hold, or an artifact that is no shard, is refused as "request refused" (exit 2) with what the shard holds in its context; a malformed `--map` value is a usage error (exit 1). Other option: a named error of its own; a caller can already tell the cases apart by the context. What would change it: a test or a team that must tell them apart by name.
- Every `show ARTIFACT` prints `drops` (empty when there are none), and every binding carries `append_eod` and `eod_in_span` (null for a binding that is no map). Other option: the keys only where there is something to print; a reader would then not know whether a drop is absent or not shown. What would change it: nothing found.
- AMENDED (exit code): a `recover` command that ran exits 0 whatever it found. A recovery that linked nothing, and a map whose verification failed or was not run, are results: `verified`, `outcome` and the counts say so in the document printed on standard output, and a map that did not verify is written to the store with its verification word (tests/tokenmap/test_cli_recover.py::test_recover_map_without_a_tokenizer_compares_nothing asserts exit 0, `verified` false and the stored map). A nonzero exit is kept for a command that was refused or faulted (docs/cli.md, exit codes of `recover`). Other option: a nonzero exit for a map that did not verify or a recovery that found no link. Result of the other option (traced for the path, inferred for the code): a nonzero exit leaves `main` only through the error path (the error object on standard error and an empty standard output), so the document that says what was found would not be printed, although the map had been written to the store; (inferred) if the code chosen were 4, "a write was refused" would no longer mean that nothing was written. What would change it: the clause already in D-30, scripts that need a failed verification as an exit code. [written at the M4 check]
- AMENDED (tokenizer): the tool imports no tokenizer library. A team's tokenizer reaches it by `--tokenizer-command` (an external process speaking the line protocol `waterwhite.tokenizer/1`) or by `--tokenizer-entry MODULE:FUNCTION` (the tool imports that module of the team and nothing else; a module or name that cannot be used is "tokenizer failed"). Test: tests/tokenmap/test_tokenmap_tool.py::test_the_tool_imports_no_tokenizer_library walks every import under the package for `tokenizers`, `transformers`, `sentencepiece`, `tiktoken`, `megatron` and `torch` and allows one exception, torch in `join/_shuffle.py`. Other option (already in D-30): one flag that imports a named tokenizer library. Result of the other option (inferred): a map verified by tokenizing again would be checked against the tokenizer the tool imports and not against the one the team ran, so a verified map could rest on a tokenizer that did not make the shard; (traced) the test above would fail for the new import, and plan 3.3 has the wrapper run the tokenizer of the team, as it is, in a process of its own. What would change it: a team whose tokenizer can be reached in neither of the two ways, or a ruling that the tool may import a named tokenizer library. [written at the M4 check]

## D-31 Check 6's import rows split between the answer and the rebuild report (plan 2.1, 3.2, 4.1, L1, L5)
A change of the hand-written table made after tool code existed, its values taken from the plan alone (W5): the rows `c06.import.parent-unresolved` and `c06.import.review-scope` of check06.toml, read 8 of fixture/table/README.md.
- `c06.import.parent-unresolved` is now a row of the answer: under "withdraw A" narrowed to the record X@A, import/T answers "may contain", the first failing link is condition (b) on its record T (a file-level link cannot decide a record-level request, plan 3.2), and the candidate source is A (plan 2.1: a "may contain" lists the candidate sources; the import's file family names A). The answer form has no field for a parent, so "none recorded" (L1; no parent by similarity, L5) is read from the failing link's record and the candidates. Before, the row expected the fields parent, action and missing link, which no answer carries. Other option: a parent field in the answer; the plan gives an answer no such field. What would change it: a plan change that gives the answer a parent field.
- `c06.import.review-scope` is now a row of the rebuild report: the record T gets the action "unresolved" with its missing link, and the scope that needs review is the whole file (plan 4.1, L1). Its test is skipped by name until the report exists (a later milestone). Other option: the answer carries the action and the scope; the plan puts both in the report. What would change it: nothing found.
- No row id changed, so the Check 10 group `c06.import.record-level` lists the same rows. Other option: new ids for the two rows; the Check 10 group and its manifest row would change with them. What would change it: a reader for whom the old ids now mislead.

## D-32 The replica's capture points for shards and mixtures, its run made without capture, and its table stores (plan 5.4, 3.3, Checks 3 and 6)
- In a generated set a generation job of many rows runs once per row and is named `<job>/<row number>` (the row's line in the job's output), never `<job>/<record id>`: plan Check 3 says the tool is not given the chunk-file ids, and a test reads the whole store file and finds no id of the set. Other option: the record id in the name; the ids would reach the store. What would change it: a caller that must tell two sets in one store apart by job name (a prefix).
- A release found afterwards (R1, R2 of a store made without capture) is registered with the role "release", so a listing covers it; other found files keep "intermediate" (D-29 as amended). Other option: every found file "intermediate"; a listing of that store would cover no release. What would change it: nothing found.
- `build_table_store` tokenizes only when asked (`tokenize=True`); a store that needs shards is asked for them. Other option: tokenizing by default; every caller of a store without shards would then need Megatron. What would change it: a ruling that the acceptance tests build shards by default.
- The confined harness is a build option (`generation_harness="confined"`), refused before anything is written where bwrap is missing, and gives the same store records as the in-process one. One sandbox starts per generated row: 90 for the 800-record set, about 11,100 for the 100k set, about 84 ms each on this laptop (one run). Which harness a build uses follows from Decision 13: a store of the table runs its jobs confined (`build_table_store`'s default), and a build may run them in process only where no graded row compares an answer on it (a generated set for Check 3, whose rows compare maps; the command line's default). Other option: one sandbox per job with all its rows handed at once; each row's bound would then be the whole job's hand-off, wider than the row's own inputs. What would change it: a measured cost that makes the per-row sandbox the bottleneck of a build.
- A run made without capture draws its shuffles from the operating system; a test that needs a planted pair in one shard draws again, up to 12 times (about 3 draws in 10 place none). Other option: skip when no pair lands in one shard; a skip is not a pass. What would change it: a seeded shuffle, which would no longer be the run made without capture.

## D-33 The exposure join and its registration (plan 3.5, Decision 5, ledger ids M2, M6, M7, M9, M12; the owner's ruling on the order of a cyclic run)
- The order of a cyclic run is computed by the join, from the run record's sizes, by calling torch as the sampler draws it: for each launch, epoch and rank, `rank * bucket` plus `torch.randperm(bucket)` from a generator seeded with the epoch, with the bucket the dataset length rounded down to whole micro batches over all ranks. It counts only when its `ids.order_digest` and its count equal the recorded shuffle digest; otherwise the segment has no trace and names "cyclic order digest mismatch". torch is imported in one function (`join/_shuffle.py`), and only once the record is known to hold every digest the launches need, so a run that records too little never loads it. Other option: a numpy restatement of torch's generator. Result: no dependency beyond numpy, a copy of another library's generator that is exact only while torch's algorithm stays; a probe (orders of 24, 40, 160 and 1,000 indices; seeds 0, 1 and 1234) found none of numpy's three permutations (`RandomState`, `default_rng`, `Generator(MT19937)`) equal to `torch.randperm`. What would change it: a test that shows a numpy route equal to `torch.randperm` on every size and seed a run can have.
- D-01 amended: numpy stays the one runtime dependency; torch is an optional dependency (`cyclic` in pyproject.toml), needed only to register a cyclic run that recorded its shuffle digests. Other option: torch required for every install. Result: every install carries torch though only cyclic runs call it. What would change it: a deployment that registers cyclic runs only.
- A cyclic run whose shuffle must be computed, on a machine where torch cannot be imported, is refused with "torch not available" (a refused write, exit 4) and nothing is written; a cyclic segment with no shuffle digest, or an epoch missing one, registers with "cyclic order not recorded" without torch (plan 3.5: that answer depends on the record, not the machine). Other option: refuse every cyclic segment without torch. Result: the cause stored for a segment that records too little would depend on the machine. What would change it: a ruling that every cyclic registration needs torch.
- The join's cache files count only against the cache digests the run record holds; with none recorded the segment has no route. This is stricter than the plan: see D-41, which records it as a decision of the project's owner with the plan's own option.
- The join does not read a kept file of a cyclic run's order: the owner's ruling names no such option. Other option: a file of each epoch's order kept beside the run record and checked against its digest. Result: exact answers for a run that kept its orders, but a training wrapper would have to write them. What would change it: a ruling that adds that option.
- "invalid record" exits with 4 wherever it is given, a run record or step record that is not one included: one name, one exit code (docs/interfaces.md section 2). Other option: exit 1 for a malformed file handed to `register-run`, as a command line the tool cannot read. Result: one error name with two exit codes. What would change it: a caller that must tell a malformed file from a store refusal by the exit code alone.
- `register-run` registers one segment in one store call: the segment, its steps, its traces and its checkpoints in one transaction (`register_run_segment(..., traces=, checkpoints=)`, both empty by default so earlier callers are unchanged). Other option: two calls. Result: a stop between them leaves a segment without traces or checkpoints until the join is run again. The run, segment and start kind come from the run record; the mixture and the parent checkpoint are given by name and must be registered; the position is the segment's own when registered, else the next free one of its run.
- The store refuses a route other than "none" for a cyclic segment with no shuffle digest, and coverage reads such a segment as "cyclic order not recorded" whatever route is stored. For other routes the store does not check that the run record holds the digests the route needs: through `register-run` the route can only come from the join. Other option: the store refuses any route its run record cannot support. Result: a hand-written store could not hold such a route, and every hand-built test store would need digests. What would change it: a caller other than the join that writes routes.
- D-25 amended: the causes a segment with no trace route may carry are five ("data-order trace missing", "data-order trace unverified for this version", "data-order trace unverified for this sampler type", "cyclic order not recorded", "cyclic order digest mismatch"), not three: plan 3.5 gives three and F6 two more. Not built yet: the store still accepts any named cause for a segment, not only these five, and D-25's rule that a segment whose run record holds no launch supports no "does not contain" (both to be built test first). No schema change was needed, so the schema stays at version 2.
- The other choices of the join are described in docs/join.md section 10: the order of the gates, cache files that do not match falling through to the replay, missing evidence as a cause and a malformed record as an error, a cyclic segment with one unusable order getting no trace, a step the map cannot place left untraced while the segment keeps its route, the extra token off giving no trace, and completed steps taken as the union of the launches and the step record.

## D-34 The replica's run wrapper (plan 3.3 "Training", 5.4, 3.5)
- The replica stands in for the training wrapper: `python -m replica build-runs` launches Megatron's own dataset builder over the fixture's shards for each segment of runs E, E-rr, D, Y and K, with a new empty cache folder, so the cache files are Megatron's; it then writes the run record (cache digests read back from the kept files), the step record and the cache folder. It imports nothing of the grading side. Other option: the grading side's record writer. Result: the join would read records written by the code that grades it. What would change it: a team's own training wrapper, which replaces this stand-in.
- K's shuffle digests are computed in the run's own environment with the replica's own torch call, never through the join's function. Other option: one shared function. Result: the row that compares the join's order with the recorded digests could not fail on the fixture. What would change it: nothing in the first build; the stand-in and the join must stay apart.
- The release tag is read from the Megatron checkout and held against the installed package's version, not copied from the spec. Other option: copy it from the spec. Result: a claim, not a reading, and the verified-join list is keyed on it. What would change it: a wrapper with no checkout, which would read the tag from its installed package.
- Data sharding where the spec states none is read from Megatron's own argument default. The step record lists every completed step with its batch size, the lowered step with `last_sample_tokens_read`. One worker process makes all segments (one import of Megatron). Shards are copied into the run's folder before Megatron is handed them, so the cache descriptions name the paths the record names. Other options, in turn: a hard-coded default (a copy, not an observation); only the lowered steps (the store could not take completed steps from the record); one process per segment (about seven times the import cost); building from the fixture build's paths (descriptions that name a folder the record does not). What would change any of them: a run whose launch differs from the replica's, such as one with data sharding off, which is written but not run.

## D-35 Acceptance of the run rows (plan 5.5 Checks 1, 2, 4, 5, 8)
- A row that needs a registered run waits for one need, "run capture", answered by whether `python -m replica build-runs` and `waterwhite register-run` exist; a command counts as absent only when its parser rejects the word, so an import error in a landed command is a failure, not a skip. Other option: any failure of `--help` counts as absent. Result: an import error would skip about 150 rows silently. What would change it: commands that report their absence another way.
- The map need is answered like the shard need: a tokenized store that registered a shard. Other option: keep the map need listed as not built. Result: the 74 map rows of Check 5 stay skipped. What would change it: a store that registers shards without the Megatron environment.
- The rows of Checks 1 and 8 that compare the join's rows with the grading side's log wait for a command that prints a segment's rows; they are not read from the store's tables. Other option: read the tables directly. Result: the tool graded by its own table layout, and a change of the schema breaks the acceptance tests. What would change it: nothing; the rows run once such a command exists.
- Check 2 (iii), "the settings withheld", is read as the seed, split and iteration counts and the cache digests removed, batch sizes and sampler kept. Other option: the whole settings object removed. Result: the record is refused as invalid and the row errors instead of answering. What would change it: the table naming the settings it withholds.

## D-36 A route follows from the run record, whoever calls the store (plan 3.5, 2.4, 2.2(e), 3.1, Check 2; D-25, D-33 amended)
- A segment with no trace route carries one of the five trace causes and nothing else; the store refuses any other named cause with "invalid record". This builds D-25's last entry as D-33 amended it.
- "cache files" and "replay" each need a run record that holds a digest of every index array the segment has: three per shard and, from two shards on, two for the blend (a replay counts only against digests recorded at training time, plan 3.5; that the cache files count only against the same digests is a decision of the project's owner, stricter than plan 3.5 and 4.6, recorded in D-41, where this entry's first citation of the plan for it is corrected); a cyclic run also needs its shuffle digest (plan 3.5). A call with no run record supports neither route. The store refuses a route that fails this with "invalid record" and writes nothing, whoever calls it; the join and the store share one rule for the arrays a segment has. Other option: leave the check to the join, as D-33 had it. Result: a direct store call could give a segment a route no recorded digest supports, and its checkpoints could answer "does not contain" on a data order nobody held against a digest. What would change it: a caller that must record a route from evidence a run record cannot hold; that would need a field of its own, not a weaker check.
- Amended by D-41, which removes the override this entry describes and closes the case at the write. As first built: a segment whose run record holds no launch supports no "does not contain": every step of it is untraced and its checkpoints answer "may contain" naming "step untraced", whatever route or cause is stored. The rule outranks every stored cause, "cyclic order not recorded" and the two causes of an unverified version or sampler included, and it also turns a "contains" that rests on a stored trace of such a segment into "may contain" (no step of it is known to be whole). A segment registered with no run record at all keeps its stored cause ("data-order trace missing", "no recorded digest"). Through `register-run` such a record is stored as route "none" with "data-order trace missing" and no reason (the join cannot place steps without a launch), and the answer names "step untraced". Other option: apply the rule to segments with a stored trace route only, so that a route "none" keeps its stored cause. Result: plan 3.5's own cause for those records, and one record answering under two names by the route its caller gave it. What would change it: a plan line that fixes the cause of a record with no launch, or a ruling that a stored trace of such a segment still supports "contains".
- `register-run` prints what became of the replay route: `"replay": {"attempted", "accepted", "reason"}`, with the reason set where the replay is decided ("no recorded digest" when none was tried for want of a digest, "digest mismatch" when one was refused), and the default (not attempted, null, null) for a segment that never reached the question of the arrays. `waterwhite replay --run-record PATH --out DIR` writes the arrays the replay derives (one file per kind, `<kind>-<shard position>.npy` for an array of a shard, `<kind>.npy` for the blend), prints each with its digest and the recorded one, writes no store, and refuses a file that already exists; whether the arrays equal the kept cache files is the acceptance test's comparison. `show SEGMENT --trace [--step N]` prints the rows the join wrote, with each step's `consumed_samples` as the store holds it (the step's batch size).
- The in-process harness's public entry point, `harness.run_job`, takes no harness word and always writes "in-process"; only `waterwhite.confine` writes "confined", through a function that is not public (Decision 13; a log of the in-process harness never establishes a bound). Other option: a guarded argument. Result: a public argument that can change the word. What would change it: nothing in the first build. A log file written by hand outside the tool is outside what the store can check (docs/confine.md).

## D-37 The replica's stores that hold runs list the joins of those runs (plan 3.5, F6, Check 1; D-26)
- The replica creates `main` with the fixture release's joins for the two sampler types of its runs and `rr` with the one of its run; the other stores list none. This is the test use of `init --verified-join` that D-26 keeps until Check 1 has passed for a release. Other option: stores with no list. Result: every segment registers as "data-order trace unverified for this version" and no row on a route, a replay or a trace can run, Check 1 included. What would change it: the tool shipping its own list once Check 1 has passed for the release (D-26). Amended by D-51: the tool ships its list and a store made by `init` takes it; the replica's stores are made by the library, which writes only the joins it is given, so they list what this entry says.
- The runs each store holds are named in the replica (`StoreVariant.runs`), not read from the acceptance folder. Other option: read the acceptance folder's list. Result: the replica would read the grading side. What would change it: the spec naming the stores of its runs.

## D-38 Acceptance of the trace, replay and registration rows (plan 5.5 Checks 1, 2, 4, 8; D-35)
- The acceptance folder reads a store's list of verified joins and never writes it: a store that is to hold runs and lists none is an error of the run. Other option: write the list before registering. Result: every pass of a checkpoint row would rest on a list the grading side declared. What would change it: nothing; the build that makes a store owns its list.
- Check 2 (i) compares each replayed array with the kept cache file of the same kind and shard as an array (dtype, shape and data), not as file bytes: the recorded digests are taken over the array (docs/interfaces.md 8.1). Other option: file bytes. Result: a failure on a header difference no plan line judges.
- The steps a trace must hold (fully traced steps, ranks, the lowered sample) come from the grading side's log, not from what the trace prints. Other option: what the trace prints. Result: an empty trace would pass three rows.
- Check 4's scratch-store rows are set up in the acceptance folder: a map writer killed after 100 rows, the same write retried, a map declared with 499 rows, and a stored count edited after a complete map. The map they write has no tokenizer behind it: the rows ask what the store holds, not what the map says.

## D-39 What show --trace and register-run print of a run's launches, epochs and computed shuffle (plan 3.5, Checks 1 and 8)
- `show SEGMENT --trace` prints the run record's launches as registered, in position order, whatever the route and whatever `--step` keeps. For a segment read with the cyclic sampler, each step also gets `epochs`: the epochs its samples are drawn from. The dataset length is stored nowhere, so the sizes of a launch's cyclic map are taken from the run record's shuffle-digest count: the bucket is the count, and an epoch is the count times the data-parallel size. The steps are walked from the launch's consumed-sample counter with the batch sizes of the registered steps. For a traced cyclic segment this count is the one the join held equal to the order it drew. A step whose launch gives no single usable count prints `epochs` null; that can happen on a traced segment, since the join accepts a recorded shuffle digest with no count. Other option: store each step's epochs at registration. Result: a schema change (version 3 with a migration step) to keep a value the run record already fixes. What would change it: a run whose shuffle digests carry no count.
- `register-run` prints `shuffle`, the digest and count of every order the join computed for a cyclic segment, beside the recorded ones, with `equal` for each entry. All the orders a launch needs are drawn before a mismatch is returned, so every differing entry is printed; the cause names the first. The printed values are the join's own, not a second computation in the command. A sequential segment, a cyclic record with no shuffle digest, and one that lacks any digest a launch needs, print `[]` (the join asks for every digest before it draws). Other option: print only the cause. Result: the row that holds the join's order against the recorded digests could not read which entry differs.
- The acceptance row of Check 1 on the skipped line reads the drop the store holds under the shard's registered input, the sanitizer's output file. The tool lists a drop under the output of the step that dropped the line: the sanitizer's drops under the JSONL file it wrote, the tokenizer's under the shard (docs/cli.md, show). Other option: show of a shard also lists its inputs' drops. Result: one more list on every shard, for a row that can read the input. What would change it: a row that asks a shard alone for its input's drops.
- The acceptance row c08.K.digests-match holds the tool's printed digests and counts against run K's run record file, which the replica wrote, and not against the tool's own `equal` and `recorded_digest`. Other option: read `equal`. Result: the tool would grade itself. What grades the order itself is Check 1's rows on run K, against the grading side's log.
- A form the tool prints (show --trace's launches and epochs, register-run's shuffle and replay object) that is absent from its output fails the acceptance row that reads it; it is not a skip. Other option: skip such a row by name, as while the forms were not built. Result: a row could come back skipped with nothing red to fix.

## D-40 A run over a generated set, for the measurements of plan 5.6 (plan 5.6, 5.4, 3.3 "Training"; D-34, D-37)
- `python -m replica probe-run` makes one run read with the sequential sampler over the shards a generated-set build wrote, with run E's settings unless flags say otherwise: it registers the run's mixture (a snapshot of those shards in the run's order) through the tool's own call, launches Megatron's dataset code into a new empty cache folder through the same path as `build-runs`, writes the run record and the step record, and registers the segment with `waterwhite register-run`. It prints the time of each of the three parts. Other option: a second launch path of its own. Result: the measured run would not be the run the fixture's records come from. What would change it: a measurement that needs the cyclic sampler.
- It refuses a store that does not list the sequential join of the run's release, before it writes anything, and never writes the list itself (D-37, D-38). To make such a store from the command line, `build-fixture` and `build-set` take `--list-join single|cyclic`: the test use of `init --verified-join` that D-26 keeps until the list ships (D-51: it now ships; the flag narrows the shipped list and accepts no other join). Other option: write the list inside probe-run. Result: a measurement whose store's list the measuring code declared.
- Its steps, when not given, are the whole steps of one pass over the shards, and the run is refused when Megatron's dataset holds fewer samples than the steps read. Its names are run `probe`, segment `probe/seg-1`, mixture `probe/<layout>` and one checkpoint at the last step, so that a query has a checkpoint to ask. Shards are taken in the order of their names, with digit runs read as numbers. It measures the run's parts only: query time, the coverage-proof walk, peak memory and bytes per stored trace are measured beside it, by their own commands.

## D-41 Kept cache files and recorded digests; the reason a record lacks; no trace without a launch (plan 3.5, 4.6, 2.2(e), 3.1, 2.4; D-25, D-33 and D-36 amended)
- Kept cache files count only against the digests recorded at training time. A segment whose run record holds no digest of an index array has no trace route even when its cache folder was kept and given, and its checkpoints answer "may contain" naming "data-order trace missing" with the reason "no recorded digest". Decided by the project's owner. This is stricter than plan 3.5 and 4.6: the plan asks a recorded digest of a replay only, and in 4.6 a checkpoint's answers rest either on kept caches or on a replay checked against the recorded digests. D-33 built it into the join and D-36 into the store; D-36 first cited the plan for it, which was wrong, and is corrected. Other option: the plan's text, under which kept cache files count without a recorded digest, tied to their shards by the order of the blend's description file or the run's saved configuration, and stale caches found by the mixture digest. Result: a run that kept its cache folder but recorded no digest gets the route "cache files", and its checkpoints can answer "contains" and "does not contain" on files that nothing recorded at training time vouches for, so a cache file rebuilt or replaced after training from the same paths would be read as the order the run used. What would change it: the owner's word, or a training wrapper that records something of each file the store can hold the file against in place of a digest. The replay rule stays as signed (plan 3.5; the owner's build-start decision (4), plan Section 7, not the plan's Decision 4 on tokenization). Its check: tests/coverage/test_kept_caches_without_digest.py.
- The reason a run record lacks is a stored word. Beside "data-order trace missing" the plan gives two reasons, "no recorded digest" and "digest mismatch", and both stay with the replay (`TRACE_MISSING_REASONS`).
