Four states of the join fit neither, and each now stores a word of its own (`TRACE_MISSING_RECORD_REASONS`), so that an answer never hides a reason the tool knows: "run record incomplete" (a setting the walk needs is not recorded: the shard list, a shard's prefix, the micro or global batch size, the sequence length, the extra-token flag, or a launch), "shard index unreadable" (a shard's index file is gone or cannot be read at registration), "extra token off" (the run was read with the extra token off) and "index array malformed" (below). The join stores the word as the segment's cause detail at the gate that decided it (docs/join.md section 3); `register-run` prints it as `cause_detail` and an answer carries it as `names_detail`. The store accepts beside "data-order trace missing" only these six words or none (a direct caller that does not know the reason stores none), and no reason beside any other cause. No schema change: the column was already text. Other option: derive the reason when an answer is made. Result: a shard file moved or changed after registration would change the reason an answer gives, and an answer would need the shard files at read time. What would change it: a ruling that an answer must state what the files are now, not what they were at registration.
- "index array malformed" (added after the three above). A kept cache file is tied to a recorded key by the kind its name gives and then by digest, so digests filed under the wrong kind find no file and end at the replay with a digest reason. What reaches this state is a cache file named for its kind whose own digest the run record holds, and whose array has a shape no index array of that kind has (the replay builds each array in its kind's shape, so only the cache-files route reaches it). The record and the files agree; the files are not what their kind must be. That is differing evidence, read only from the files, and so a cause (docs/join.md section 10, item 4), stored with this word: the segment is registered with route "none" and its checkpoints answer "may contain" naming "data-order trace missing" with "index array malformed". Other option: treat it as a malformed record, an error ("invalid record", exit 4). Result: nothing of the segment is registered, its checkpoints are not in the store, so a query on them answers "not covered", and the reason exists only in the refusal's text. Other option: store no reason, as first built. Result: the answer names the cause and hides a reason the tool knows. What would change it: a run record format that states the shape of each array, which would make the mismatch visible in the record alone and so an error. Its check: tests/coverage/test_array_shape_reason.py, written before the code.
- No trace without a launch, closed at the write (D-36 amended). The store refuses a trace route other than "none" for a run record that holds no launch (empty, null or absent), and refuses any trace for a segment whose route is "none" (`write_step_traces` and `register_run_segment(traces=)`); both are "invalid record" and write nothing, whoever calls the store. Every write of a step trace goes through one method, so no other path writes one. A segment with route "none" answers with its stored cause and reason, with or without a launch: through `register-run` the cause is the join's, in the join's own order of questions, so a cyclic run with no shuffle digest names "cyclic order not recorded", an unverified release or sampler names its F6 name, and a plain record with no launch names "data-order trace missing" with "run record incomplete". Only a store edited by hand can hold a stored route on a record with no launch; it answers "may contain" naming "data-order trace missing" with "run record incomplete", as the join would have stored it, after the cyclic case (the join asks about the cyclic order first). Other option: D-36 as first built, an override at read time that names "step untraced" whatever cause or route is stored. Result: one record answering under two names by the route its caller gave it, and a stored cause, reason and answer that disagree. What would change it: a plan line that fixes the cause of a record with no launch as "step untraced". Limit: a store written before this rule, or edited by hand, can hold a stored route on a record with no launch and be given traces later, since the trace write checks the stored route and not the launches; coverage reads such a segment as above.

## D-42 A proof is for one artifact under its own names (plan 2.2, W6, Decision 1)
- A proof keeps the names its artifact had when the walk was made, and an answer built from it shows either one of those names or the artifact's instance id. An answer that shows any other name is refused when it is built and when it is written (`ProofRequired`), whatever the request. Other option: check the instance id and the request only, as before, leaving the name an answer shows free. Result: a proof made for one artifact could be shown under the name of another, and the printed "does not contain" would name an artifact the walk never covered. What would change it: answers that carry no name, only the instance id. Its check: tests/coverage/test_proof_only_construction.py.

## D-43 The kind that decides the rule is the kind the store holds (plan 2.2, 2.2(a) to (g))
- `prove` and `Session.outcome` look an `Artifact` up again by its instance id and use what the store holds: its kind and its names. An instance id the store has no row for is answered "not covered" by `prove` and refused with `ValueError` by `Session.outcome`. Other option: use the object handed in. Result: an `Artifact` built around the store (a shard given as a JSONL file, other names) would choose which rule walks it, and a negative could come from the rules of the wrong kind. What would change it: an artifact type that only the store can make, so that no object handed in can differ from the store's row. Its check: tests/coverage/test_proof_only_construction.py.

## D-44 A mark of the store's state (plan 2.2, 3.4)
- `Store.state_mark()` returns the tuple (SQLite's `PRAGMA data_version`, the connection's `total_changes`). It is the same on two calls with no write between them and differs after any write, made through this connection (which the second part counts) or committed by another connection to the file (which the first part counts). A closed store has no mark and the call is refused with `WriteRefused`. Reads change neither part. A change to an artifact's file on disk is not a write to the store and does not move it (docs/coverage.md, docs/store.md). Other option: a write counter kept in a table of the store. Result: a schema change (version 3 with a migration step) to keep a value SQLite already reports. What would change it: a store engine whose data version does not move for another connection's commit. Its check: tests/coverage/test_proof_only_construction.py.

## D-45 A session is valid for one state of the store (plan 2.2, 2.2(e), 3.4)
- A `Session` takes the store's mark when it begins. `Session.outcome` and `prove` refuse with `ValueError` ("the store was written after this session began") once the mark differs, and `prove` checks it before the integrity checks and again after the walk, so a write that falls before the checks, during the walk or after it is found. A new session reads the store as it is. Other option: let the session go on, or read the store again without telling the caller. Result: going on serves the verdicts a walk kept of a store that no longer exists; reading again mixes two states of the store in one listing. What would change it: a caller that must write while it walks, which would ask for an explicit refresh of the session. Limit: a commit by another process during a query raises this `ValueError`, which `cli.main` does not catch (it catches `WaterwhiteError`), so the command ends with a traceback and not the JSON error; a named error with an exit code and a line in docs/interfaces.md section 2 would close it and is not built. Its check: tests/coverage/test_proof_only_construction.py.
- AMENDED by D-56, D-75 and D-76 (the limit above is closed): a commit by another process during a query raises `StoreWrittenDuringQuery` (`errors.py`, name "store written during the query", exit code 2), a `Refused` that `cli.main` catches (it is a `WaterwhiteError`), so the command ends with the JSON error on standard error and nothing on standard output, and not with a traceback. The `ValueError` this entry names is no longer raised by the session or by `prove`; docs/interfaces.md section 2 holds the named error. Its check: tests/rebuild/test_query_store_written.py.

## D-46 A pass of the loud checks covers nothing once the store is written (plan 3.4, 2.2(c), (d))
- `coverage.check_store` records the store's mark from before its checks run, and the pass it returns covers an artifact only while the mark is the same. After any write, by this connection or another, the pass covers nothing and `prove` runs the loud checks itself. Other option: a pass valid for as long as it exists, as before. Result: a fault written after the pass (a map digest, an edge parent) would be skipped by a `prove` that trusts the pass, and the artifact could answer "does not contain" on a store never checked in that state. What would change it: nothing found. Its check: tests/coverage/test_proof_only_construction.py.

## D-47 A pass made by hand covers nothing (plan 3.4, 2.2)
- `IntegrityPass` stays a class a caller can construct. Only `check_store` registers a pass, and the register is closed over, as the proof mint is; a pass made by calling the class is not in it, covers no artifact, and `prove` runs its own checks for it. Other option: close the constructor, so that making a pass by hand raises at once. Result: the refusal moves from "this pass spares no check" to an error at construction, and the test that hands `prove` a pass made by hand would end in an error at that call and not in the refusal it asks about. What would change it: a ruling that making a pass by hand is itself an error. Its check: tests/coverage/test_proof_only_construction.py.

## D-48 A proof made before a write builds no answer after it (plan 2.2, W6, Decision 1)
- The mint remembers each proof with a function that asks whether the store it read is still as the walk read it (the session's mark, the store held weakly). When an answer is built, `Answer` asks it, and a proof made before a write, or whose store is closed, builds no answer ("the store was written after this proof was made, or is closed; prove again"). An answer already built is not asked again when it is written as JSON, so it can be written after its store is closed. Other option: no such check, a proof speaking for the store forever; or the same check again at the write. Result: with no check, an answer built after a write states a negative about a store that has changed; with a check at the write, an answer built in time could not be written once the store was closed. What would change it: a ruling against it, which removes the check in `answers._check` and the proof's function (the mint's `current` argument). Its check: tests/coverage/test_negative_open_rulings.py.
- AMENDED by D-75: the refusal message quoted above ("the store was written after this proof was made, or is closed; prove again") is no longer a message of the tool. Built (as D-75 corrects it): a proof the mint made whose store was written since the walk ends in `StoreWrittenDuringQuery`; a proof whose store is closed or gone ends in `ProofRequired` with the sentence that the store the proof was made from is closed or gone. Other option: keep the one message for both cases. Result of the other option: a write between prove's last check and the answer would end in a refusal with no name or exit code. What would change it: a ruling that a closed store is also a write.

## D-49 A generation edge that names no input gives no recorded origin (plan 2.3, 2.2(a))
- A record whose generation edge names no parent and was handed no input fails condition (a) like an edge of any other kind: "may contain" naming "missing origin", for every source, and never "does not contain" (docs/coverage.md, parent modes). This reads plan 2.3 literally: an occurrence whose chain does not end at an ingested revision fails 2.2(a). Other option: let a generation edge with no input pass condition (a), as before (the walk's test for "names no parent" excluded generation edges). Result: a record made by a job handed nothing could answer "does not contain" for a source although no recorded chain connects it to an ingested revision. What would change it: a ruling that a job handed nothing is a legitimate root of a chain; the exclusion is then restored in `Session._edge` and the test, which sits in a file of its own, is dropped. Its check: tests/coverage/test_negative_open_rulings.py.

## D-50 A coverage mark above the stored rows makes the step untraced (plan 2.2(e), 3.5, 2.4)
- A step is untraced when a sample's coverage mark claims more tokens than the sample's stored rows add up to: the rows that show what the step read are gone, or the mark was written beyond them, and the mark is no evidence without them. `trace_states` counts such samples (`unbacked_samples`, the sum taken in the query, no row loaded), `check_integrity` does not treat it as a fault, and the checkpoints that hold the step answer "may contain" naming "step untraced". This follows plan 3.5's own definition of the mark, a count of the sample's tokens its rows cover: a mark above the rows contradicts that definition, so the step is untraced, as Check 5's table treats a deleted or half-covered trace, and not a stop; it is a reading of the plan, not a rule stricter than it. Other option: the mark alone decides, whatever rows remain. Result: rows removed from the store with the mark left whole leave a step "traced" that nothing can show, and a checkpoint could answer "does not contain" about data whose rows the store no longer holds. What would change it: a ruling that the mark alone decides; the rule is then reverted and its test goes red. Its check: tests/coverage/test_negative_open_rulings.py.

## D-51 The shipped list of verified joins (plan 3.5, F6, Check 1; D-26, D-37, D-38)
- The joins plan Check 1 verified ship with the tool as two literals of `waterwhite.shipped_joins`: release `core_v0.20.0` with the sequential sampler type and `MegatronPretrainingSampler`, and with the cyclic sampler type and `MegatronPretrainingRandomSampler`. `waterwhite init` writes them when no `--verified-join` is given; given, the flag's joins are the store's whole list, and the flag cannot add to the shipped list or leave it empty. `Store.create` writes only the joins it is given, so the replica's stores (D-37) and every store made by the library keep their lists, and the acceptance folder still never writes a list (D-38). The list is a literal of the tool, not read from a fixture or any file beside the store. Other option: read the list from the fixture's spec at run time. Result: a store would list what the files beside it say, not what the tool was verified for. Other option: `Store.create` writes the shipped list when none is given, or always. Result: every library store that is given no list, the replica's stores that list none included, would list the shipped joins, and a test could no longer make a store that lists none; with "always", a test could not name a list of its own either. What would change it: a release or a sampler class that Check 1 passes for later (a row is added), or a command that runs Check 1 and records its result (D-26). The placement of the default (`init` only) is a decision not covered by a test. Its check: tests/coverage/test_shipped_verified_list.py.
- Amended after the first build of this entry (on D-26 and plan F6): no store made through the tool's own command line lists a join the tool does not ship. `init --verified-join TAG,TYPE,CLASS` refuses, as a usage error (exit 1, nothing written), any triple that is not as a whole one of the shipped joins, and one such join refuses the whole command; a subset of the shipped list is accepted as the store's whole list. A store made through the library (`Store.create` with a list of the caller's own) lists what its caller gives and is outside what the tool verified; the docs say so. Other option: remove the flag. Result: a command-line store always lists the whole shipped list, no narrowing is possible from the command line, and an existing caller of the flag fails as unknown usage. Before this amendment the flag's joins replaced the shipped list whatever they were, so a "does not contain" for a checkpoint could rest on a join nobody verified. What would change it: a command that runs Check 1 and records its result (D-26), or a ruling that a store may list a join the tool did not verify. Its check: tests/coverage/test_shipped_verified_list.py, tests/coverage/test_coverage_listing_cli.py.

## D-52 Acceptance of Check 5's core rows (plan 5.5 Check 5; D-35, D-38)
- Every core row of Check 5 runs against the tool through the fault set-ups the acceptance folder already holds: each fault is made on a copy of the store, and the rows keep the table's expected values. The extension row (the generating-model rule on) is skipped by name while the extension is off. No helper code was added. Other option: add helper code or new fault set-ups. Result: unreviewed code and no new coverage, since each clause of plan 5.5 Check 5 has a row. What would change it: a failed row, a skip that says "not run" or "not built yet", or a fault the plan names that has no row. With D-42 to D-51 built, the 220 core rows pass unchanged and the extension row is skipped by name. A Check 5 run is valid for the tree it ran on: the proof hardening (D-42 to D-50) and the shipped list (D-51) change what answers, and a row that then differs is a conflict between the code and the table, not a reason to change the row.

## D-53 How the tests of the proof's construction are written (plan 2.2, W6, Decision 1)
- A test that tries a manipulation of the proof (an artifact object built around the store, a session or a pass kept across a write or made by hand, a proof shown under another artifact's name) accepts any refusal as the safe outcome: a named error of the tool, `ValueError`, `TypeError` or `RuntimeError`. It fails only when the manipulation gives a proof, or a "does not contain", that a fresh walk of the store does not give. Other option: name the error each door gives. Result: the tests would fix the way the tool refuses, and closing a door by a different refusal would turn them red with no negative built. What would change it: a refusal the interfaces document as part of a door's contract, which a test would then name.
- The scan that no module outside the coverage package, the answer module and the vocabulary (which holds the words) builds the verdict CLEAR or a "does not contain" answer reads the source of each module as a tree: the word itself, the names that only the makers use, the answer, proof and finding constructors, the mint and private modules of the coverage package. It is green by design at the first build, since no such module exists; what can fail is the test that plants a line for each way and requires the scan to report it. Other option: a scan with no planted lines. Result: a scan that finds nothing could be a scan that cannot find. What would change it: a new way to build the negative, which gets a planted line of its own. Its check: tests/coverage/test_proof_only_construction.py.

Entries D-54 to D-72 settle the choices of the rebuild report, the verification and the replica's repair (docs/interfaces.md 7.1 to 7.4 and 9.5). Where the text of an entry had no clause "What would change it", the clause is written at the merge and marked "[added at the merge]".

## D-54 The error name of a store written during a query is a vocabulary constant (docs/interfaces.md 2; plan 2.2, 3.4)
- Built (superseded by the AMENDED line below: the name is now a literal in `errors.py`): `vocabulary.py` holds `ERROR_STORE_WRITTEN_DURING_QUERY = "store written during the query"` (words only), and docs/interfaces.md section 2 names it; the error class takes its name from the constant (`errors.StoreWrittenDuringQuery`). Other option: leave the name only in `errors.py`, as every other error name is. Result of the other option: the mechanical check "every new printed word is a vocabulary constant" fails on this one error name. Result of the built option: this one error name is a constant while all others stay literals in `errors.py`; harmless, since the import runs from `errors.py` to the vocabulary and no cycle arises. What would change it: a ruling that error names are outside "words", or that all error names move into the vocabulary (a wider change in `errors.py`).
- AMENDED: the import is dropped. `errors.py` states the name of `StoreWrittenDuringQuery` as a literal, as every other error name in it, and imports nothing of the vocabulary; `vocabulary.py` keeps `ERROR_STORE_WRITTEN_DURING_QUERY`, and tests/rebuild/test_query_store_written.py pins the two equal. Result of keeping the import: the replica with capture off loads `waterwhite.vocabulary`, because it loads `errors` through `normalise`, against the wall of docs/interfaces.md section 1 (tests/replica/test_replica_build_fixture.py fails). The constant still exists, so the mechanical check "every printed word is a vocabulary constant" is met, and the literal is pinned equal to it by the test. What would change it: a ruling that the replica may load the vocabulary with capture off.

## D-55 JSON keys and the detail sentence of an error are not words (docs/interfaces.md 2, 7.1, 7.3)
- Built: keys such as `not_covered` and the free-text detail after an error name are literals in `cli.py`, `listing.py` and `errors.py`, not vocabulary constants; docs/interfaces.md section 2 says the name is fixed and the detail is free text. Other option: a constant for every new key and detail. Result of the other option: dozens of constants for keys nothing compares against, out of step with the keys that already exist. What would change it: a ruling that the "words" of the vocabulary include keys; constants for `first_exposure`, `from_scratch`, `holds_unresolved`, `bytes_unknown`, `needs_successor` and the others are then added as words only.

## D-56 Where the negative is read and the store-written refusal is raised (docs/interfaces.md 2, 6, 7.4; plan 2.2, W6; D-45, D-53)
- Built: `Answer.is_negative` (a read-only property of `answers.py`), `coverage.is_clear` (in the coverage package's public list), the one raise of `StoreWrittenDuringQuery` in `Session.ensure_current` (`coverage/_walk.py`) and the class itself (`errors.py`). The rebuild package reads answers and verdicts through these and builds none. Other option: leave these four files unchanged. Result of the other option: `coverage.prove` keeps raising `ValueError` and the class does not exist, so the tests of the named error go red; the restart points of the report have no way to ask whether an answer is negative; and the rebuild package would have to name the verdict CLEAR or the answer "does not contain", which the scan of D-53 forbids. What would change it: a ruling that these four files belong to another part of the tool; the predicates and the raise then move with the same names.
- AMENDED by D-75 and D-76: `StoreWrittenDuringQuery` is no longer raised in one place. It is raised in `Session.ensure_current` (`coverage/_walk.py`), in the answer type when a proof the mint made is built into an answer after the store was written (`answers._check`), and by `cli._unchanged_since` after the last answer of `query`, `report` and `verify`. The three raises say the same thing (the store changed after the query began) and share the one default detail of the class. The rebuild package still raises none of its own. Other option: one raise only, in the session. Result of the other option: a write in the window between prove's last check and the answer, and in the window after the last answer, stays outside it.

## D-57 The usage strings of `main` after `report` and `verify` (docs/cli.md "report", "verify")
- Built: the import line of `cli.py` gains `rebuild`; the usage string for no command names `report` and `verify` ("name a command: init, query, report, show, check, register-run, replay, verify or recover"); the usage string for a command with no request names the command ("name a request: <command> withdraw <source>"). The word `verify` in the first string is [added at the merge]: `verify` is a command that needs no request and the list otherwise omitted it. Other option: restore the old strings. Result of the other option: `report` with no request would print a message that names `query`, and [added at the merge] the list of commands would leave out `verify`; no test or document pins either string (a search of the tests and docs found none). What would change it: [added at the merge] a test or document that pins the text of either string.

## D-58 How the replica reads the report (docs/interfaces.md 7.1, 9.5)
- Built: a JSON file read with the json module; the replica reads `form`, `artifacts` and `records`, and the build is driven only by `records[].action`. Other option: also use `removed`, the counts or `rows_in_successor`. Result of the other option: a report edited by hand would still drop the record from `removed`, so a wrong report would not make the sides differ. What would change it: the form making `removed` the only list of a rewritten shard's records.

## D-59 Re-source (`--swap OLD=NEW`) (docs/interfaces.md 9.5)
- Built: `--swap OLD=NEW` as the form says, and `select()` in `replica/build.py` changed so that where NEW is already selected in the same list with the same normalised text, OLD just leaves and NEW stays at its own place. A swap onto a record with another text is still refused. Other option: leave OLD out at the driver level and read the dedup log. Result of the other option: it contradicts the form, `swapped` would be empty where a re-source was asked, and the driver would depend on the dedup log. Before the change, `--swap A-001=B-001` ended with "release R1: record 'B-001' is selected twice"; after it, `swapped` is `{"A-001": "B-001"}`. What would change it: someone needing the old refusal for equal-text copies. The new branch of `select()` has no unit test of its own; [added at the merge] a test of an equal-text swap and of the refusal for a different-text pair belongs in tests/replica/test_replica_build_fixture.py.

## D-60 Retire and keep of one id in two entries of a report (docs/interfaces.md 7.1, 9.5)
- Built: the replica refuses, naming both entries ("the report contradicts itself: record P is retired at R1:L2 and kept at R1:L2"). Unresolved next to retire is not a contradiction, and the id is left out of the whole build, text-only import included. Other option: keep wins, or retire wins. Result of the other option: keep wins hides a wrongly retired record and retire wins hides a wrongly kept one, so neither detects both wrong-report edits. What would change it: the report builder never listing a record twice.

## D-61 Locators in a report as the replica reads them (docs/interfaces.md 7.1, 9.5)
- Built: `L<n>` (a line, from 1) through the manifest `origin`, or a generation row's own `id`; `D<n>` (a document, from 0) through the tokenizer-input manifest and the shard's index; anything else is refused. Other option: refuse every `D<n>`. Result of the other option: every real report with a rewritten shard's documents would be refused, if the form lists them under `records`. What would change it: the form stating that a shard's documents stay out of `records`.

## D-62 Defaults of a repaired build (docs/interfaces.md 9.5)
- Built: layouts, tokenization, releases, imports, whether generation is captured, and the harness are read from the historical folder; an option overrides each. The seeds are not read: the caller passes `--unseeded` or `--shuffle-seed` as for the historical build. Other option: fixed defaults (both layouts, tokenize, all releases, in-process). Result of the other option: a repair of a historical build with no layout or no tokenization would start Megatron and register releases or imports it never had, and a confined build would be refused by the store. What would change it: [added at the merge] a historical folder that does not record these settings.
- AMENDED (the options named above): of the defaults of a repaired build, three have an option and three have none. Built: `--layout`, tokenization (`--no-tokenize`) and the harness (`--generation-harness`) can be overridden; the releases, the imports and whether the generation step is captured have no override and come from the historical folder only (`Historical.releases`, `has_imports`, `generation_captured`). The seeds are read from the options, as before. Other option: an option for each of the three. Result of the other option: a caller could ask for releases or imports the historical folder never held, or a captured generation step with no harness log behind it, and the store would register files its history does not have or refuse a job name whose stored log differs. What would change it: a repair that has to build a release the historical folder does not hold.

## D-63 The reference side of a repair (docs/interfaces.md 7.2, 9.5)
- Built: capture off, no store, the same seeds, layouts and options; a set uses the generator's clean folder (`--reference-set`); the fixture uses the spec with the records of the withdrawn source, and every generation output derived from them, left out (`--reference-withdrawn` names the source). Other option: take the leave-out list from the report. Result of the other option: the reference would equal the repaired side whatever the report says, so no wrong report could be caught. What would change it: the fixture spec gaining a clean variant.

## D-64 Names of the successors a repair registers (docs/interfaces.md 9.2, 9.5)
- Built: the repaired build runs with `--name-prefix "successor of "` plus the historical prefix, so a rewritten artifact registers as `successor of R1`; source names, generation outputs, releases and imports left unchanged keep their instance and gain a second name; member and job names carry no prefix. The summary lists `successors`, `successors_not_registered` and `kept`. For shards and for the mixtures that snapshot them the kept artifact is not the same instance: a shard's input files are registered as found files under their prefixed names, and a found file's name is part of its instance id; `kept` reports `same_instance` as true or false for each. Other option: a separate store or no prefix. Result of the other option: either splits the history or collides on names. What would change it: [added at the merge] a ruling that a kept shard must be the same instance; the registration of found files would then change, and docs/replica.md and the verification's kept check follow.
- AMENDED (the `kept` field named above): `kept[].same_instance` is true, false or null, and the entry gains `registered_as`. Built: `registered_as` is the successor name when the repaired build registered the artifact under its second name and null when it did not; `same_instance` is true when that instance equals the one the report holds, false when it differs, and null when the artifact is not registered under its second name or the report holds no instance for it. Other option: a boolean only, false where nothing was registered. Result of the other option: an artifact the repaired build never registered would read as a different instance, a statement the replica cannot make. What would change it: a ruling that an unregistered kept artifact is an error of the repair.

## D-65 The replica's words (docs/interfaces.md 1, 9.5)
- Built: the report form, the sides form, the successor prefix and the action words are local constants in `replica/repair.py`, because docs/interfaces.md section 1 allows the vocabulary import only in the capture hooks; a check confirms they equal `waterwhite.vocabulary`. Other option: import `waterwhite.vocabulary` lazily. Result of the other option: it breaks the wall text as written. A test should pin the equality too. What would change it: [added at the merge] a change of the wall text of section 1 that lets the replica read the vocabulary.
- AMENDED (the test this entry asks for): a test now pins the equality, `tests/replica/test_replica_repair_words.py`. Built: it reads the thirteen words of `replica/repair.py` (the two form words, the successor prefix, the five record action words, the three artifact action words, the two kinds) and the two action tuples with `ast`, never by importing the replica, and compares them with `waterwhite.vocabulary`; the tuples are the vocabulary's `RECORD_ACTIONS` and `ARTIFACT_ACTIONS` each plus `UNRESOLVED`. Other option: a test that imports the replica module. Result of the other option: the test would run the replica's imports, which the wall of docs/interfaces.md section 1 keeps out of the report-reading code. What would change it: a change of the wall text that lets the replica read the vocabulary.

## D-66 Tool errors during the capture build of a repair (docs/interfaces.md 9.5)
- Built: any exception from a waterwhite module becomes a one-line `CaptureError` (exit 1); the folder and the store are left part-written, and docs/replica.md says so. Other option: a traceback, as the other capture builds do today. Result of the other option: it breaks the one-line-refusal contract of 9.5. What would change it: [added at the merge] a ruling that every capture build prints a traceback, or that the folder is removed on a failure.
- AMENDED (the scope of the conversion): Built: the one-line `CaptureError` covers an exception whose class module begins "waterwhite", raised inside the repaired side's capture build (the `build` call with capture on) only; an error of the replica itself passes through as it is, and so does a failure outside that call (the checks before it, the opening of the store, the reference side's build). The store is opened by `StoreCapture.open` before the `try`, so an input error about the store path (D-81) is raised before it. Other option: convert every exception of the command. Result of the other option: a defect of the replica would be reported as a refusal of the tool's store. What would change it: a ruling that every failure of a repair prints one line.

## D-67 How `verify` is handed the sides (docs/interfaces.md 7.2, 7.3)
- Built: a sides file (docs/interfaces.md 7.2) that names, by the historical artifact name, the file of the repaired side and of the reference side; `verify` takes the parsed documents plus `base`, the folder of the sides file; the artifact's `kind` in the report (not the file name) decides JSONL multiset versus shard digest; a mixture has no file. Other option: `--repaired DIR --reference DIR` with a layout convention the tool knows. Result of the other option: the tool must know a team's folder layout, which the wall of section 1 and the builder's independence rule out, and a file the builder named differently could not be compared. What would change it: a builder that always writes one fixed layout.
- AMENDED (the build mode, as built; plan 4.2; docs/interfaces.md 7.3; docs/cli.md "verify"): the caller tells `verify` how the build was made. Built: `verify --mode seeded|unseeded` (`cli.py` `--mode`, `choices=v.BUILD_MODES`, default `v.BUILD_UNSEEDED`; `verify_sides.verify(mode=...)`) says whether every step of the team's build was seeded, and the default is "unseeded", the weaker claim. The value is echoed as `mode` in the document and in the text form, and a value outside the two is refused as a usage error (exit 1) before anything is read. It decides one thing: `per_shard_digests` reads "equal" or "differ" only in seeded mode and only where at least one shard is named on both sides of the sides file, and reads "not compared" otherwise; `shards` lists the digests of every shard named on both sides in either mode. In seeded mode "equal" also needs every keep or rewrite shard of the report to be named on both sides, with the one exception that D-82 and its AMENDED line state for a rewritten shard the report's own count and the store show is left no document. The record multisets do not read the mode. Ruled during M4: `--mode` is plan 4.2's build mode and nothing else; the restart of the exact rerun does not read it and is read through the run record (D-73, D-74), under a key that cannot be taken for it. Other option: read the mode from the run record (its random seed), or from a key of the sides file. Result of the other option: for the run record (traced: D-74 says so of the mode), a record that holds a seed says the training run's shuffle was seeded and says nothing of how the team's builder built the shards, so a build with one unseeded step would be called seeded and its shards that differ only by what that step does would read "differ" in `per_shard_digests` (inferred); for a key of the sides file (docs/interfaces.md 7.2 gives the file no such key), the claim would be written by whoever built the sides, and a builder that wrote "seeded" for a build with an unseeded step would turn on a byte comparison that build cannot meet (inferred), and the form of the sides file would gain a key. Other option: default "seeded". Result of the other option (inferred): a verification run without the flag on a build with an unseeded step would read "differ" for shard digests while the multisets are equal, a difference the build is not at fault for; the default "unseeded" reads "not compared" there (the built behaviour: tests/rebuild/test_verify_sides.py::test_in_unseeded_mode_the_digests_are_listed_and_not_compared). What would change it: a sides file or a build record that carries the builder's own statement of how its steps were seeded, read in place of the flag. Its checks: tests/rebuild/test_verify_command.py::test_verify_prints_the_document_of_the_form (the default reads "unseeded"); tests/rebuild/test_verify_sides.py::test_in_seeded_mode_equal_shards_have_equal_digests and ::test_in_seeded_mode_one_changed_byte_makes_the_digests_differ. At 42f3068 what `verify` compares in a shard comparison is the shards the sides file names on both sides and the keep or rewrite shards of the report that the sides lack (named in `differences`, never "equal"); a shard that both builds wrote and that neither the report lists nor the sides file names is seen by neither loop, so it cannot keep "equal" from being read, and one the sides file names on both sides but the report does not list is a note in `differences` and is not compared. The owner decided a change to this, not built at this commit (the entry on where a returning copy lands, D-141). [written at the M4 check]

## D-68 The wording and basis of "no exposure-free restart is proven" (docs/interfaces.md 7.3; plan 4.3)
- Built: a run entry with `proven` false, checkpoint, segment and step null, the basis the sentence `RESTART_NOT_PROVEN`, and `from_scratch` = the first step of the first segment of the run the lineage descends from (a walk up the parent checkpoints), the basis `RESTART_BASIS_LINEAGE_START`; a checkpoint qualifies when its own coverage answer is negative (read through `Answer.is_negative`), and the restart is the last such checkpoint in segment then step order. Other option: name the earliest checkpoint, or give no entry for such a run. Result of the other option: the earliest checkpoint would be called clean without a proof (a checkpoint that cannot be shown clean named as a restart); no entry would hide the run from the report. What would change it: a ruling that a team wants the earliest registered checkpoint offered with the word "unproven".
- AMENDED by D-73: a run that has a stale segment and no registered checkpoint has an entry too, built by `restart_free.unproven_entry` (the entry of this decision for a run with no qualifying checkpoint: `proven` false, `from_scratch` the lineage start), and every entry ends with the key `exact_rerun`. `restart_free.exposure_free_restarts` itself is unchanged and still gives an entry only for a run that has registered checkpoints; `build_report` adds the other entries, in the order the runs were registered.

## D-69 Whether the exact-rerun restart needs a verified join (docs/interfaces.md 7.3; plan 4.3)
- Built: the corrected order is built with the join's own `compute_join` on a copy of the segment's run record (shards replaced by the corrected mixture's shards, the digests of the index arrays replaced by those of what `replay_arrays` derived, `store.verified_joins()` as the gate), and the registered traces are compared step by step in order; a release or sampler the join is not verified for, a cyclic run whose shuffle digests do not hold, an unseeded run or any failure give `rebuilt` false with the reason and the fallback of the phase start (`step_comparison` "not possible"). `rebuilt` and the restart use the same build, so they cannot disagree. Other option: derive the arrays with `replay_arrays` and the sampler directly, with no gate. Result of the other option: a step is stated for a release nobody verified the join for, a statement about a data order the tool has no check behind. What would change it: a ruling that the exact rerun rests on the team's own settings and needs no verified join.
- AMENDED by D-74: "an unseeded run" in this entry reads "a run whose record lacks a setting a replay needs". Built (as D-74 corrects it): one function, `join.missing_replay_settings`, names the settings a replay needs (sequence length, random seed, split, add-extra-token setting, and the train length, iterations or samples, with its batch size), and the rebuild asks it. Other option: the random seed alone. Result of the other option: a record that holds a seed and lacks the split would read as replayable and still fall back to the phase start in `verify`. What would change it: a replay that needs another setting.

## D-70 The cause a successor's "may contain" names (docs/interfaces.md 7.3; plan 2.2; D-49)
- Built: the answer is passed through as `coverage.answer` gives it; `names` is the named cause of the failing link of the proof (a missing origin in the test); `holds_unresolved` is a separate boolean computed from the per-record verdicts of the session; `NAMES_UNRESOLVED_RECORD` stays unused. Other option: make the answer name "unresolved record". Result of the other option: a change in coverage and answers, and the answer would hide which link failed, replacing the link by a word of the rebuild. What would change it: a ruling to use the constant or to remove it from the vocabulary.

## D-71 What a mixture digest is, for the stale-cache check (docs/rebuild.md 5; docs/interfaces.md 7.3)
- Built: `ids.mixture_content_digest` of the content digests recorded in the mixture's snapshot, in the trainer's order, so it covers what the index caches were built from and nothing of the folder; a segment is stale when the digest of its registered mixture differs from that of the mixture the corrected run reads. Other option: a digest that also holds the shard paths, or digests of the shard files read from disk at verification time. Result of the path option: a mixture moved to another folder looks stale; of the disk option: it needs the files at verification time and could disagree with the snapshot a segment was registered with (the `matches_sides` field already covers a file that is not the registered one). What would change it: a trainer whose cache key holds content.

## D-72 Further choices of the verification (docs/interfaces.md 7.1, 7.3)
- (a) `successor` is a required key of an artifact entry of a report (null allowed), as 7.3 says; 7.1 says a missing key and a null are alike. Other option: treat a missing key as null. Result of the other option: a report that lost a key is verified instead of refused. What would change it: [added at the merge] the text of 7.1 and 7.3 made to agree on a missing key.
- (b) A line that holds no text is counted under the fingerprint null as one multiset key, so a difference in blank or unparseable lines is a difference of the sides. Other option: leave them out. Result of the other option: a repaired file with junk lines would read equal. What would change it: [added at the merge] a team whose files hold blank lines by design.
- (c) `holds_unresolved` is read per record through `Session.occurrence`, `shard_gate` and `document` (a verdict with a failing link, or a chain that ends at a file-level link), false for a shard with no map, null where no successor is registered. Other option: derive it from the answer's word. Result of the other option: an upstream-missing record would be missed and a missing map would be called an unresolved record. What would change it: [added at the merge] a coverage answer that names the unresolved record itself.
- (d) The step comparison compares (sample position, rank, shard position, ordinal, token range) of each row and leaves out `global_index`. Other option: compare the whole row as the oracle's log does. Result of the other option: a different global index for the same document would count as a difference. What would change it: [added at the merge] a global index that the trainer fixes for a document.
- (e) A name only one side holds, or that the report does not list, is a `differences` entry with null counts and a `note`, not counted in `differing_records`. Other option: refuse. Result of the other option: a builder's extra file would stop the verification. What would change it: [added at the merge] a ruling that an extra file must stop a claim of equality.
- (f) A loud fault (`IntegrityFault`) raised inside the cache or restart build is not turned into a reason and stops the command (exit 3), as 7.3 says; every other failure of the build is a reason. Other option [added at the merge]: turn every failure into a reason. Result of the other option [added at the merge]: a store that fails its loud checks would read as an ordinary "not rebuilt". What would change it: [added at the merge] a change of 7.3 on loud faults.
- AMENDED (a): the keys `verify` requires of a report, as built (`verify_sides.report_artifacts`, `report_unresolved`, `report_request`): the form word; for every entry of `artifacts` its `artifact`, `instance`, `kind`, `action` and `successor` (null allowed), where the action is one of "keep", "rewrite" and "unresolved"; `counts.records.unresolved` as a whole number; and a `request` that names a source and is a withdrawal. An entry whose `instance` the store does not hold is refused as well (D-80). Other option: require fewer keys and read the rest from the store. Result of the other option: a report that lost a key would be verified against what the store holds now and not against what the report was made for. What would change it: [none found]; a report form that drops one of these keys.
- AMENDED (c): ruled by result: `verify` never shows a successor as holding no unresolved record when the report's rule would count one of its records unresolved. Built: one check, `records.is_unresolved` (and `records.is_unresolved_document` for a document of a shard, through `records.judge_document`, which `artifacts.shard_entry` calls too), is the judgement of `records.judge` read for its word, and for a document it is also true where the document's own verdict is unresolved by itself (so no failing link that the old check saw is lost); the report's entries read `records.judge` and `records.judge_document` directly and `verify_successors._holds_unresolved` reads the same judgement through these two, so for the failing link, a file-level edge, a file-level link and a mapped link at any hop of the chain, and the "any" edge whose other parents decide nothing, `verify` is never less strict than the report. The two do not always agree: the document's own verdict, which `verify` also reads, can count a document unresolved that the report retires or keeps. No write verb makes such a store (every map row is written through the label check, and only a "recovered" row names a parent); a map-row label edited outside the write path does. Its check: tests/rebuild/test_unresolved_branches.py (both cases, and the "any" edge). The old check in `verify_successors` looked only at the last hop and only for the file-level link, so a file-level edge in a chain, or a mapped link, was reported unresolved and verified as clean. The report's answers and counts are as they were; only `holds_unresolved` changes, and no acceptance row reads it. A pointer-only edge (a mapped edge) keeps a definite action and is not a source link. Other option: a second hop check in `verify_successors` that repeats the report's weak-link list. Result of the other option: two lists of the weak links that agree today and part the day one of them is edited. What would change it: a ruling that `verify` states its own word for an unresolved record.
- Its check: tests/rebuild/test_verify_one_rule.py.
- AMENDED (D-72 (e); D-67): in `verify_sides.verify` the check of the report's kind comes before the check of the sides. A name the report lists with a kind that is neither JSONL nor shard (a mixture; a hand-written report may also give a checkpoint) is skipped without a note, whether one side or both name it, because a mixture has no file (D-67): it is neither compared nor listed in `differences`. A name the report does not list at all, and a JSONL or shard name that only one side holds, still get the `differences` entry with a `note` of (e). Other option: a `differences` entry with a note for every name that is not compared, a mixture's too. Result of the other option (inferred): a sides file that names a mixture would show in `differences` with null counts and would not move `differing_records`; as built it shows nothing. None found that pins the skip (grepping tests/rebuild/test_verify_sides.py and test_verify_command.py for "mixture" and "mix-" finds nothing); tests/rebuild/test_verify_sides.py::test_a_name_on_one_side_only_is_not_compared_and_is_listed pins the one-sided note of (e). What would change it: a builder that writes a file for a mixture. [written at the M4 check]

## D-73 Where the exact-rerun restart is stated (plan 4.3, BF-5; docs/interfaces.md 7.1, 7.3)
- Built (plan 4.3, BF-5): `verify` states the exact-rerun restart of a replayable run, and the report says so; the report itself gives the restart of a run that is not replayable. Every entry of the report's `restarts` ends with the key `exact_rerun`: null for a run with no stale segment (a segment whose mixture the report lists under `needs_successor`), otherwise an object with the keys `run_record_replayable`, `segment`, `step`, `basis` and `step_comparison`, `segment` being the earliest stale segment of the run. A replayable run has `step` null, `basis` = `RESTART_EXACT_BY_VERIFY` and `step_comparison` null; a run that is not replayable has the phase start as `step` (the first step of the earliest stale segment, as the fallback of `restart_exact` computes it), `RESTART_BASIS_PHASE_START` and `STEP_COMPARISON_NOT_POSSIBLE`. In code: `restart_free.exposure_free_restarts` keeps its entries exactly; `build_report` adds `exact_rerun` from `restart_exact.exact_rerun_of_report`, and the entry of a run with a stale segment and no registered checkpoint (D-68). The text form prints one line per run with a stale segment under that run's line. The key is `run_record_replayable` and never "seeded", so that no reader takes it for `verify --mode` (plan 4.2's build mode, the byte compare of shards), which the docs name apart. The report's `exact_rerun` and `verify`'s `restarts` give the same runs, and for a run that is not replayable the same segment, step and basis (the first-step function is shared, in `replay_check.py`). Where they can differ: a mixture the report says needs a successor and for which the store holds no successor is stale for the report and has no entry in `verify`'s `restarts`; the verification says why in the `reason` of its `caches` entry. Other option: the report computes the restart of a seeded run itself. Result of the other option: the report would need the successor shards, which exist only after the team's builder has made them from this report, so the report could not be the document the builder is handed. What would change it: a corrected data order that can be computed without the successor shards.

## D-74 One check of a replayable run record, and why nothing refuses a changed record (plan 4.3, 3.5; docs/interfaces.md 7.1, 7.3)
- Built: a run is replayable when its record holds every setting a replay needs, which is the definition of docs/interfaces.md 7.3: the sequence length, the random seed, the split, the add-extra-token setting, and the train iterations or samples with the global batch size, the five that `join.replay_arrays` checks. One function, `join.missing_replay_settings`, makes the check; `replay_arrays` refuses by it, and `rebuild/replay_check.py` asks it for the report and for `verify` (`restart_exact.py`). A verification of a run that fails it gives the phase start without trying the replay. The check is made run by run over every stale segment of the run: the run is replayable only when each stale segment has a run record that holds every setting, which matches the rule of `verify` that the corrected order is built only when every stale segment was rebuilt; a stale segment with no run record, or one the join cannot parse, makes the run not replayable. A seed outside the range of the generator is a held setting that the replay refuses on its own, and the check does not look at it. Other option: `random_seed` alone, or the caller's `--mode` handed to the report. Result of the other options: a record that holds a seed and lacks the split would be called seeded by the report and would still fall back to the phase start in `verify`, so a report and a verification could state different restart bases for one run; and the mode is the caller's claim about how the builder built (plan 4.2) and says nothing about the run record. What would change it: a replay that needs another setting, which moves the one function and both users with it.
- Why nothing refuses a changed record: a registered run record cannot change through the tool (`run_segment` has no UPDATE path, and a segment registered again with other contents is refused in `Store.register_run_segment`); only a hand edit of the store file, or `verify` handed another store than the one the report was made from, can make the report's statement and the verification's differ. Nothing is built for it. The other-store case is open: `verify` reads the report's request and artifacts and no store identity, and a report made on one store and verified on another is not refused for that reason.
- What this entry changes in D-69 (read D-69 with it): the restart of a run whose records fail the shared check is the phase start without the replay being tried; the corrected order is built for the restart only for a run that passes the check. The `rebuilt` of a `caches` entry still comes from the build and gives its reason as before. "An unseeded run" in D-69 reads "a run whose record lacks a setting a replay needs" (the random seed is one of five).
- AMENDED by D-80: the other-store case that this entry leaves open is settled by result. Built: `verify` refuses a report that names an artifact by an instance the store does not hold, and puts the report's request to the store again, so a member or a record of the report that the store no longer holds is refused by name; a store that holds every instance of the report and expands its request alike, such as a faithful copy with the successors registered, is certified. It is not keyed on which store file `verify` is handed. Other option: record a store identity in the report and compare it. Result of the other option: the copy that Check 9 verifies, with the successors registered between the report and the verification, would be refused as another store. What would change it: a store that carries an identity that survives registering successors.
- AMENDED (the restart over a mixture that reads the fourth word; D-141, D-146; docs/interfaces.md 7.1): `restart_exact.exact_rerun_of_report` takes the segments over a mixture that is rewritten and the segments over one that reads "clean, place not stated" as the segments that may be stale, and gives a run with a segment over the second kind the phase start of its earliest one as an assumption (`RESTART_BASIS_PHASE_START`, step comparison "not possible"), whether or not its run record is replayable (`run_record_replayable` still states what the records say, and the text form tells the two cases by the basis): `verify` names no corrected mixture for a mixture that reads the word, so it could not state the restart the replayable case leaves to it. A run over a kept mixture still has `exact_rerun` null. Other option: give such a run `exact_rerun` null, as a run over a kept mixture. Result of the other option (traced: the decided condition in D-141, no run reads "exact rerun not needed" over a mixture whose place is not stated): the report would say a run needs no exact rerun while a copy that may land in its shard is not placed. Other option: leave the restart to `verify` as for a replayable run. Result of the other option (inferred from `verify_caches`, which reads such a mixture as an unresolved one): the run would never get its restart stated. What would change it: a `verify` that builds the corrected mixture for a mixture that reads the word. The sentence of the first bullet above, "The other-store case is open", was settled by D-80 and stands as D-80 says. [written for the keep part]

## D-75 How a stale proof is told apart from a closed store and from a forged proof (docs/interfaces.md 2; plan 2.2, W6, Decision 1)
- Built: the mint's register answers three ways for a proof (current, written, unknown), and `answers._check` takes the staleness last, after every check of identity (minted, same artifact and request, a name of the artifact, the notes, the shape). A proof not minted by `prove`, of another artifact or request, with a name that is no name of the artifact or with other notes is `ProofRequired` whatever the store holds; a minted proof whose store is open and whose mark moved is `StoreWrittenDuringQuery`; a minted proof whose store is closed, or gone (the store is held weakly), is `ProofRequired`, because a closed store has no mark and cannot say whether it was written, and handing the answer type a proof of it is a mistake of the caller (docs/interfaces.md section 2). The proof's function (`Session.holds_for_proof`) returns False for a written store and raises for a closed or gone one; the register turns a raise into "unknown". The answer type imports the error from `errors.py`, which imports nothing of the package. Other option: any stale or closed proof as `StoreWrittenDuringQuery`, or all as `ProofRequired`. Result of the other option: the first would call a closed store a write and send a caller who closed the store to run the command again; the second leaves the window between prove's last check and the answer with a refusal that has no name or exit code. A finding ("contains", "may contain", "not covered") carries no proof to check, so the same window for a finding is closed by the command's final check (D-76), and a library caller that builds an answer from a finding after a write is not refused. What would change it: a ruling that the closed case is also a write.
- What this entry changes in D-48 (read D-48 with it): a proof the mint made whose store was written since the walk builds no answer and ends in `StoreWrittenDuringQuery`, not in `ProofRequired`; a proof whose store is closed or gone stays `ProofRequired`, and so does a proof the mint did not make, whatever the store holds (the checks of the proof's identity run first). The text "the store was written after this proof was made, or is closed" is no longer a message of the tool: the closed case says that the store the proof was made from is closed or gone. Result of keeping the old refusal: a write between prove's last check and the building of the answer ended in a refusal with no name or exit code, and the command in a traceback. Its check: tests/coverage/test_stale_proof_named_error.py.
- AMENDED (plan 2.2, W6, Decision 1; docs/interfaces.md 2): D-75 says the register "turns a raise into 'unknown'" for a closed or gone store. Built, exactly: `state_of` in `answers._proof_gate` catches ANY `Exception` raised by the proof's function and answers unknown, not only the `LookupError` for a store that is gone and the refusal of a closed store; an unknown state of a minted proof ends in `ProofRequired` ("the store the proof was made from is closed or gone; prove again"). Other option: catch only those two. Result of the other option (inferred): another exception from the store's mark, a failure of the connection for instance, would reach the caller as that exception and not as `ProofRequired`; no answer would be built in either case, so no "does not contain" passes. No test of its own found: grep of tests/coverage/test_stale_proof_named_error.py and tests/coverage/test_coverage_answers.py for a closed store finds none (the one `close()` in the first is another connection's). What would change it: a ruling that an unexpected error of the store must show as itself. [written at the M4 check]

## D-76 The final check of the store's mark before the output (docs/interfaces.md 2; docs/cli.md)
- Built: `query`, `report` and `verify` take `Store.state_mark()` right after opening the store, build their output (the JSON form and the text form), and compare the mark once more as the last step inside the open store, before anything is printed (`cli._unchanged_since`), refusing with `StoreWrittenDuringQuery` when it moved. A write by another connection in any window of the command so ends in the named error, with the JSON error on standard error, exit code 2 and empty standard output; a write after the output is printed is outside the command. Other option: only the checks inside the session and the answer type. Result of the other option: a write after the last answer is built, or in a window a session did not cover (the answers of `verify` come from a session of its own, and a finding carries no proof to check), would print the output of a store that had changed. What would change it: none found; a command that wrote to the store would need its own mark.

## D-77 Further choices of the exact-rerun restart in the report (docs/interfaces.md 7.1; docs/cli.md "report")
- (a) The text form prints, under the line of each run that has a stale segment, one line that names the run and the segment and says the same as the JSON: for a replayable run the `RESTART_EXACT_BY_VERIFY` wording; for another run the step, the `RESTART_BASIS_PHASE_START` wording and the `STEP_COMPARISON_NOT_POSSIBLE` word. Other option: a separate list of exact-rerun lines after the restarts. Result of the other option: a reader of the restarts would see two lists that name the same runs. What would change it: a ruling on the wording of the text form (docs/cli.md says it is not fixed).
- (b) A run that has a stale segment and no registered checkpoint gets the entry of a run for which no exposure-free restart is proven (D-68), because no checkpoint can be shown clean for a run that registers none, with its lineage start in `from_scratch`. Other option: an entry with only `exact_rerun`. Result of the other option: the keys of an entry would differ from run to run. What would change it: a ruling that such a run has no exposure-free restart to state.
- (c) `StoreWrittenDuringQuery` has one default detail (the store changed after the query began; run the command again), so that its three raises (D-56) say the same. Other option: each raise its own sentence. Result of the other option: the same error would read differently by the window that found it. What would change it: a ruling that the named error carries a detail that names the window.
- (d) The first-step helper of a segment lives once, in `rebuild/replay_check.py`, and `restart_free.py` and `restart_exact.py` both use it. Other option: a copy in each. Result of the other option: the two restarts of a run could name different first steps if one copy changed. What would change it: a restart that takes its first step from something other than the segment's launches and steps.

## D-78 The files upstream of the artifacts are listed in the report (plan 4.1, BF-6; docs/interfaces.md 7.1; docs/rebuild.md 2.2a)
- (a) Built: a new key `upstream_files`, after `artifacts`, lists every registered file of kind jsonl that `artifacts` does not hold (`artifacts.wanted` is false for it) whose answer is not the negative one, in registration order, each as an entry with `artifact`, `instance`, `kind`, `role`, `answer`, `action` and `reason`, plus `names` and `failing` where the answer is "may contain". The list is built in `rebuild/upstream.py` from `coverage.answer` in the report's own session, and it is outside `counts`, `bytes_to_rewrite`, `groups` and `mixtures`, which keep every value they had. Other option: put the files into `artifacts` with an action. Result of the other option: `counts` and `bytes_to_rewrite` would change, and the builder's own files would have an action the plan does not give them. What would change it: a ruling that these files count.
- (b) Built: a generation output is a file whose records carry a generation edge in the store (`store.edges`, `is_generation`), read as the store records it and not from a name or a role, because the role of a generation output and of a rebuilt intermediate file is the same word. Other option: tell them by the file's name, or by the step name. Result of the other option: a name is the team's to choose and a step name is free text, so a renamed generation output would be called a rebuilt file. What would change it: a role word for generation outputs in the vocabulary.
- (c) Built: the order of the rules is the answer first. "May contain" and "not covered" give "unresolved" (with the answer's own `names` and `failing` for the first); then a source file that holds a part of a member the request names gives action null, with the reason "the revision the request withdraws" for a request that names no records and, for a request narrowed to records, a reason with the count of the records the request names in that file; then a file with a generation edge gives null with the reason that the records of it that the request reaches leave the release that copies them and the file itself is left as it is; every other file whose answer is "contains" gives "rewrite". A source file the request does not name whose answer is "contains" gives "unresolved" with a reason. Other option: decide by role first. Result of the other option: a source file whose answer is "may contain" would read as a withdrawn revision, hiding the open question the answer carries. What would change it: a ruling on what a source file the request does not name is to be called.
- (d) Built: the replica and `verify` read nothing of the list (the tests of the replica's repair and of the verification hand each the same report with the key changed or left out). Other option: let the verification check the list against the store. Result of the other option: a second place that states what a file's answer is, which can disagree with the answers `verify` puts itself. What would change it: a ruling that the list is a contract.
- Its checks: tests/rebuild/test_report_upstream_files.py, tests/replica/test_replica_main_upstream_files.py, tests/replica/test_replica_repair_upstream.py.

## D-79 The per-layout count states whether it was made (plan 4.1, BF-7; docs/interfaces.md 7.1; docs/rebuild.md 2.3)
- Built: `groups.by_layout` stays as it was in both cases, and a new sibling key `groups.layouts` holds `counted`, `reason` and `unmatched_shards`: with no layout named `counted` is false, `reason` says that no layout is recorded and names `--layout NAME=PREFIX`, and the list is empty; with layouts named `counted` is true, `reason` is null and `unmatched_shards` holds, in registration order, every shard that no named prefix matches (the same test as `by_layout`, `counts._matching`). Shards are counted per run in both cases. Other option: fill `by_layout` with a key such as "unmatched" or with a note. Result of the other option: `by_layout` would hold a key that is not a layout, and the rows of Check 9 that read it, and the tests that pin it as an empty object without layouts, would read a statement as a group. What would change it: a ruling to change the form of `by_layout` itself.
- Its check: tests/rebuild/test_report_layout_statement.py.

## D-80 Verify certifies a repair only for the scope and the artifacts of the report (plan 4.2, 4.4; docs/interfaces.md 7.3; D-74)
- Built: two refusals of the family "request refused" (exit 2), both before any comparison. (a) The scope: the code already gives the result and no code was added for it. `verify_sides.report_request` puts the request of the report to the store again through `requests.withdraw` with the members and the records the report lists (not with the source alone), and `requests.expand` refuses a member the store does not hold (`MemberNotRegistered`, the loop over `asked`), a record whose artifact or line the store does not hold (`_resolve_record`, `RecordNotInSource`) and a record no covered member holds (`RecordNotInSource`, in `expand`). A report for more members or records than the store has is therefore refused, and the store can never expand the report's own list to less, because the report's list is what is asked. (b) The artifacts: `verify_sides.require_instances_held` refuses an entry of `artifacts` whose `instance` the store does not hold (compared as the instance id itself, not a name that resolves to one), naming the artifact. It lives in `verify`, not in `report_artifacts`, because the successor and cache checks read the entries without that precondition (a test hands `check_successors` an all-zero instance and expects `instance_unchanged` false). The result is by what the store holds and not by which file it is: Check 9 prints the report from one store and verifies it on a copy where the successors are registered, which holds every instance of the report and is certified. Other option: record an identity of the store in the report and refuse any other. Result of the other option: that copy would be refused. Other option: refuse only a narrower request. Result of the other option: a store that has lost an artifact would be certified for it. What would change it: a store identity that survives the registration of successors.
- Its checks: tests/rebuild/test_verify_scope.py.

## D-81 The replica's repair refuses a store path that holds no store (docs/interfaces.md 9.5; docs/replica.md 15)
- Built: `replica.repair.repair` raises the replica's `InputError` (exit 1, one line "replica: InputError: ..." naming the path) when the path given as `--store` is not a file. It is the last refusal before the store is opened, after the report, the folders, the sides path and the checks of both builds (which write nothing), so every other refusal keeps its precedence. Other option: let `StoreCapture.open` create the store, as it did. Result of the other option: a mistyped path ended in a repair registered into an empty store, with successors whose history was nothing, and a folder for the store. What would change it: a repair that is meant to start a store (the historical build is then the caller's job, and a creating option would say so).
- Its check: tests/replica/test_replica_repair_store.py.
- AMENDED: the refusal covers a path that holds a file that is not a store. Built: `replica.repair._require_a_store` reads the file with the standard library `sqlite3` through a connection that cannot write (`immutable=1` where no write-ahead log lies beside the file, so no sibling file is made; `mode=ro` where one does) for the schema version row of the store's `meta` table, and raises the same `InputError` (exit 1, one line) for arbitrary bytes, for a database with no such row or table, and for any other `sqlite3` error; it runs at the same place as the is-a-file refusal, before `StoreCapture.open`, so nothing is created and the file keeps its bytes. A store of any schema version passes: the migration and the newer-version refusals stay with the tool's own open. Other option: let `StoreCapture.open` raise the tool's `NotAStore` and have `replica/cli.py` catch it. Result of the other option: the writable open has already rewritten a foreign database (journal mode and the file header change) and a sibling file may exist, and the tool's error name would be printed as an error of the replica's command line. What would change it: a ruling that the replica imports the tool's store module for its own checks.
- Its check: tests/replica/test_replica_repair_store_file.py.

## D-82 A verification never reads "equal" over a set that lacks an artifact of the report (plan 4.2, 4.4; docs/interfaces.md 7.3)
- Built: for every entry of the report whose action is keep or rewrite and whose kind is jsonl or shard, a name the sides do not give on both sides is kept in a list per kind; with a sides file given, a name on neither side is added to `differences` as a one-sided entry with the note "named on neither side, not compared" (a name on one side only was listed already), and `multisets` and `per_shard_digests` read "differ" where a compared file differs, "not compared" where a file lacks and nothing compared differs, and "equal" only when none lacks. `unresolved_records` above 0 still gives "not claimed" for the multisets, and the per-shard digests are still "not compared" outside the seeded mode. Without a sides file `differences` stays empty. Other option: a new top-level key naming the missing files. Result of the other option: the verification would hold a key the pinned list of keys of the command does not have. Other option: leave a missing file out of the reading. Result of the other option: a repair that rebuilt one release of two would read "equal". What would change it: a ruling that the verification has a key of its own for this.
- Its check: tests/rebuild/test_verify_missing_artifacts.py. (As first built; for a shard or file the report leaves no record, the AMENDED line below supersedes the rest of this line.) The sides file of the replica names only what its builds wrote (an artifact a side did not write is left out of that side, docs/replica.md section 15), so a report with a shard no record reaches, which a build does not write, reads "not compared" for the digests and not "equal". The fixture rows of Check 9 are repaired with no reference side (every name is on one side only, as before), so their comparisons are unchanged; the rows of the 10k sets are gated and were not run here, so whether the sides of those builds name every keep or rewrite file and shard on both sides is not shown by this change.
- AMENDED (the emptied artifact; ruled by result, amending the reading above): an artifact of the report that is rewritten and on neither side of the sides file does not hold "equal" back, for the multisets and for the per-shard digests, only when the tool's own record shows the repair leaves it no record: `verify_sides.left_with_no_record` reads the report's own entry (`rows_in_successor` 0 for a file, `documents_in_successor` 0 for a shard, a whole number and not a truth value) and the store (a successor registered for it must hold no record either). It is never decided on the replica's word: `successors_not_registered` of the replica puts every rewrite with no registered successor there, whatever the reason, and cannot tell an emptied shard from a lost one. Such an artifact is named in `differences` with a note of its own ("the report leaves the file no row, so neither side writes it; nothing to compare", and the same for a shard with "document") and no new key, since the pinned list of keys of the command allows none; a file or shard that should hold records and is on neither side keeps its note and still blocks. A report with no count (a hand-written one) shows nothing and blocks as before. Other option: decide emptiness from the replica's `successors_not_registered`. Result of the other option: a shard lost from the repaired side would read as emptied and the digests would read "equal" over a set that lacks it. Other option: leave every file on neither side blocking. Result of the other option: the 10k partitioned set, which has a shard per source file, could never read "equal" under a withdrawal of one source, since the repaired shard of that source holds no row and is not written, and the reference has none. What would change it: a report that states where re-sourced copies land, which would let a shard whose documents are all re-sourced elsewhere be shown as left with no record (today `documents_in_successor` counts the documents that stay and does not show it, so such a shard is read as one that holds records).
- Its check: tests/rebuild/test_verify_empty_artifacts.py, tests/replica/test_replica_repair_empty_shard.py.

## D-83 `identical` of a kept artifact is read against the report's own instance only (plan 4.4; docs/interfaces.md 7.3)
- Built: `verify_sides._kept_entry` takes the recorded digest from the artifact whose instance id is the one the report names, and from nothing else. Where the store does not hold that instance, `identical` and `recorded` are null and the entry gains a `reason` key that says so; where it holds it the entry has the four keys it always had. Other option: fall back to the artifact the name resolves to now. Result of the other option: a kept file would be called identical to an artifact of another history because the two share a name. What would change it: a store that is renamed by design between the report and the verification.
- Its check: tests/rebuild/test_verify_missing_artifacts.py (the unit test of the entry; through `verify` the case is refused first, D-80).

## D-84 The replica's words are tested, and the wall of section 1 names the probe-run imports (docs/interfaces.md 1, 9.5; D-65)
- Built: a test reads the words of `replica/repair.py` with `ast` and compares them with the vocabulary (D-65 amended); it passes on the tree it was written for, by design, and fails when one word of the replica changes. docs/interfaces.md section 1 now says that `replica/probe_run.py` imports `waterwhite.vocabulary`, `waterwhite.errors` and `waterwhite.store.store` outside the capture hooks, as the probe-run command registers what it runs. No code changed. Other option: change `probe_run.py` to read no vocabulary or store. Result of the other option: a command that registers runs would have to write the store through the tool's own command alone, and its checks of the verified-join list and of the store's shards would need a second route. What would change it: a ruling that the replica's modules outside the capture hooks import nothing of the tool.
- Its check: tests/replica/test_replica_repair_words.py.

## D-85 The printed verification names each cache entry it has no answer for (docs/cli.md "verify"; docs/interfaces.md 7.3)
