- Built: `cli._verification_text` prints one line for every `caches` entry whose `stale` is null, "no answer for the caches of SEGMENT: mixture NAME, REASON", with the reason the JSON entry holds (the report rewrites the mixture and no successor is registered, or the report leaves the mixture unresolved). The line for a stale entry and the JSON form are as they were; the exit code stays 0, since a missing answer is an answer of the verification and not an error. Successors that are not registered still print "(none registered)". Other option: print only stale entries, as before. Result of the other option: a verification that has no answer about a mixture reads in the text form as if the mixture had been checked and found current. Other option: exit non-zero when an entry has no answer. Result of the other option: a verification of a repair whose mixtures are not all rebuilt yet would end in an error that scripts read as a fault of the command. What would change it: a ruling that the text form fixes its wording, or that an unanswered entry is an exit code of its own.
- Its check: tests/rebuild/test_verify_text_unanswered.py.
- AMENDED (a successor or a mixture the store does not hold; ruled during M4 by result: no answer is given about an artifact the store does not hold, and the printed verification names each one it could not answer, with its reason): D-85's clause "Successors that are not registered still print '(none registered)'" is the text form of this choice, which D-85 does not record for the JSON. Built: `verify_successors.check_successors` makes, for every artifact the report rewrites whose successor name the store does not resolve, an entry with `registered` false and `instance`, `answer`, `names`, `holds_unresolved`, `matches_sides` and `answered` all null (its `kind` is the report's), and puts no question to the name; the entry of `history` for the artifact is made as for any other. `cli._verification_text` prints for it "successor NAME: (none registered)", one line for every such entry. A mixture the store holds no successor of, or that the report leaves unresolved, is a `caches` entry with `stale` null, `corrected_mixture` null and a `reason`, and a line of its own (D-85); the `stale` and `reason` keys belong to that entry and the `successors` entry has no `reason`. The command goes on with the rest of the document and the exit code is 0, as for every answer of the verification (docs/interfaces.md 7.3, its closing paragraph); the ruling decided what is answered and printed and left the exit code as built. The entry of a rewritten artifact that was lost and the entry of one the repair emptied (D-82 AMENDED: `left_with_no_record` reads no registered successor as the expected state for an artifact whose report entry has no row or document in its successor) are the same nulls; with a sides file the `differences` note ("the report leaves the file no row, so neither side writes it; nothing to compare", or "named on neither side, not compared") is what tells them apart, and without one nothing in the verification does. Other option: refuse the verification (exit 2, no document) when a successor of a rewritten artifact is not registered. Result of the other option (traced: `left_with_no_record`, and the acceptance rows, which expect no registered successor for a shard the repair leaves no document): a repair that has emptied a shard, or whose mixtures are not all rebuilt yet, would get no document at all, so the answers about the other artifacts (history, kept, caches, restarts) would not be given. Other option: put the question to the unregistered name and print its answer. Result of the other option (traced: `coverage.answer`, `_unregistered`): the entry would read "not covered" with the reason "not registered" ("record missing" where another record names the name), the text line would read "not covered" in place of "(none registered)", and the text could no longer tell an unregistered successor from a registered one whose map is missing, since both lines would print "not covered"; only the `registered` key of the JSON would differ. What would change it: a ruling that an unanswered successor stops the verification or has an exit code of its own (the same clause as D-85's), or that the entry carries the word of the name's own query.
- Its checks: tests/rebuild/test_verify_successors.py `test_a_successor_that_is_not_registered_is_said_so_and_the_history_still_answers` (`registered` false and `answer` null for the three rewritten artifacts, the history answering "contains"), tests/rebuild/test_verify_text_unanswered.py `test_a_successor_the_store_does_not_hold_is_still_printed_as_none_registered` (the three entries and the printed line for each), `test_the_json_form_and_the_exit_code_are_what_they_were` (the exit code 0 and the key list, with the mixture entries that have no answer). [written at the M4 check]

## D-86 The four trees and the wall between the tool and its expected answers (plan 1.3 W5, 5.2, 5.3, 5.8 B-7; docs/interfaces.md 1; D-17, D-34, D-65, D-84)
- Built: the repository holds four trees beside the tests and the developer scripts, and docs/interfaces.md section 1 states what each may import. `src/waterwhite/` (the tool) imports the standard library and numpy, with torch optional and only inside `join/_shuffle.py`, and no tokenizer library (D-01, D-33, D-30). `replica/` (the pipeline replica) imports the tool's public packages that section 1 lists module by module: the normaliser in every mode; the capture, harness, store and vocabulary packages in its capture hooks and the capture, store, recovery and tokenmap packages in the modules that register files after a build made without capture; the confine package where a job runs out of process; the vocabulary, the errors and the store module in `probe_run.py` (D-84); the id functions inside two functions of the run worker; and the package itself to find the tool's source folder. It imports nothing of `waterwhite.rebuild`, `waterwhite.coverage` or `waterwhite.answers`: it reads the report and writes the sides file as plain JSON. `oracle/` imports nothing of `waterwhite` or the replica (Megatron and torch only at test time; `oracle/__init__.py` imports nothing). `fixture/plant/` imports the standard library only. The fixture spec and the table of expected answers are written by hand and committed before the tool module they grade. So an expected answer comes from the table, the plant list through its counting script, the oracle's log or the replica's manifests, and never from the tool's own code; for that reason the oracle carries its own definitions of the digests, and a test compares them with the tool's. D-17, D-34, D-65 and D-84 decide single consequences: the replica's normaliser, its run wrapper, its words and one exception to the list. Other option: one package, with the oracle, the plant generator and the replica as subpackages of `waterwhite` that share its helpers (digests, normaliser, line reader). Result of the other option (traced for the oracle): the rows that compare the join's digests and orders with the oracle's would be graded by the tool's own `ids` functions and could not fail; the oracle keeps `digests.array_digest`, `order_digest` and `file_sha256`, and tests/oracle/test_oracle_plain.py compares each with the tool's. Other option: the replica imports nothing of the tool, not even the capture package. Result of the other option (inferred): the capture rows would exercise a writer the replica made for itself instead of the calls a team's wrappers make, and the replica's sanitizer would need its own copy of the normaliser, which D-17 rules out because two copies can drift and a drift shows as lost links. What would change it: for the oracle and the plant generator, nothing (plan W5 is lifted never for a check with a pass or a fail); for the replica, a team's own builder or wrapper taking its place (D-34), or a ruling on which tool packages it may read, which moves the list in section 1 together with the code.
- Its checks: tests/oracle/test_oracle_plain.py::test_oracle_imports_nothing_from_the_tool, tests/plant/test_plant.py::test_scripts_import_the_standard_library_only, tests/replica/test_replica_build_fixture.py::test_with_capture_off_the_command_line_loads_the_tools_normaliser_and_nothing_else, tests/replica/test_replica_probe_run.py::test_the_probe_imports_no_oracle_no_join_and_no_torch, tests/replica/test_replica_repair_words.py::test_the_thirteen_words_of_the_replica_are_the_vocabularys (D-84). No test found walks the tool's imports against the standard library and numpy as a whole, or the replica's imports against the list of section 1 as a whole (tests/tokenmap/test_tokenmap_tool.py::test_the_tool_imports_no_tokenizer_library is the nearest).
[written at the M4 check]
---

## D-87 The store is one public class over a core, a read layer and a check layer, and no read method hands back a database row (plan 3.4; docs/store.md "Reading", "The loud checks")
- Built: `waterwhite.store` exports one name, `Store`. `Store` (`store/store.py`) holds the write methods and inherits `IntegrityMixin` (`store/_integrity.py`, the loud checks), which inherits `ReadMixin` (`store/_reads.py`, the read methods documented one by one in docs/store.md), which inherits `StoreCore` (`store/_core.py`: the connection, the one write transaction, the stored-path rule). Every public read method returns a type of `store/views.py` or `records.py`, or a plain value (such as the dict of the `meta` or `setting` rows, the mark of `state_mark` or a `Path` from `disk_path`); none returns a database row, and no module outside `waterwhite/store/` touches the connection. The rule for which of a shard's maps a reader gets lives once in the read layer (`_reader_order`, D-20 as amended by D-24), so every caller that reads a shard's map reads the same one. The check layer runs its own SQL over the same connection, as part of the package. Other option: public read methods that return rows, or callers that read the tables themselves. Result of the other option (inferred): two callers could pick different maps for one shard, so the proof's walk and another reader could rest on different records of the same shard; and a column that a schema step changes (step 2 added the harness column to a log and narrowed the index over a shard's maps, D-30) would reach the answers of every caller instead of one read method. What would change it: a read that typed results cannot serve at the size of a corpus (plan 6, RK3); the method would then return an iterator, as occurrences, edges, map rows and trace rows already do, and still no row. [written at the M4 check]

## D-88 The store keeps a record's place and fingerprint and never its text (plan 3.1, Decisions 9 and 11; docs/store.md introduction)
- Built: the tables hold locators, 16-byte fingerprints (a NULL fingerprint for a line with no text), digests, counts, names and paths, and no column holds a record's text. The capture wrappers (`capture.py`) read a JSONL file, take its digest, line count and one fingerprint per line, and hand the store those and the links; a recovery that must compare two texts reads the files again (D-29: the direct comparison reads the candidate texts themselves and fingerprints each again against the stored fingerprint). The free text a job chooses reaches two fields: `harness_read.outside_ref`, the name asked for in a refused read, and `harness_log.model` (see the entry on the harness log). Other option: store the normalised text beside the fingerprint. Result of the other option (inferred, from plan Decision 9, which keeps the store with the team, on machines it runs because of what a store reveals about a corpus): the store file and every copy of it (docs/store.md "Opening a store", the bullet on copying a store) would hold the corpus text, so a store could not be handed to a reader who may see the answers and not the records; and a stored text would be a third value beside the file and its digest, which no loud check compares. What would change it: a team that keeps the store under the same access as its corpus and wants answers that show a record's text; the text would then be an option, and the loud checks would have to compare it with the file. A test asserts that a record's text is not in the harness log of one job: tests/integration/test_store_capture.py::test_a_generation_job_through_the_harness_and_its_edge_in_the_store. [written at the M4 check]

## D-89 A reference that may point at nothing is stored by value, and one that must resolve is a key (plan 2.1, 3.4, Decisions 21 and 26; docs/store.md "Named, with no record of its own")
- Built: these columns hold an instance id or a name as text, with no foreign key: `artifact_input.input_instance_id`, `mixture_shard.shard_instance_id`, `run_segment.mixture_instance_id`, `run_segment.parent_checkpoint`, `checkpoint.segment_name`, `drop_record.output_instance_id`, and `edge.job_name` (D-24: the edge keeps the name of its job, so an answer can name it when the log is gone). The first five are the five relations of `Reference` (`REL_*` in `store/views.py`), read by `named_without_record` and `references_to`; a thing that is named and has no row answers "not covered", "record missing", a thing never named "not registered" (`coverage._unregistered`). The references that carry `REFERENCES` in `schema.sql` are keys, enforced because every connection runs with `foreign_keys=ON`. One reference is by id with no foreign key on purpose: `edge_input.occurrence_id`. The write refuses an edge whose parent is not a record of the store (`_check_edges_against_store`; D-15), and a parent row that is gone afterwards is the loud fault "edge parent missing" (plan 3.4, and the comment above `edge_input` in `schema.sql`), which a foreign key would turn into a refusal of whatever removed the row. Pinned by tests/store/test_store.py::test_mixture_reads_back_and_names_a_shard_the_store_was_never_told_of and ::test_checkpoints_and_their_history_through_ancestors, which register a mixture, a checkpoint and a resumed segment that name things the store was never told of and read them back as named without a record. Other option: a foreign key on every reference. Result of the other option (the registrations above succeed today: traced; their refusal: inferred from `_transaction`, which turns a schema refusal into "invalid record"): a mixture naming an unregistered shard, a checkpoint of an unregistered segment and a segment resumed from an unregistered checkpoint would be refused when written; a history with a missing link could not be recorded, and the answer "may contain" naming the missing record (plan 2.1) and the listing of "record missing" (Decision 26) could never be produced. What would change it: a ruling that a store holds only histories whose every link is registered. [written at the M4 check]

## D-90 An id or a composite digest is the hash of canonical JSON led by a domain tag, and the exceptions are listed (plan 3.1, Decisions 11 and 29; docs/interfaces.md 3)
- Built: `ids.canonical` is JSON with sorted keys, no spaces and ASCII-only escapes. The instance id, the ingest id, the parameter digest, the shard digest, the mixture and checkpoint content digests and the header of an array digest are the hash of `canonical([tag, ...])` where the tag is the first item (`ww.instance.v1`, `ww.instance.ingest.v1`, `ww.params.v1`, `ww.shard.v1`, `ww.mixture.v1`, `ww.checkpoint.v1`, `ww.array.v1`), so an id of one kind cannot equal an id of another kind computed from the same fields. Not of that form, by design: the edge digest takes its tag as raw bytes and then each edge record in canonical JSON, sorted, each with an 8-byte length in front (so it does not depend on the order the records are given); an array digest is the tagged header, a zero byte and the array's bytes (D-08); the shuffle digest `order_digest` is SHA-256 of a plain JSON list of integers with no tag (D-16); a content digest and a harness log digest are SHA-256 of the file's bytes (D-02) and a fingerprint is BLAKE2b of the normalised text (plan Decision 11); the digest of a run record is SHA-256 of its canonical JSON with no tag (`Store._segment_from`, asserted by tests/store/test_store.py::test_segment_reads_back_with_launches_digests_and_steps); `tokenmap/_shard.py::token_digest` is a 16-byte hash of one token array's bytes that its docstring says is used to find equal sequences and not kept. Widths and hash functions are D-02 and D-03. Pinned by tests/store/test_ids_and_words.py::test_a_mixture_id_covers_its_shards_in_order_their_digests_and_its_settings and ::test_a_checkpoint_id_covers_its_segment_and_step_and_nothing_else, which build the encoding by hand and compare, the digest of no edge record included (`no_edges`: the raw tag and nothing else); the length-prefixed records of a non-empty edge digest are compared only with `ids.edge_digest` itself (tests/integration/test_store_capture.py), not rebuilt by hand. Other option: a binary or delimiter encoding of the inputs, without sorted keys and without a tag. Result of the other option (inferred; the parameter digest is an input of the instance id): the same step parameters written with their keys in another order would give another parameter digest and so another instance id, so one history registered twice would be two instances, against plan Decision 29; and two kinds of id built from equal fields could be equal. What would change it: a hash input that is not a JSON value, or an id that another tool must compute; any change of the encoding moves every instance id, so a store made before it would no longer equal the ids recomputed from its records. [written at the M4 check]

## D-91 The run record is kept whole in the run segment, with the columns read from it (plan 3.1, 3.5; docs/store.md)
- Built: `Store.register_run_segment` (through `Store._segment_from`) keeps the run record as the wrapper wrote it, as canonical JSON in `run_segment.run_record` (`ids.canonical`), and its digest in `run_segment.run_record_digest`, taken over that canonical text and not over the bytes of the wrapper's file. The columns of the same row (sampler type and class, data sharding, micro and global batch size, sequence length, extra-token flag, random seed, three library versions and the release tag), the launches, the cache and shuffle digests and the steps are read from the record at registration; a record whose run, segment or start kind differs from the registration's is refused ("invalid record"). `Store.segment` hands the record back as a dict, and a segment registered with no record holds null in both fields. The record cannot change afterwards through the tool (D-74: no update path, and a second registration with other contents is refused). Other option: keep only the columns and the child rows. Result of the other option (traced): the settings a replay needs that no column holds (the split and the train iterations or samples; `schema.sql` has no column for either) would be missing from the store, so `rebuild/replay_check.record_replayable` would read every segment as not replayable and `rebuild/verify_caches.corrected_run_record`, which copies the record and replaces its shard list and digests, would raise "the segment has no run record" for every segment; the report and the verification would give the phase start for every run and never a corrected order. Other option: keep the record in its file and read it whenever a report or a verification needs it. Result of the other option (inferred): a report or a verification of a store whose wrapper files were moved or lost would answer less than the same store does now, and the digest the segment holds could not be checked against the store alone. What would change it: a record too large to hold in a row of every segment, or a wrapper that keeps its records in a place of its own and registers a digest only, with the report and the verification then reading the record from that place.
- Its check: tests/store/test_store.py::test_segment_reads_back_with_launches_digests_and_steps (the record is kept whole and its digest is that of the canonical form).
[written at the M4 check]
---

## D-92 A stored path is relative to the store file's folder when the file lies under it, and absolute otherwise (plan 3.4; docs/store.md "Opening a store"; D-30)
- Built: the store resolves its own path at `create` and `open`; `StoreCore._stored_path` resolves an artifact's path (links followed) and stores it as a POSIX path relative to the store's folder when it lies under that folder, else as the absolute resolved path; `_disk_path` reads a stored path back (a relative one is joined to the folder) and `Store.disk_path` is the one reader from outside the store (D-30: "defined once in `waterwhite.vocabulary`, as are the line reader, the path rule (`Store.disk_path`) and the shard reader"). `artifacts_by_path` tries a path as given, as a stored form and as relative to the folder. A file under the folder keeps its stored path when the store and the folder are moved together; a file outside it does not. Pinned by tests/store/test_store.py::test_register_jsonl_reads_back (a file under the folder is stored as `src/A.jsonl`) and tests/store/test_store_maps_and_paths.py::test_the_store_gives_an_artifacts_place_on_disk (a file outside is stored and given back absolute; a moved file reads as absent at its recorded place). Other option: absolute paths only. Result of the other option (traced): after a store and its files were moved together, every artifact would be at another place than the one recorded, `file_state` would read "absent" for each (`_integrity.py`), the digest checks of the files could not be made (a gone file is no fault, D-18, D-26), and every "does not contain" would list every artifact of its closure in `files_not_checked` (`coverage/__init__.py`, `_conclude`; the case of one gone file is pinned by tests/coverage/test_coverage_proof.py::test_a_negative_names_the_files_whose_digest_could_not_be_checked, D-26). What would change it: a team that stores artifacts on a mount that differs by machine; an absolute path would then need a root named in the store. [written at the M4 check]

## D-93 A harness log holds references and no clock time, so equal jobs give equal bytes (plan 3.3 "Generation", F10; docs/capture.md section 3 "The harness", "The log file")
- Built: the log is JSON lines in the canonical form of `ids.canonical`, each entry numbered, and holds the job's name and format, the harness word, the references (instance id and locator) to the handed and the read records, the name of the model called, and the end status with the name of the error that ended a failed job; it holds no time of any kind and no record's text. Two strings a job chooses do reach it: what it asked for in a refused read (`outside_ref`) and the model identifier (the store keeps both: `harness_read.outside_ref`, `harness_log.model`). The in-process harness writes the asked-for text as given, with no bound, and accepts a model identifier only when it is non-empty text with no space at its ends, not "none" and not starting with "[" (`_check_model_id`, both harnesses); the confined harness cuts the asked-for text at 200 characters and appends the SHA-256 of the whole, and refuses a model identifier longer than that. So the same job with the same hand-off and the same reads gives the same bytes and the same `log_digest`, and the in-process and the confined log of the same reads agree entry for entry except the harness word of the first entry. Pinned by tests/capture/test_capture_harness.py::test_the_same_job_gives_the_same_log_bytes, tests/confine/test_confine_job.py::test_the_log_is_the_in_process_harness_log_but_for_the_harness_it_names and ::test_a_text_the_job_chooses_is_bounded_in_the_log. The store compares a log registered again with the one it holds for that job name, digest included, and refuses a difference ("a second run of a job needs its own job name", `_check_edges_against_store`). Other option: a time on each entry. Result of the other option (traced to that comparison): a job run again under the same name, with the same hand-off and the same reads, would write a log with another digest, and its registration would be refused as a log with other contents; and two runs of one job could never give equal logs. What would change it: a need to answer when a job read a record; the time would then be kept beside the log, outside its digest. [written at the M4 check]

## D-94 The coverage walk reads the store only through its documented read methods and runs no SQL (plan 2.2, 3.4; docs/coverage.md introduction and section 10 "What the walk reads")
- Built: `coverage/_walk.py` holds the walk over records, shards and mixtures (`Session`), `coverage/_checkpoint.py` the checkpoint rule (it imports the walk), and `coverage/__init__.py` the public names (`prove`, `answer`, `check_store`). None of them imports `sqlite3` or touches the store's connection; the walk reads the store through the read methods docs/store.md documents, one record or one small group of rows at a time, and holds the mark of the store from `state_mark()` (D-45). The introduction and section 10 of docs/coverage.md state the rule. Other option: a walk written in SQL over the tables. Result of the other option (inferred): the rule for which map a shard's reader gets (D-20 as amended) and the rule for what is named without a record would be written again in the walk, a schema step (D-30) would change what the proof reads with no change to a documented method, and an answer could rest on a map or a link that the documented read methods would not give for that shard. The split of the walk from the checkpoint rule changes no answer or refusal (inferred); the scan of D-53 (tests/coverage/test_proof_only_construction.py, `PRIVATE_MODULES`) names the two private modules and would name the one. What would change it: a measured walk that the read methods cannot serve at the size of a corpus (plan 6, RK3); a method would then be added to the read layer, not SQL to the walk. [written at the M4 check]

## D-95 An extension that is switched on and not built is refused, never answered as if it were off (plan 1.4, 2.2(h), 2.3, Decision 30, Section 7; docs/cli.md "query ban, query flagged"; docs/coverage.md; D-11)
- Built: while a switch is off, a request of that extension is refused with "extension off" (exit 2; the table row c07.ban.off). With the switch on, `requests.ban` and `requests.flagged` always raise `Refused` ("request refused", exit 2, the extension in its context) saying this build holds only the off state; and in `_conclude`, at the one place condition (h) would go, a negative past a generation edge is refused when the generating-model switch is on. An answer that passes no generation edge is given as before. `report ban` and `report flagged` (`cli.py`) call `requests.ban` and `requests.flagged`, which always raise, so the line after the call ("the report of ... is not built") is never reached and the user gets the request's own refusal, "extension off" with the switch off and "request refused" with it on. A switch that holds neither "on" nor "off" is refused by `extensions.is_on` (D-25). Pinned by tests/coverage/test_coverage_requests.py::test_a_switched_on_extension_is_refused_as_not_built_and_never_answered (the ban, in the library) and tests/coverage/test_coverage_proof.py::test_with_the_extension_on_a_negative_past_a_generation_edge_is_judged_by_its_model (the generating-model negative, and that answers passing no generation edge are given; superseded by D-E2-1 for the generating-model clause, the test now pins that with every model "none" the standing negative stands with no exposure note, and answers passing no generation edge are still given). The generating-model clause of this entry (the refusal of a negative past a generation edge while the switch is on) is superseded by D-E2-1; the rest stands. The eight rows of fixture/table/check07.toml with `core = false` (c07.ban.R1, c07.ban.R2, c07.ban.scope, c07.R3.G6, c07.R3.G9, c07.R3.GU, c07.R3.GM, c07.R3.release) expect an answer with the switch on; this build refuses where they expect one (superseded by D-E2-1 for the generating-model clause: the five rows of release R3 are answered with the switch on, and the three ban rows are still refused). The acceptance run skips them as "extension off" (tests/acceptance/acceptance_rows.py, `grade`, run by tests/acceptance/test_check07.py::test_check07), so no row is run against a switched-on tool; plan Section 7 (what the owner decided when the build began) puts each extension into the build as a milestone of its own after the core, and the generating-model rule as a switch that is off by default. Other option: read a switched-on switch as off. Result of the other option (traced to `_conclude` and plan 3.3): a store whose owner switched the generating-model rule on would get "does not contain" past a generation edge with the note "generating-model exposure not assessed", the answer of the off state, for a record whose model the rule would have named as exposed or unregistered; and a ban would be refused as "extension off" while the store says it is on. What would change it: an extension built after the core (plan Section 7); the table rows named above then run and the refusal goes for that extension. [written at the M4 check]

## D-96 Exit codes by family, and every named error is a class of errors.py (plan 2.3, 3.4, 5.8 B-7; docs/interfaces.md 2)
- Built: `errors.py` holds five exit codes: 0 answered, 1 usage and every error of no family, 2 `Refused` (a request the tool will not answer), 3 `IntegrityFault` (a loud fault of the store), 4 `WriteRefused` (a record the store rejects). Every named error is a class of that module, whichever module raises it, with its name a literal class attribute and its code from its family; `cli.main` prints the JSON error on standard error, nothing on standard output, and returns the error's code. The detail after a name is free text (D-55). A refusal with no name of its own carries the name of its family ("request refused", "integrity fault", "write refused"), as the refusal of a report that names an instance the store does not hold does (docs/interfaces.md section 2). The plan fixes that a refusal is a named error and that a loud fault gives a nonzero code and no answer; the split into these families, the numbers and the names beyond the plan's are the builder's. Single cases already decided: D-33 (an invalid record is one name with one code), D-54 as amended (the name of a store written during a query is a literal in `errors.py`, pinned equal to a vocabulary constant) and D-77 (c) (that error has one default detail). Other option: one nonzero code for every error, with the family told by the name in the text. Result of the other option (inferred): a script could not tell a refused request (nothing was answered and nothing is wrong with the store) from a loud fault (the store disagrees with its own files, so no answer may be trusted) or from a refused write without reading the text of standard error. Other option: each package defines the errors it raises. Result of the other option (inferred): the names would be spread over the tree and no one place could show that two errors share a name or that a family's code is the same everywhere. What would change it: a caller that must tell errors apart by a code of their own, or a plan sentence that fixes the numbers; a new loud fault is a change of the plan's list of four (docs/interfaces.md section 2), not of this scheme.
- Its checks: tests/store/test_ids_and_words.py::test_every_error_name_is_fixed_and_unique (eleven names with their codes, and that no name is used twice), tests/store/test_ids_and_words.py::test_no_module_of_the_tool_defines_a_named_error_of_its_own, and tests/rebuild/test_query_store_written.py::test_the_error_is_a_class_of_the_errors_module_in_the_family_request_refused. No test found pins every row of the table in docs/interfaces.md section 2.
[written at the M4 check]
---

## D-97 The command line prints one JSON document built whole, and an error goes to standard error (plan 5.8 B-7, 3.4; docs/cli.md "What every command does")
- Built: every command builds its whole output first and writes it once, as one JSON document on standard output (`--format text` gives a few lines for a person instead); nothing is printed before the command has finished. A refusal or fault prints one JSON object `{"error", "detail", "context"}` on standard error (with `--format text`, one line `error: <name>: <detail>`), exits with the code of the error's family and prints nothing on standard output. A usage error is printed in the format that was asked for: `main` reads `--format text` from the arguments before the parser runs (`_wants_text`). The commands that only read, `report` and `verify` among them, open the store read-only (D-30: "every command that only reads a store opens it read-only, so it refuses a store that needs a step and names that command"). Tests: tests/coverage/test_coverage_listing_cli.py::test_a_loud_fault_prints_nothing_on_standard_output (error on standard error, empty standard output, the text form of the error line), tests/rebuild/test_report_command.py::test_a_loud_fault_stops_the_report_with_exit_3_and_no_output, tests/tokenmap/test_cli_recover.py::test_recover_prints_text_for_a_person.
- Other option: a streamed output, one line or one result at a time as it is found, or a log on standard error beside the result. Result of the other option (traced): the last check of `query`, `report` and `verify` (D-76) refuses with `StoreWrittenDuringQuery` before anything is printed; with a streamed output a store written during the command would be found after part of the answer was already printed, so a refusal could no longer leave standard output empty. (inferred) A reader of standard output would see a partial document before the error and would have to read the standard error and the exit code to know that it must be discarded.
- What would change it: a command whose output is too large to build whole (the listing of a store far beyond the sizes of plan 5.6), or a caller that needs progress lines while a long command runs.
[written at the M4 check]

## D-98 `recover --dry-run` opens the store read-only and prints what the recovery would find (plan 3.3, 5.4; docs/cli.md "recover"; docs/recovery.md 8)
- Built: every `recover` sub-command takes `--dry-run`: the store is opened read-only, the recovery runs with its write flag off, the result is printed with `dry_run` true and the written counts 0, and nothing is written. For `recover file` and `recover shard` the printed `instance` is null. A dry run does not see a refusal that the store would give only at the write (docs/recovery.md section 8: an edge that would replace a stored one), so a dry run that prints a result can still be followed by a real run that is refused with exit 4. Tests: tests/tokenmap/test_cli_recover.py::test_recover_file_registers_a_found_file_and_a_dry_run_registers_nothing and ::test_recover_map_in_position_mode_token_matching_mode_and_both (both compare the table counts of the store before and after; neither asserts the read-only open itself), tests/recovery/test_recovery_decisions.py::test_with_write_false_a_recovery_returns_its_result_and_writes_nothing (the library flag).
- Other option: no preview, a recovery always writes. Result of the other option (traced): a person learns what a recovery links only after the links are in the store, and a stored recovered edge is not replaced (the clause "A record that already has another edge" of D-29 refuses a different one), so a wrong link would stay. Other option: a dry run that writes inside a transaction and rolls it back. Result of that option (inferred): the preview would meet the store's refusals and say what the real run would answer, but the command would open the store for writing and could not run on a store the person may only read.
- What would change it: a store that can supersede or withdraw a recovered link (the preview would then matter less), or a caller who needs the preview to report the store's own refusals.
[written at the M4 check]

## D-99 `show` runs no loud check (plan 2.2, 3.4; docs/cli.md "show"; docs/interfaces.md 7)
- Built, ruled during M4 (an entry): `cli._show` opens the store read-only and prints what it holds (`describe`, `describe_map`, `describe_record`, `describe_trace`) without calling `check_integrity` or `coverage.check_store`. The commands that give an answer run the loud checks first: `query` over the ancestor closure of the artifact it answers about (`coverage.prove`) and over the whole store for a listing, `report` over the whole store (`rebuild.build_report`), `verify` through each answer it asks for (`verify_successors` calls `coverage.answer`, which runs the checks for the artifact unless a pass covers it), and `check` over the whole store as its own command. `show` gives no answer word and decides nothing (docs/cli.md), so a loud fault does not stop it. Reading one record can still meet a parent that is gone and then exits 3 ("edge parent missing"). A person who reads `show` on a faulted store therefore reads rows that no check has vouched for, and the output does not say that the store is faulted; `check` says it. Other option: `show` runs the loud checks first, as `query` does. Result of the other option (traced against docs/cli.md): on a store that `check` faults, `show` would print nothing and exit 3, so the rows that carry the fault (a map whose count differs, a binding whose digest does not match) could not be looked at with the command made to look at them; no answer would be protected by it, since the output of `show` is never an answer. What would change it: a ruling that every read of the store stops on a loud fault, or a caller or table row that takes the output of `show` as evidence of the store's state.
- Its check: none found. No test runs `show` on a store that `check` faults (grep of the tests for "faulted store" and "can be used on a store": no hit; "no loud check" hits tests/coverage/test_coverage_proof.py:729, a comment about a different store state; tests/acceptance/test_faults.py reads mutated copies back through `show --record` and says nothing of the loud checks).
[written at the M4 check]
---

## D-100 A confined job may be given read-only code mounts, named by the caller and by library only (plan 3.3 "Generation", 5.8 B-1, F10; docs/confine.md 6 "Code mounts are allowed", 5 "What it rests on")
- Built: besides the system library folders, the interpreter, the job's script and the client, which D-27 lists, a confined job can be given further read-only mounts: `python_program(script, code=[CodeMount(source, target)])`, or `Program(argv, mounts)` for another program. Every mount is `--ro-bind`; the target must be an absolute normalised path that is not the root, not in or over `/scratch`, `/dev` or `/waterwhite/client`, not given twice, and the source must exist (`InvalidRecord` before anything starts). The route is a library call only, as the shipped list of D-51 is a literal of the tool and not an option of a command: no `waterwhite` command and no replica option names a mount (grep "CodeMount|code=|python_program" over src, replica and tools finds the library, one replica call that passes no `code` and, by the text alone, an unrelated `dtype_code=code` in the store's shard reader), and the replica's confined job (`replica/capture_hooks.py`) is given none. Nothing a mount holds passes the harness or reaches the log, so a mount may hold code and must hold no record; the harness cannot check that, and a mount that holds records breaks the bound of every edge the run gives unseen (docs/confine.md section 5). Tests: tests/confine/test_confine_job.py::test_a_mount_target_that_would_cover_the_sandboxs_own_folders_is_refused and ::test_the_command_line_mounts_code_only_and_shares_no_namespace (nothing is mounted writable, and every read-only source is a library folder, the interpreter, the script, the client or a named mount). No test found for a mount that holds records (grep "def test.*mount" over tests finds only these two names).
- Other option: a job that gets the standard library and its one script and nothing else. Result of the other option (traced, docs/confine.md section 6): it shuts the one route by which a caller can hand a job files the log does not show, and it shuts out every job that imports a library, so such a job could not be confined and its edges could not establish a bound.
- What would change it: a command or a replica option that names a mount (the route would then be open to a person who never reads the library); a log format that records each mount, so that a later reader could see what a job could read unlogged (the log has no place for it today, and a mount that holds records would then be seen); or a ruling that jobs get the standard library only.
[written at the M4 check]

## D-101 A recovery of links writes through three store methods, each one transaction (plan 3.1, 3.3, Decision 7; docs/recovery.md 8; D-05)
- Built: `recovery.recover_copy_step` and `recover_sanitizer` (the degraded run, made when the normaliser is not available, included) write the links of a step nobody observed through three methods of the store and no other: `Store.add_recovered_edges` (all edges of the later file in one call), `Store.add_drops` (all drops in one call) and `Store.add_source_link` (one call per member, the degraded run only). Each call is one transaction and none changes an instance id (D-05). The order is the edges, then the drops; the degraded run writes the drops, then the links. An edge, a drop or a link the store already holds is passed over, another edge for a child that has one is refused whole, an edge of the derivation "stated" is refused (a stated edge is part of an artifact's identity), and a recovered edge may name a parent outside the inputs the artifact was registered with (D-15). With `write=False` none of the three is called and the result says what was found. The registration of a found file (`register_found_file`, through `Store.register_jsonl`) and the writing of maps (`Store.write_maps`, D-30) are outside this rule. Other option: hand the recovered edges and drops to the registering call (`Store.register_jsonl`). Result of the other option (traced for the refusal and the new id, inferred for what attaches to the new instance): a found file is registered with no producing step and takes no drops and no stated edges, and its id comes from its content and member name; handing the same file again with its recovered edges is refused as a registration that differs from the stored instance (D-14: the store's own message sends an edge found after registration to `add_recovered_edges`); registering it under the step that wrote it gives an id computed from the step and its inputs, so a second instance stands beside the first and the step traces, snapshots and answers keyed to the first do not attach to it (the case D-05 rules out). Other option: one transaction around the edges, the drops and the links. Result of the other option (inferred): a recovery stopped between two calls would leave nothing, where now the earlier call stays stored whole and running the recovery again completes it (docs/recovery.md section 8). What would change it: a recovery that has to write links and a new file together, or a ruling that an interrupted recovery leaves nothing.
- Its checks: tests/recovery/test_recovery_decisions.py::test_with_write_false_a_recovery_returns_its_result_and_writes_nothing (a recovery with `write=False` writes nothing) and tests/store/test_store.py::test_recovered_edges_never_change_an_instance_and_never_replace_an_edge (the method's own rule). No test found names the three methods as the only route a recovery writes through (grep of tests/recovery for the three names: no hit).
[written at the M4 check]
---

## D-102 The order of the join's questions (plan 3.5, F6, Check 2 (iii); docs/join.md 3, 10 item 2)
- Built: `compute_join` asks eight questions in one fixed order, and the first "no" names the cause the segment is stored with. (1) A run record that states a release the store's list of verified joins does not hold: "data-order trace unverified for this version", whatever else the record lacks. (2) A cyclic run whose record holds no shuffle digest: "cyclic order not recorded", even where cache files were kept (plan 3.5); torch is not loaded. (3) No run record, or no recorded digest for an index array the segment has: "data-order trace missing", reason "no recorded digest", and no replay is tried. (4) The rest of the gate: a record that states no release, a release verified but not with this sampler type and class, a sampler type the join does not walk, or the cyclic sampler without data sharding: "data-order trace unverified for this version" for the first, "data-order trace unverified for this sampler type" for the others. (5) What the walk needs of the record (D-41 lists the words; the extra-token case is asked here). (6) Each shard's index file against the digest the record holds. (7) The index arrays: kept cache files, then the replay. (8) For a cyclic run, each epoch's shuffle digest and then the computed order. A record that states no release is therefore asked after the digests, a stated release before them, and a cyclic run's missing shuffle before any cache digest. What stands behind each place: the cyclic question before every cache file is plan 3.5's "whatever cache files it kept"; the digest question before the sampler half is the table's Check 2 (iii) rows (`c02.iii.no-replay`, `c02.iii.E@6`: settings, caches and digests withheld give "no recorded digest", and the sampler type is one of the settings); no table row and no plan sentence fixes where a stated unverified release stands against a missing digest, so that place is the join's own. Other option: the whole gate (release, sampler type and class) first. Result of the other option (traced, docs/join.md section 10 item 2): a run whose settings are withheld would name "data-order trace unverified for this sampler type", which is not the cause the table row for Check 2 (iii) expects, and a run with no run record would name the version where the built order names the missing digest. Other option: a stated release asked after the digests. Result of the other option (inferred): a run that states a release the store does not list and records no digest would name "no recorded digest", so its checkpoints would not say that the version is unverified. What would change it: a reading of Check 2 (iii) in which the sampler type stays in the run record. Its checks: tests/join/test_join_causes.py::test_another_release_tag_names_the_version_whatever_else_is_missing, ::test_a_run_record_with_no_release_and_no_digest_names_the_missing_digest, ::test_settings_and_digests_withheld_give_the_same_cause, ::test_cyclic_with_no_shuffle_digest_whatever_cache_files_were_kept; tests/coverage/test_no_launch_segment.py::test_an_unlisted_release_is_asked_before_the_cyclic_order. [written at the M4 check]

## D-103 Kept cache files that do not match fall through to the replay (plan 3.5, 4.6, Check 2; docs/join.md 4, 10 item 3)
- Built: kept cache files are read only against the digests the run record holds (D-33; D-41 decides the case where none is recorded). The choice is per segment. When every index array the segment needs (three per shard, two for the blend) has a kept file with the recorded digest, the route is "cache files". When any one lacks such a file, no kept file is used and all arrays come from the replay, which counts only when each array it makes has the recorded digest; the route is then "replay". Files that are there and differ do not end the join. If the replay cannot be made (a setting it needs is missing) or its digests differ, the segment has route "none" with "data-order trace missing": reason "digest mismatch" when the folder held any index file or when a replay was made and differed, reason "no recorded digest" when no file was kept and no replay is possible. Other option: files that are there and do not match end the join with "digest mismatch". Result of the other option (traced, docs/join.md section 10 item 3): a run whose cache folder was rebuilt later at the same place loses exact answers it could have had from its settings, and its checkpoints answer "may contain" naming "data-order trace missing". What would change it: a ruling that a cache folder with wrong files is itself a finding that must stop the run's registration. Its checks: tests/join/test_join_replay.py::test_cache_files_that_do_not_match_give_way_to_a_replay_that_does (one array changed, route "replay"), tests/join/test_join_causes.py::test_a_changed_cache_file_and_no_matching_replay_is_a_digest_mismatch and ::test_cache_files_that_differ_and_no_setting_to_replay_from. No test has a folder in which some files match and others do not and then reads which arrays the replay used; the route word is what the first test pins. [written at the M4 check]

## D-104 Missing or differing evidence is a cause; a record that is not a record is an error (plan 3.5; docs/join.md 3, 8, 10 item 4)
- Built: when the run record or the files give less than the walk needs, or give something that differs from what was recorded, the join does not fail: the segment is registered with route "none", a named cause and, where one applies, a reason, and its checkpoints answer "may contain" (D-41 adds "index array malformed" as one more instance). A field the record does not give (absent or null) is read as None and never replaced by a default (`_record.py`, `_opt`): a missing micro batch size, global batch size, sequence length, extra-token flag or launch gives "data-order trace missing" with "run record incomplete"; a shard with no prefix gives the same; a shard index file that is gone or unreadable gives "shard index unreadable"; one with no recorded digest gives "no recorded digest"; one whose digest differs gives "digest mismatch". Only a document that is not a record of the known form is an error, "invalid record", which exits with 4 (D-33): a format other than the known one, a launch that lacks one of its five numbers or has a negative counter or a first step after its last, two launches with one position or one step, shard positions that are not 0, 1, 2 and so on, a cache or shuffle digest row of the wrong shape or given twice, a field present with the wrong type, a step record written for another segment or with a malformed step or checkpoint. Nothing of an erroring record is registered. The reading is narrower in three places: `settings` and `versions` read as empty when they are falsy (an empty list, 0 or an empty string reads as absent); a step record's `steps` and `checkpoints` read as empty when falsy; `settings.batch_schedule` is not type-checked. Other option: raise for a missing shard file or a missing setting too. Result of the other option (traced, D-41's account of an erroring record): nothing of the segment is registered, its checkpoints are not in the store, so a query on them answers "not covered" and the reason exists only in the refusal's text; the caller must decide what to register. Other option: fill a default for a missing setting. Result of the other option (inferred): the walk would run on a value no one recorded, and a checkpoint could answer "does not contain" on a data order built from a guess. What would change it: a registration command that should never register a segment without a trace unless told to. Its checks: tests/join/test_join_causes.py::test_a_setting_the_walk_needs_is_never_guessed, ::test_a_shard_index_file_that_is_gone, ::test_a_run_record_with_no_launch, ::test_a_document_that_is_not_a_run_record_is_refused; tests/join/test_join_store.py::test_a_segment_with_no_trace_is_registered_with_its_cause; tests/coverage/test_register_run_command.py::test_a_run_record_that_is_no_run_record_exits_with_4_and_the_name_invalid_record. [written at the M4 check]

## D-105 A cyclic run with one unusable order gets no trace at all (plan 3.5, 2.4; docs/join.md 6, 10 item 5)
- Built: for a cyclic segment the join needs, for every epoch that a placed step reads in and for every rank, a recorded shuffle digest, and the order it computes (D-33) must equal that digest and, where the record gives a count, that count. If a needed digest is not recorded the segment is stored with "cyclic order not recorded"; if any computed order differs, with "cyclic order digest mismatch", naming the first that differs by launch, epoch and rank. In both cases route "none" and no step of the segment gets a trace, the steps of the epochs whose orders are good included. Only epochs read by steps the map places are needed (a step the map cannot place needs none; see the entry on untraced steps), and a digest recorded with no count is accepted. Other option: trace the steps of the epochs whose orders are good and leave the rest untraced. Result of the other option (traced, docs/join.md section 10 item 5): a checkpoint before the bad epoch keeps an exact answer, and a later checkpoint names "step untraced" and not the cyclic cause. Result of the built option (traced): every checkpoint of the segment names the cyclic cause, as plan 2.4 words it for a shuffle whose recorded digest matches nothing. What would change it: a store that can hold a cause for a range of steps. Its checks: tests/join/test_join_causes.py::test_cyclic_with_no_digest_for_an_epoch_a_launch_reads_in (needs no torch) and, with torch (marker `megatron`), ::test_cyclic_with_a_wrong_digest, ::test_the_same_record_joins_when_the_launch_stays_inside_the_recorded_epoch. [written at the M4 check]

## D-106 A step the map cannot place is untraced; the segment keeps its route (plan 2.2(e), 3.5; docs/join.md 5, 10 item 6)
- Built: `_place_steps` gives a step a trace only where the step-to-sample map covers it. A step it cannot place is registered, gets no trace and is listed in `untraced` with the reason; the segment keeps its route and the traces of its other steps. The reasons are: no launch holds the step; nothing gives its batch size (the step record gives none and the run record names a batch schedule, which gives no default); the batch is not a whole number of rounds of micro batch times data-parallel size; for the sequential sampler the step would read past the end of the dataset; for the cyclic sampler the step's batch is not the global batch size, its counter is not on a round boundary inside an epoch, or the dataset holds no whole round. Once a step of a launch cannot be placed, every later step of that launch is untraced too, because the counter after it is unknown. An untraced step is a condition of plan 2.2(e) and is not a cause of the segment: a checkpoint whose history holds it cannot answer "does not contain". Other option: one unplaceable step refuses the whole segment. Result of the other option (traced, docs/join.md section 10 item 6): a sequential launch that ran past its dataset would lose the exact traces it has up to that point, and the checkpoints before that point would answer "may contain" naming a cause instead of an exact answer (inferred for the checkpoint answers). What would change it: a trace given beside an untraced step shown to be wrong (the segment would then be refused whole, the other option), or a run record that states the counter after a step the map cannot place, so the later steps of that launch could be placed again. Its checks: tests/join/test_join_walk.py::test_the_step_record_gives_a_batch_schedule_for_the_sequential_sampler, ::test_a_step_no_launch_holds_is_registered_and_gets_no_trace, ::test_cyclic_step_with_another_batch_size_than_the_global_one_gets_no_trace (marker `megatron`); tests/join/test_join_sampler.py::test_sequential_a_step_that_ends_at_the_dataset_end_is_read_and_one_past_it_is_not. [written at the M4 check]

## D-107 A run read with the extra token off gets no trace (plan 3.5, F9, 5.5 Check 1; docs/join.md 3, 10 item 7)
- Built: the join resolves each sample over sequence_length + 1 tokens (plan 3.5) and is verified for that span only. A run record whose add-extra-token setting is false is stored with route "none", "data-order trace missing" and the reason "extra token off" (D-41 holds the word and its place among the stored reasons); a record that does not give the setting at all is "run record incomplete" and is asked in the same question (the fifth, before any shard file is read). Other option: resolve samples of sequence_length tokens. Result of the other option (traced, docs/join.md section 10 item 7 and the plan's note on F9): traces would be written for a span no check verified, since F9 covers the +1 span alone, and a checkpoint could answer "contains" or "does not contain" from rows the oracle comparison never covered. What would change it: an oracle run with the extra token off. Its checks: tests/join/test_join_causes.py::test_a_run_read_with_the_extra_token_off and ::test_a_setting_the_walk_needs_is_never_guessed (the add_extra_token_to_sequence case). [written at the M4 check]

## D-108 A step's samples are counted from its launch's consumed-sample counter (plan 3.5, F4; docs/join.md 5)
- Built: every launch of the run record carries `consumed_at_start`, a required number (a launch without it is "invalid record"). `_place_steps` starts the first step of a launch at that counter and every later step of the launch at the counter plus the batch sizes of the launch's earlier steps. The step number is a label of the trace and takes no part in the place: a resume that starts at counter 2 and calls its steps 7 and 8 reads samples 2 and 3 in step 7; a warm start has counter 0 whatever the parent checkpoint's counter was; two launches of one segment each start at their own counter. The counter is the one the run record states for the launch (plan 3.1: the state of training when each launch begins); the tool does not read a checkpoint's own counter, and plan F4's fallback (the completed step of every checkpoint, registered by the operator) is not used for it. The table's rows for run K say the same: `c01.K.warm-start` (a first launch read from counter 0 although K starts from a checkpoint saved at 24) and `c01.K.resume` (the second launch continues inside epoch 0 from counter 12). Other option: take a step's place from its step number times the batch size. Result of the other option (traced, from the expected places of the two tests below): after a resume or a warm start every step would be placed on samples the trainer did not read, so a checkpoint could answer "does not contain" for a record the step did read, or "contains" for one it did not. What would change it: plan F4 settled by a real checkpoint's recorded fields, so that the counter could be read from the checkpoint and held against the run record's. Its checks: tests/join/test_join_walk.py::test_a_resume_is_counted_from_its_counter_and_not_from_its_step_number, ::test_a_warm_start_restarts_the_counter, ::test_two_launches_of_one_segment_each_start_at_their_own_counter; tests/join/test_join_causes.py::test_a_document_that_is_not_a_run_record_is_refused (a launch lacking its numbers). [written at the M4 check]

## D-109 The oracle imports nothing of the tool, reaches Megatron through one module, and takes one JSON config in which an unknown key is an error (plan W5, 3.5, 5.3, 5.8 B-7; docs/oracle.md "The oracle and the training stand-in", "Rules every tool keeps")
- (a) Built: no module of `oracle/` imports `waterwhite` or `replica`. Where the run record needs a digest the tool also computes (the array digest, the order digest, the file digest), `oracle/digests.py` computes it again from the written definition. Tests: tests/oracle/test_oracle_plain.py::test_oracle_imports_nothing_from_the_tool, ::test_array_digest_equals_the_tools_definition, ::test_order_digest_equals_the_tools_definition, ::test_file_digest_equals_the_tools_definition. Other option: import `waterwhite.ids` for those digests. Result of the other option (inferred): the value the oracle writes and the value the tool computes would come from one function, so a defect in that function would pass both sides of Check 1 and Check 2 alike. What would change it: a ruling that the digest definitions may be shared, or a change of plan W5.
- (b) Built: `oracle/megatron_data.py` is the one module that imports Megatron, torch and numpy at module level, and it requires the offline environment before the import (`_require_offline` raises `EnvironmentNotReady` when `HF_HOME` is not set or `HF_HUB_OFFLINE` is not 1). `oracle/environment.py` also imports `megatron` and `torch`, each inside one function (the check that the installed rule files equal the checkout, and the versions of a run record). `oracle/pipeline.py`, `oracle/stepping.py` and `oracle/f1_probe.py` reach Megatron only through `oracle.megatron_data`. `build_index`, `consume`, `replay_index`, `run_record` and `run_segments` import `oracle.pipeline` inside a function and so reach Megatron the same way; the modules that import nothing of these run where Megatron is not installed. Test: tests/oracle/test_oracle_plain.py::test_only_named_modules_import_megatron_or_torch checks the 14 modules of its `STDLIB_ONLY` list (environment is allowed `megatron` and `torch`); it does not check the nine other modules of `oracle/` (`build_index`, `consume`, `f1_probe`, `megatron_data`, `pipeline`, `replay_index`, `run_record`, `run_segments`, `stepping`), and no test found pins that `megatron_data` is the only module with a module-level import of Megatron (grep over oracle/*.py shows it). Other option: import Megatron wherever a module needs it. Result of the other option (inferred): the config reader, the spec reader, the grouping of log rows and the spec's conditions would need the Megatron environment to run at all. What would change it: a Megatron entry point that cannot be driven from one module.
- (c) Built: each oracle script takes one argument, the path of a JSON config file; a key that the tool does not know is an error and not a default (`config._only` for the segment config, the same helper in `fixture_spec` for the TOML spec, and explicit checks for the top level of the config, the positions config and the F1 probe config). A failure the oracle names itself prints `{"error", "detail"}` on standard error, exits 2 and prints nothing on standard output. Tests: tests/oracle/test_oracle_plain.py::test_segment_config_refuses (the cases `dataloader_typ` and `extra`), tests/oracle/test_oracle_search.py::test_a_spec_that_disagrees_with_itself_is_refused (the case `sequence_len`); no test found for the top-level, positions or F1 probe config (grep "unknown key" over tests/oracle). Other option: ignore a key it does not know, or merge it as a default. Result of the other option (traced to the config module's own reason and to the test of the defaults): a misspelt key would pass as the default of Megatron's setting (tests/oracle/test_oracle_plain.py::test_segment_config_is_read_with_megatrons_defaults), and the oracle would write a log for a setting that the person did not mean. What would change it: a config form that is generated by a tool and never written by hand.
[written at the M4 check]

## D-110 The report is computed on demand, and the tool keeps none (plan 2.2, Decision 1, 4.1; docs/rebuild.md 2; docs/interfaces.md 7.1)
- Built: `waterwhite report withdraw` (`cli._report_withdraw`) opens the store read-only, takes the store's mark, puts the request, runs the loud checks of the whole store (`coverage.check_store`) and builds the document in one coverage session (`rebuild.build_report`, `report_without_restarts` and the restart parts), builds the text form, compares the mark once more (`cli._unchanged_since`, D-76) and prints. Nothing is written to the store, and the store has no table or column for a report (`store/schema.sql` holds no report). A write to the store while the report is made ends the command with `StoreWrittenDuringQuery` and no output (D-45 AMENDED, D-76). A team that wants to keep a report keeps the printed file, and `verify` is handed that file (the entry on `verify` taking the report as a file). Ruled during M4: kept as built. Its checks: tests/rebuild/test_report_command.py::test_the_report_writes_nothing; tests/rebuild/test_query_store_written.py::test_a_store_written_during_the_walk_ends_the_command_with_the_named_error.
- Other option: the store keeps the report it last made, in a table, and later commands read it back. Result of the other option (traced, docs/rebuild.md 2 and docs/interfaces.md 7.1): any later write to the store (a registered successor, a new run segment, a new map) would leave the kept report differing from the report the store gives now, so every write would have to drop or redo it; and a verification handed the stored report after the successors are registered would be handed a report made for another state of the store than the one the repair was driven by.
- What would change it: a ruling that a repair is driven by a report the store holds, together with a way for the store to say which state of itself that report was made for (inferred).
[written at the M4 check]

## D-111 `verify` is handed the report as a file and never computes it (plan 4.2, 4.4; docs/interfaces.md 7.3)
- Built: `cli._verify` reads `--report` through `verify_sides.read_document(path, v.FORM_REPORT, "report")` and hands the parsed document to `verify_sides.verify`. A file that cannot be read or is not JSON is a usage error (exit 1); JSON that is not a document of the report's form is refused as "request refused" (exit 2). `verify` calls nothing of the report's builder: `rebuild.build_report` has one caller in `src/`, `cli._report_withdraw`. What `verify` takes from the document is the keys of D-72 AMENDED (a) and the request, which it puts to the store again; the document is refused at two points, where the request does not expand to the members and records the report lists and where an instance the report names is not held (D-80, D-74 AMENDED). Nothing the report says is recomputed, so a report that is wrong (a descendant of the source kept, a record that is not affected retired) is verified as it is: the two table rows c09.10k.wrong-report and c09.10k.wrong-report.unrelated-paraphrase-retired expect the sides to differ for such a report. Its checks: tests/rebuild/test_verify_sides.py::test_the_report_and_the_sides_are_read_as_files_of_their_form; tests/rebuild/test_verify_command.py::test_a_report_that_is_not_a_report_is_refused_with_exit_2 and ::test_a_report_that_lacks_a_key_verify_reads_is_refused_with_exit_2. No test asserts that `verify` leaves `build_report` uncalled; "never computes it" is read from the code and from the single caller of `build_report`.
- Other option: `verify` computes the report itself, from the store and the request given on its command line. Result of the other option (traced, docs/interfaces.md 7.3, plan 4.4): a report computed after the successors are registered would be another report than the one the repair was driven by (inferred: a successor holds nothing of the withdrawn source, so its action would read otherwise); and a wrong report could never reach the comparison (inferred): the two sides would be graded against the tool's own account of what leaves, and the verification could not show that a repair followed a wrong report.
- What would change it: a ruling that the store keeps the report a repair is driven by (the other option of the entry on the report computed on demand); `verify` would then read that one and not a file.
[written at the M4 check]

## D-112 An artifact's action has a third word, "unresolved" (plan 4.1, 2.2; docs/rebuild.md 2.2, 2.2a)
- Built: `artifacts._artifact_action` gives an artifact "keep" for the negative answer alone, "rewrite" when the answer is "contains" and at least one of what it holds has a definite action (a JSONL file's rows or a shard's documents, counted as retire or re-source; a promote does not count), and "unresolved" in every other case: the answer "may contain" or "not covered", and "contains" where every record in scope is unresolved. A shard's "keep" is the negative answer alone: `shard_entry` returns it before `receives` is filled, and `receives` lists only the new parents of the shard's own re-sourced documents. The owner decided a change to this, not built at this commit (the entry on where a returning copy lands, D-141). A mixture takes its action from its snapshot (`mixture_entry`): "rewrite" exactly when the snapshot names a rewritten shard (also where another shard of it is unresolved), else "keep" for the negative answer, else "unresolved". The words are read by `counts.totals` (`unresolved_artifacts`) and `counts.mixtures` (`unresolved`), and `verify` accepts the word in a report (`verify_sides._ARTIFACT_ACTIONS`); the check of artifacts the sides lack and the check of kept artifacts take keep and rewrite only. The files upstream of the artifacts follow their own rule (D-78 (c): "'May contain' and 'not covered' give 'unresolved'"; a file whose answer is "contains" is rewritten, left without action or called unresolved by its role): the artifact rule "contains with no definite record is unresolved" is not applied to them, and `upstream.py` never reads a record's action.
- Its checks: tests/rebuild/test_report_artifact_actions.py, `test_an_artifact_that_holds_an_unresolved_record_and_none_in_scope_is_unresolved` and `test_an_artifact_whose_only_records_in_scope_are_unresolved_is_unresolved` (the word for an artifact that holds unresolved records, and for a mixture over such a shard). Other tests of the artifact-level word were not searched for this entry; whether either world reaches the branch "contains with no definite record" is not traced, and docs/rebuild.md 2.2 describes that case as an artifact that "contains" only through file-level evidence, so that every record in scope is unresolved.
- Other option: decide the artifact's action from its answer alone, "contains" being "rewrite". Result of the other option (traced in `counts.totals` and `counts.add_bytes`, which read the word "rewrite"): an artifact whose records in scope are all unresolved would be counted among the rewritten shards and the mixtures that need a successor, would be given a successor name and a `bytes_to_rewrite`, while its records list nothing to take out (the replica builds from the records' actions alone, D-58).
- What would change it: a ruling that "unresolved" is a word of records only (plan 4.1 gives it to records), or a ruling that an artifact whose records in scope are all unresolved is rewritten.
[written at the M4 check]
- AMENDED (the shard's keep, as built; D-141, D-143 to D-147): `shard_entry` still gives a shard "keep" for the negative answer, and it still fills `receives` only from the shard's own re-sourced documents. After it, `placing.apply` changes the entry of a shard whose answer is the negative: it reads "rewrite" when a stated placement names it for a returning copy it does not hold (and its `receives`, `successor` and `documents_in_successor` follow), and it reads the fourth word "clean, place not stated" when a copy is placed nowhere and no shard of its group holds it. "keep" for a shard is therefore the negative answer and nothing that a returning copy can change. A mixture reads the fourth word when its own answer is the negative, no shard of its snapshot is rewritten and a shard of it reads the word (`mixture_entry`); otherwise the rule above stands. Other option: leave the shard's keep to the answer alone, as the body above says. Result of the other option (traced: D-141): a shard that a returning copy lands in reads "keep" while its bytes change. What would change it: a ruling that a returning copy never changes a shard. [written for the keep part]

## D-113 A record whose only evidence is a publisher's pointer is unresolved, with the whole file as the scope to review (plan 3.2, 4.1, L1; docs/interfaces.md 7.1; docs/rebuild.md 2.1)
- Ruled during M4: this is not a departure from the plan. A pointer-only link is a source link, read as the file-level link is (reading 4 of D-22), and a pointer-only edge to a record gets a definite action.
- Built: a hop whose link word is "mapped link" is a weak hop beside "file-level link" and an edge labelled file-level (`records._WEAK_LINKS`), at any place in the chain (`_weak_hop`). `records.judge` gives the record "unresolved" through `_unresolved_by_file`, with `review_scope` naming the file of the hop, "every record of the file" and the count of its records. The walk makes the chain end at that hop for a request that names a whole source (`coverage/_walk._link`: a requested member linked by a pointer, request not at record level, gives "contains" with the hop), and gives "may contain" with the failing link "level too weak" for a request narrowed to records, so the record is unresolved by link and the scope is the record (traced in `_link`). The missing-link sentence of the pointer case, `MISSING_LINK_POINTER`, is a literal of `records.py` filled with the member; its sibling for the file-level link is a vocabulary constant (`MISSING_LINK_FILE_LEVEL`); docs/interfaces.md 7.1 gives the text of the pointer's sentence. The verification side is D-72 AMENDED (c): "a file-level edge, a file-level link and a mapped link at any hop of the chain ... `verify` is never less strict than the report", and "A pointer-only edge (a mapped edge) keeps a definite action and is not a source link", so this entry records the report's side and the sentence only.
- Its check: tests/rebuild/test_verify_one_rule.py, `test_verify_and_the_report_give_one_word_to_the_record`, cases `mapped-link-on-the-record` and `mapped-link-behind-a-copy` (word "unresolved") and `a-mapped-edge-has-a-definite-action` (word "retire"). The test reads the action word and the count of unresolved rows; a grep of tests, fixture and src for the pointer sentence's own text finds only `records.py`, and a grep of tests for `review_scope` finds five files: the acceptance helper that compares it (tests/acceptance/acceptance_report.py), the table code that lists it among the report's keys (tests/acceptance/table.py), a literal in a test of the table reader (tests/acceptance/test_table.py), values planted in stub reports for the import artifact import/T (tests/acceptance/test_check07.py) and one record-level case of a missing origin (tests/rebuild/test_report_record_actions.py); none of them is a pointer, so no test found reads the sentence or the review scope of a pointer.
- Other option: give a record tied to its source by a pointer a definite action, as a record-level link gets. Result of the other option (inferred from docs/interfaces.md 7.1, where the pointer names a member and no record): a record would be retired or re-sourced on evidence that does not show which record of the member is behind it, so a release would lose or change a row that the evidence cannot tie to the withdrawn source.
- Other option: make the pointer's sentence a vocabulary constant like its sibling. Result of the other option (inferred): the report prints the same text; the sentence would become a fixed word of the tool, so a later change of its wording would be a change of a word.
- What would change it: a ruling that a pointer names a record, or a pointer that the store records with a record behind it.
[written at the M4 check]

## D-114 A retired record names the nearest generation job on its chain (plan 4.1; docs/interfaces.md 7.1; docs/rebuild.md 2.1; D-24)
- Built: for a retired record `records._retire` walks the chain from the record itself toward the source, takes the first hop that passes a generation edge and stops there. `regenerate_with` is that hop's job, else the edge's own `job_name` (the edge keeps the name, so it is given when the harness log is gone: D-24), and `step` is that edge's transform: `name`, `version` and `parameter_digest`. Where the chain holds no generation hop (a retired copy of the source's own record, D-19) both are null. For a translation made from a paraphrase made from a record of the source, the entry names the translation job, not the paraphrase job. Tests: tests/rebuild/test_report_record_actions.py::test_a_retired_row_names_the_job_that_made_it_and_the_step_to_run_again (R1:L3 names "translation"; the chain of R1:L3 runs through gen/T:L1 and gen/P:L1 to src/A:L1, in ::test_the_chain_of_a_translation_runs_through_the_paraphrase_to_the_source). Every record entry has the two keys; only a retire entry gives them a value.
- Other option: the generation edge farthest from the record, or every generation edge on the chain. Result of the other option (inferred): the farthest edge would name the paraphrase job for a retired translation, a job whose output is another record of the chain and not the retired one, so the step to run again would not be the step that wrote this record; every edge would change `regenerate_with` and `step` from one value to a list, a change of the form of docs/interfaces.md 7.1.
- What would change it: a chain whose record can be regenerated only by running every job on it (the list form), or a plan sentence that names which edge the report gives.
[written at the M4 check]

## D-115 A layout is NAME=PREFIX of store names, and a shard that two layouts match is a usage error (plan 4.1, 4.5, BF-7; docs/interfaces.md 7.1; docs/rebuild.md 2.3)
- Ruled during M4: the refusal of a shard that two layouts match is an entry.
- Built: no layout is held in the store; the caller names each one as `--layout NAME=PREFIX` (`cli._layouts`: both parts needed, a name given once, else a usage error; `counts.check_layouts` asks both parts again). A shard belongs to a layout when any name the store holds for it begins with the prefix (`counts._matching`). `counts.by_layout` refuses with a usage error (exit 1) when one shard matches more than one layout, naming the shard and the layouts; it runs inside the report, so no report is made. A prefix that matches no shard gives an empty group and is not an error. Shards are counted per run in every case, and whether the per-layout count was made, and which shards no layout matched, is D-79: "a new sibling key `groups.layouts` holds `counted`, `reason` and `unmatched_shards`". The groups count the shards the store holds as artifacts and their action as the report gives it ("keep" is the negative answer alone, as the entry on an artifact's action word records); a shard that a build writes and the store does not hold is in no group. The owner decided a change to this, not built at this commit (the entry on where a returning copy lands, D-141).
- Its checks: tests/rebuild/test_report_artifact_actions.py, `test_a_shard_that_two_layouts_match_is_a_usage_error` and `test_the_shards_are_grouped_by_the_layouts_the_caller_names`; the statement is checked by tests/rebuild/test_report_layout_statement.py (D-79).
- Other option: let the first layout named take a shard that two match. Result of the other option (inferred from `by_layout`, which would take the first of the matches): the groups would depend on the order of the `--layout` flags, and the same command with the flags swapped would give other counts.
- Other option: count a shard under every layout it matches. Result of the other option (inferred): the group counts would add up to more than the number of shards.
- What would change it: a store that records the layout of a shard.
[written at the M4 check]
