## D-116 The bytes to rewrite are read from the disk when the report is made (plan 4.1; docs/rebuild.md 2.3; docs/interfaces.md 7.1)
- Built: `counts.size_of` reads the size of a rewritten artifact's file from the disk at the moment of the report: for a JSONL file the size of the file, for a shard the sizes of its `.bin` and `.idx` added. It gives null where the store's file state for the artifact is not "present", where the store holds no path, or where reading the size fails. `counts.add_bytes` fills `bytes_to_rewrite` only for an entry whose action is "rewrite" and whose kind is jsonl or shard (a mixture has no file, so its value is null and it is not named as unknown). `counts.totals` adds the known sizes into `bytes_to_rewrite` and names every rewritten artifact with a null size in `bytes_unknown`, leaving it out of the sum. The store holds no size (docs/rebuild.md 2.3), so a size is read now or not at all.
- Its checks: tests/rebuild/test_report_artifact_actions.py, `test_the_bytes_to_rewrite_are_read_from_the_files_of_the_artifacts_that_are_rewritten` and `test_the_bytes_of_a_shard_whose_file_is_gone_are_unknown_and_left_out_of_the_sum`; `bytes_unknown` also appears as an empty list in `test_the_counts_of_the_report` of the same file and in tests/rebuild/test_report_upstream_files.py (line 213).
- Other option: store a size when the file is registered and report that. Result of the other option (inferred): the report would give a number for a file that has since changed or moved, would not name it as unknown, and the sum would be what was registered and not what is on the disk now.
- What would change it: a store that records the size of a file at registration, and a ruling that the report states the registered size.
[written at the M4 check]

## D-117 The report, the verification and the sides file are JSON documents with a versioned form word (plan 5.8 B-7; docs/interfaces.md 2, 7.1 to 7.3)
- Built: each document has a key `form` with a word that carries a version, `FORM_REPORT` "waterwhite.report/1", `FORM_VERIFICATION` "waterwhite.verification/1" and `FORM_SIDES` "waterwhite.sides/1" (`vocabulary.py`). `verify_sides.read_document` reads a file given to `verify`: a file that cannot be read, or is not JSON, is a usage error (exit 1); JSON that is not an object, or whose `form` is not exactly the word asked for, is "request refused" (exit 2), so a document of a later version would be refused, as would a sides file handed as the report. `report_artifacts` asks the report's form again for a report handed as a parsed document, which is the form key of D-72 AMENDED (a) ("the form word" among the keys `verify` requires). The replica reads the report's word and writes the sides file's word through its own constants (D-58, D-65), and refuses a report of another form as an input error (exit 1). At this commit no code in `src` or `replica` reads the verification document: a grep of both for `FORM_VERIFICATION` and for the literal "waterwhite.verification" finds the definition and the writer only.
- Its checks: tests/rebuild/test_verify_sides.py, `test_the_report_and_the_sides_are_read_as_files_of_their_form`; tests/rebuild/test_verify_command.py, `test_a_report_that_is_not_a_report_is_refused_with_exit_2` and `test_a_report_or_sides_file_that_cannot_be_read_is_a_usage_error`; tests/replica/test_replica_repair.py, `test_a_report_of_another_form_is_refused`.
- Other option: read a document by its keys and ignore the `form` word. Result of the other option (inferred): a sides file handed as the report, or a report of another shape, would be read by whatever keys it holds, and the refusal would come only from a missing key, or not at all.
- Other option: accept a document of an earlier version as well. Result of the other option (inferred): a change of a form would be read as the old form by a tool that does not know what changed.
- What would change it: a second version of a form; a ruling is then needed on what the tool reads of the old one.
[written at the M4 check]

## D-118 The order of the repair driver and what a failure leaves (docs/interfaces.md 9.5; docs/replica.md 15; D-66, D-81)
- Built: `replica.repair.repair` works in one order. First it reads the report, plans the repaired build from the historical folder and reads the report's contradictions (`plan_repair`). Then every refusal that needs no write: the store file or the sides file inside a build folder, a sides file that exists already, a reference folder that is not new, no Megatron environment where tokenization is on, the contradictions of the changes themselves (`RecordChanges`), and `check_build` of the repaired side and of the reference side (it writes nothing and creates no folder: it reads the plan and the options and calls `require_new_folder`, which only looks). Then the last refusal before the store is opened: a `--store` path that is not a file, or a file that is not a store (D-81). Only then it opens the store (`StoreCapture.open`), builds the repaired side with capture on (an exception raised by a waterwhite module in that call is turned into one line, D-66), builds the reference side with capture off after the repaired side, and writes the sides file last. So a refusal before the store is opened leaves no store, no build folder and no sides file, and a failure after it leaves what had been written: the repaired folder part-written with the names registered so far in the store; and a failure of the reference build leaves the repaired side built, its successors registered in the store, the reference folder part-written and no sides file. An error of the replica in the reference build prints one line through the command's handler, while an exception from a waterwhite module there is not turned into one line (D-66 as amended puts the reference build outside the conversion). D-81 decides where the store-path refusal stands in this order and D-66 where the conversion stops; the order as a whole and what a failed reference build leaves are decided here. Other option: no up-front refusals, so that each refusal comes from the build that meets it. Result of the other option (traced for the store path, inferred for the rest): a mistyped store path ends in a repair registered into an empty store (D-81), and a sides file that exists, a reference folder that is not new or a missing environment would be found only after the repaired side was built and registered. Other option: build the reference side first (it uses no store), then the repaired side. Result of the other option (inferred): a failure of the reference build would leave the store untouched and no successor registered; a failure of the repaired build would leave a finished reference folder beside the part-registered store, as it leaves the repaired folder now. What would change it: a repair that must leave nothing behind when it fails (the folders removed and the registration undone, the ruling D-66 names as its own change), or a reference side that needs something the repaired build produces.
- Its check: tests/replica/test_replica_repair_store.py::test_a_store_path_that_holds_no_store_is_refused_and_nothing_is_created (a refusal before the store leaves no store, no repaired folder and no sides file; that test passes no reference folder). No test found for the order of the two builds or for what a failed reference build leaves.
[written at the M4 check]
- AMENDED (no code change; both results of a failed reference build, left to the owner at the end of the round; D-149 for the refusal that moved): the order above stands, with one move: the refusal of a shard that lies in no layout is now among the refusals that need no write (D-149). Whether a failed reference build counts as a refused repair is not decided here. Result if it counts as one (inferred): a repair whose reference build fails leaves nothing registered, so the store is as it was and the repair can be run again. Result if it does not count (as built; traced in the text above): the repaired successors stay registered without a reference, no sides file is written, and `verify` given the store and no sides file reads the two comparisons as "not compared", that is, the reference side as missing, while the successors, the history and the caches are still answered. What would change it: the owner's ruling for one of the two results. [written for the keep part]
---

## D-119 A report whose re-source entries contradict is refused (docs/replica.md 8, 15; docs/interfaces.md 9.5; D-59, D-60)
- Built, ruled during M4 (the stricter option, recorded as an entry): the replica refuses, before anything is built or any store is opened, a report in which (a) a re-source entry names one record as both the withdrawn parent and the new parent (`plan_repair`, `InputError`), (b) one withdrawn record is re-sourced to two different records in two entries (`plan_repair`, `InputError` naming both targets), (c) a record is both swapped (as the old or as the new record) and retired, or (d) a record is swapped in and also swapped out, which is a chain of two re-sources (both from `RecordChanges.__post_init__`, `SpecError`, raised when `repair` builds the changes). Each is a one-line error of the replica, exit 1, with the record named. The same re-source written in two entries with the same pair is not a contradiction. D-60 decides retire against keep of one id and says that unresolved next to retire is no contradiction; these four are the other contradictions the report can hold. Other option: let one statement win, the first entry for a re-source to two records, the retire over the swap, a chain applied in order, or a re-source onto itself read as nothing. Result of the other option (inferred, as D-60 gives it for retire and keep): the repaired side would be built from one of two contradicting statements and the other would be dropped without a word, so a report with one entry edited by hand would drive the build as a report different from the one handed in (the build reads the actions as edited, D-58), and a build could not be told from a wrong report by what it refuses. What would change it: the report builder never listing a record in two places in these ways (D-60's own condition), or a build that has a meaning for a chain of re-sources.
- Its check: tests/replica/test_replica_build_fixture.py::test_a_swap_onto_a_record_that_is_already_selected_is_refused pins case (c) for a swap and a leave-out given to `RecordChanges` (its last assertion, `SpecError` with no message pinned). No test found for (a), (b) or (d): a grep of the tests for the four messages finds no hit, except "re-sourced to" at tests/rebuild/test_report_record_actions.py:7, a docstring about the report's own actions that is not a refusal of the replica.
[written at the M4 check]
---

## D-120 What the replica's sides file names, and the refusal of a shard that lies in no layout (plan 4.4, 5.8 B-7; docs/interfaces.md 7.2; docs/replica.md 15)
- Ruled during M4: an entry; the refusal of a shard in no layout, the stricter option, is recorded with it.
- Built: (a) `replica.repair._side` visits only the entries of the report's `artifacts` whose action is keep or rewrite, and names, for each side, the artifacts whose file the build holds: a JSONL file that is a file on the disk, or a shard whose `.bin` and `.idx` both are. An artifact the build did not write (a missing file, or a missing pair of shard files), every artifact whose action is "unresolved", and every kind with no file (a mixture) are left out without a refusal and without a null. A shard that a build wrote and the report does not list is never visited, so the sides file does not name it. This leave-out rule is what lets `verify` read "equal" without comparing a shard the report does not list. The owner decided a change to this, not built at this commit (the entry on where a returning copy lands, D-141). D-67 decides what the sides file holds; D-82 states the leave-out as a fact in its check bullet, and its AMENDED line rules how `verify` reads a name that is on neither side.
  (b) An entry that names no `kind` is looked for first as a file (`Historical.file_of`, which refuses a name that does not begin with the historical name prefix) and then as a shard. The replica's `read_report` does not require `kind` of an artifact entry, while `verify` does (`verify_sides.report_artifacts`).
  (c) An entry of kind "shard" whose store name begins with no layout's prefix is refused: an input error (exit 1), "shard ... lies in no layout of the build", naming the layout prefixes. An entry with no kind in that position is left out. `write_sides` runs last in `repair`, after both builds and the registration of the successors, and it raises before the sides file is opened, so no sides file is written and no summary is printed.
- Its checks: tests/replica/test_replica_repair.py, `test_the_sides_file_names_the_repaired_file_of_every_artifact_of_the_report` (the repaired side names R1 and R2 and the reference side is empty when none was built), and tests/replica/test_replica_repair_empty_shard.py, `test_the_sides_file_names_the_shard_on_neither_side_and_the_others_on_both` (a shard no record reaches is left out of both sides). A grep of tests, replica and docs for "in no layout" finds only the raise in `replica/repair.py`: the refusal and the kind-less lookup have no test found.
- Other option for (a): name every artifact of the report, with a null where the build holds no file, or refuse. Result of the other option (traced in D-82 AMENDED for the refusal, inferred for the null): with a null, the sides file would name artifacts with no file and `verify` would have to read a null path as "this side wrote none"; with a refusal, a repair whose withdrawal leaves a shard with no record could write no sides file, which is the case the 10k partitioned set meets (D-82 AMENDED: "the repaired shard of that source holds no row and is not written").
- Other option for (b): refuse an entry with no `kind`, as `verify` does. Result of the other option (traced: `report_artifacts` requires `kind`, `read_report` does not): the replica would stop on a report that the verification would refuse as well, and would not build from a report that the verification then cannot read; as built, the replica builds from it and looks the artifact up by file and then by shard.
- Other option for (c): leave such a shard out, as the file case does. Result of the other option (inferred): a layout option that does not match the store's shards (the wrong layout for the store) would give a sides file that names no shard, and the verification would read "not compared" and not fail at the input.
- What would change it: for (a), the change the owner decided (the entry on where a returning copy lands, D-141), which would let the sides file name every shard a build wrote; for (b), a report form in which `kind` is required of the replica too; for (c), a store whose shards lie in layouts the build does not know and are to be left out.
[written at the M4 check]
- AMENDED (the change condition of (a), as built; D-141, D-148, D-149, D-150): the decided change reads the stores and the disk, not the sides file. The sides file still names, for each side, only the artifacts of the report that the build wrote, and `_side` still does not visit a shard the report does not list; it now also names a shard whose action is "clean, place not stated" (D-150). What the sides file leaves out is no longer what lets `verify` read "equal": `verify` reads which shards each build wrote from the repaired store (the shards registered as `successor of <name>` under the layout's prefix) and from the disk of the reference build, and a shard both builds wrote that the report does not name keeps the two comparisons from reading "equal" (D-148). The condition for (a) therefore reads: a ruling that the sides file is to name every shard a build wrote, which would make the reading of the stores and the disk redundant. The refusal of (c) is no longer made after both builds: it is made before either build, reading the report and the layouts only (D-149); `_side` still holds the same refusal for a caller that writes a sides file from builds it made itself. [written for the keep part]
- AMENDED (the sentence on what the sides file names; D-150, the ruling that a landing shard is compared): the sides file now names, for each side, the artifacts of the report that are kept, rewritten or read the fourth word (D-150), and after them each shard the report names under `groups.placements[NAME].landing` that the build holds (`<prefix>.bin` and `.idx` both present), found by its layout prefix as a shard of the report is. A landing shard the build did not write, or one that lies in no layout, is left out of that side, as a shard no record reaches is. So the sentence that `_side` names "every keep or rewrite shard" and a shard the report does not list is never visited now reads: it names the keep, rewrite and fourth-word shards of the report and the landing shards the build holds, and a shard the report names nowhere is still never visited. The order of the file is the report's artifacts first and the landing shards after them. [written for the keep part]
- AMENDED (D-144 as amended; docs/replica.md, the repair section): a landing shard that lies in no layout is no longer left out of the sides file. `refuse_shards_in_no_layout` reads the landing shards too, so when `--sides` is given such a landing is refused before either side is built, and `_side` refuses one in the same way instead of skipping it; a landing shard the build did not write is still left out of that side. So the words of the amendment above, "or one that lies in no layout, is left out of that side", no longer hold. Other option: keep the skip. Result of the other option (traced: the amendment above and D-144 as amended): a report whose landing lies in no layout repairs, and its sides file names nothing for that landing, so the place the report names for a copy is never looked up. What would change it: the D-144 amendment withdrawn. Its checks: tests/replica/test_replica_repair_landing_refused.py. [written for the keep part]

## D-121 A file the sides file names that is not there refuses the verification (plan 4.4, 5.8 B-7; docs/interfaces.md 2, 7.3; docs/cli.md "verify")
- Ruled during M4: an entry. The refusal the code builds is recorded, with its test.
- Built: `verify_sides.require_file` asks that a JSONL file exist and that both files of a shard exist, and `file_digest_of` asks it before it reads. It is asked wherever `verify` reads a file the sides file names: for a name on both sides, before the file is compared (`verify`); for a kept artifact the repaired side names (`_kept_entry`); and for a successor's `matches_sides` (`verify_successors._matches_sides`). A missing file raises "request refused" (exit 2) and no verification document is printed, so nothing already compared in the loop is shown. A name that only one side holds is listed in `differences` and its file is not asked for there. The refusal is the stricter of the two ways to treat the fault; D-82 (amended) concerns artifacts the sides file does not name, not a named file that is missing.
- Its check: tests/rebuild/test_verify_sides.py, `test_a_file_the_sides_file_names_that_is_not_there_is_refused` (a name on both sides, called through `verify`); the two other reads have no test of their own.
- Other option: list the name in `differences` with a note that the file is missing, and compare the rest. Result of the other option (inferred; read against D-82, where a file that lacks keeps the comparison from reading "equal"): the document would be printed with exit 0, and a typo in the sides file or a moved folder would read as an answer about the repair ("not compared" or "differ") and not as a fault of what was handed in.
- What would change it: a ruling that the verification answers over what it can read and states what it could not.
[written at the M4 check]

## D-122 `verify` exits 0 whatever its comparisons say (plan 5.8 B-7; docs/interfaces.md 2, 7.3; docs/cli.md "verify")
- Built: `cli.main` returns 0 for every verification that is made. "differ", "not compared" and "not claimed" in `multisets` and `per_shard_digests`, a non-empty `differences`, a stale cache and an entry with no answer all exit 0; only a refusal (exit 2), a usage error (exit 1), a loud fault (exit 3) or a store written during the query (exit 2) gives another code, and none of them prints a document. The code follows the table of docs/interfaces.md 2: 0 means answered, and a difference between the sides is an answer. D-85 states the same for one case: "the exit code stays 0, since a missing answer is an answer of the verification and not an error". A caller that reads only the exit code sees 0 for "equal" and "differ" alike and reads `multisets` for the result.
- Its checks: tests/rebuild/test_verify_command.py, `test_a_difference_between_the_sides_is_an_answer_and_exits_with_0` ("differ"); a run with no sides file exits 0 in `test_a_report_or_sides_file_that_cannot_be_read_is_a_usage_error` (its first call). A grep of tests for "not claimed" finds two assertions on the word in tests/rebuild/test_verify_sides.py, which call `verify` and read no exit code; no test found reads the exit code of "not claimed".
- Other option: a nonzero exit when the sides differ or equality is not claimed. Result of the other option (traced in the exit table of docs/interfaces.md 2, where code 2 means no answer and nothing printed): a difference would read as a failure of the command with the same code as a refusal, or the table would gain a code of its own, and a script would have to read stdout to tell a verification that found a difference from a command that made none.
- What would change it: a ruling that a difference, or "not claimed", has an exit code of its own.
[written at the M4 check]

## D-123 `verify` without a sides file still makes every check that needs none (plan 4.2, 4.4; docs/interfaces.md 7.3; docs/cli.md "verify")
- Built: with no `--sides`, `verify` reads both sides as empty. `differences`, `releases` and `shards` are empty, `multisets` reads "not claimed" where the report counts unresolved records and "not compared" otherwise, `per_shard_digests` reads "not compared", and the entries of `kept` have `identical` and `found` null and a successor's `matches_sides` is null. The successors, the history, the caches and the restarts are made as with a sides file, and the refusals of D-80 (a store that lacks an instance of the report, a request the store does not expand as listed) still apply. D-82 states the empty `differences` as a fact ("Without a sides file `differences` stays empty"); this entry records that the other checks are made.
- Its check: tests/rebuild/test_verify_sides.py, `test_without_a_sides_file_nothing_is_compared_and_the_rest_is_made`.
- Other option: refuse a verification with no sides file, since the comparison of the sides is its first purpose. Result of the other option (traced in docs/cli.md "verify", which names the successors, the history, the caches and the restarts as made without it): a person who wants the answers of the successors, the stale caches and the restarts alone would have to hand a sides file that holds no file.
- Other option: read "equal" when nothing is compared. Result of the other option (traced in D-82, whose rule is that "equal" is never read over a set that lacks an artifact of the report): a verification over no file would claim equality.
- What would change it: a ruling that a verification needs the sides to be a verification.
[written at the M4 check]

## D-124 A re-source takes the first remaining parent the edge lists (plan 4.1, Decision 15; docs/interfaces.md 7.1)
- Built: for a release row made by an "any" edge whose selected parent is in scope, `records._equivalent_parent` asks the edge's other inputs in the order the edge lists them (`edge.inputs`, the selected one left out; the code does not read whether an input was stated as used). The first whose verdict is clear (`coverage.is_clear`) is `new_parent` and the row is re-sourced; plan 4.1 and Decision 15 name an equivalent copy that remains and do not rank several. If none is clear and some other parent has a failing link, the row is unresolved with the failing link of the first such parent in the edge's order (docs/interfaces.md 7.1: nothing shows that an equivalent parent remains and nothing shows that none does); if every other parent is in scope, the row is retired. The choice also decides whether a promote entry follows: `records.promoted` gives one only where `new_parent` is a record the step dropped as a dedup loser from this release. Tests: tests/rebuild/test_report_record_actions.py::test_the_re_source_names_both_parents_and_the_chain_that_made_it_necessary and the table row c07.report.R1.X have one remaining parent (B's copy); grepping tests/ and fixture/table for `new_parent` finds rows that name A's or B's copy of X, and none names a third copy, so none found pins which of two remaining parents is taken.
- Other option: the last parent, the one with the lowest id, or refuse to choose (unresolved) when more than one remains. Result of the other option: the last or the lowest id (inferred) could name another copy, and a promote entry would exist only if that copy is the dedup loser (traced: `new in losers`); refusing to choose (traced) would list a row that two other sources supply as unresolved, and the successor holding it would answer "may contain" for the source although a remaining copy is known.
- What would change it: a plan sentence or table row that ranks equivalent parents, or an "any" edge whose listed order carries no meaning.
[written at the M4 check]

## D-125 An edge labelled "candidates" is never re-sourced, and a document whose map row names a set of candidates is judged without a parent record (plan 3.2, 4.1, L3; docs/interfaces.md 7.1)
- Built: `records._equivalent_parent` gives no re-source for an edge labelled "candidates" or for an "any" edge with no selected parent, so such a record, once the failing-link and weak-hop rules have not made it unresolved, is retired when its verdict is "contains". `records.judge_document` judges a document whose row is labelled "candidates" by the document's own verdict, through `judge(..., None)`, even where the row also names a parent; a row that names a single parent is judged by that parent's record. The verdict of a candidate set comes from the walk (`_twins`): "contains" when every candidate is in scope, which the report retires (the document is named in `removed` as `D<n>`), unless a hop of the chain is a weak link, which makes it unresolved; "may contain" when some candidates are in scope and some are not, which makes it unresolved; clear when none is in scope and nothing may contain, which keeps it. The documents of a candidate set add nothing to a shard's `receives`, since only a re-source gives a new parent. The owner decided a change to this, not built at this commit (the entry on where a returning copy lands, D-141). D-72 AMENDED (c) names `judge_document` for the one-rule choice and does not record the candidate-set choice.
- Its check: none found at the report level. A grep of tests/rebuild for "candidates" finds the docstring of `rb_kit.py` and tests/rebuild/test_verify_successors.py, `test_a_successor_rebuilt_without_capture_with_an_identical_candidate_may_contain_the_source`, which reads a verification; no test builds a report over a "candidates" row.
- Other option: re-source a record or document of a candidate set to a candidate that remains. Result of the other option (inferred): where the walk says only some candidates are in scope, a record would be given a parent that the evidence cannot show it came from, and the history of the successor would state a derivation nothing recorded; as built it stays unresolved.
- Other option: leave a candidate set whose every candidate is in scope unresolved. Result of the other option (inferred for a row of a JSONL file, whose unresolved rows the report counts in `counts.records.unresolved`, which `verify` reads; a shard's documents are not counted there): a record that every one of its possible parents reaches would stay in the successor, and `verify` would not claim equality for the repair.
- What would change it: a ruling that a candidate set is re-sourced to a candidate that remains, or that a set with every candidate in scope waits for a team's decision.
[written at the M4 check]

## D-126 A session's table of finished verdicts is writable by code in the process, on purpose (plan 2.2, W6, Decision 1; docs/coverage.md 2 "How "does not contain" is closed off")
- Built: a `Session` keeps its table of finished verdicts (`_outcomes`) and its other private fields (`_memo`, `_names`, `_facts`, `_shard_gates`) as plain attributes, and `Session.outcome` returns a stored verdict as it is. `prove` takes the verdict of `walk.outcome(found)` and `_conclude` builds from it: for the verdict CLEAR it builds the scope from the verdict's fields and calls the mint, which records the proof with the function that says whether the store was written and does not derive the verdict again. So code in the process that sets a CLEAR verdict in the table of a session and then hands that session to `prove` gets a proof (traced from `outcome` to the call of `mint`; `prove` first runs the mark check and the loud checks, which a planted verdict passes). docs/coverage.md section 2 states the limit and accepts it: "No query reaches" the fields, and a program that reaches into the interpreter's internals on purpose can defeat the closing-off in any case. Tests: none found for the limit (grep "_outcomes" over tests: no hit; the only test that names the memo is tests/coverage/test_coverage_proof.py::test_the_memo_is_a_cache_and_its_limit_changes_no_answer); the closing-off that does hold is tested in tests/coverage/test_proof_only_construction.py (D-42, D-45, D-47, D-53).
- Other option: keep the verdict table where only the mint's side reaches it, as the register of the mint and the register of the loud-check passes (D-47) are closed over. Result of the other option (inferred): a plain assignment to a session's attribute could no longer plant a verdict; code that inspects the closure or the interpreter's internals still could, which is the limit the documentation already states for the other doors.
- What would change it: a query path that reaches the fields of a session (a session handed in by a caller, loaded from a file or built from request text), or a ruling that in-process code lies outside the trust a proof gives.
[written at the M4 check]

## D-127 `matches_sides` ties the answered successor to the file that was compared (plan 4.4; docs/interfaces.md 7.2, 7.3; D-71)
- Built: for each artifact the report rewrites and whose successor is registered, the entry of `successors` carries `matches_sides`. It is true or false: the content digest of the file the repaired side of the sides file names under the HISTORICAL name of the artifact (not under the successor's name), read as a JSONL file or as a shard pair by the kind the store holds for the successor, against the content digest the registered successor has. It is null without a sides file, where the repaired side names no file for the artifact, and for a mixture or a checkpoint (no file). The field changes no answer, no count and no exit code: `answer` and `answered` are always about the registered successor, and a false is a statement only (a verification that finds a difference exits with 0). A file the sides file names that is not there refuses the document ("request refused", `require_file`) and is never a null. Other option: no such field, the answer about the successor standing alone. Result of the other option (inferred): a sides file that names a file other than the registered successor's would verify, its multisets equal, while the "does not contain" printed beside it is about another instance; nothing in the document would tie the answered successor to the file the comparison used. D-71 names the field in passing and decides nothing about it. Pinned by tests/rebuild/test_verify_successors.py::test_the_answer_is_about_the_file_the_repaired_side_names_only_when_the_digests_agree and ::test_a_file_that_differs_in_one_line_does_not_match_the_registered_successor. What would change it: a sides file that names the registered successor itself (its instance or store path), which leaves nothing to tie; or a plan sentence that says what binds the answered successor to the compared file. [written at the M4 check]

## D-128 The corrected order of a stale segment reads the shards' index files at verification time (plan 4.4, 3.5; docs/rebuild.md 5; docs/interfaces.md 7.3; D-71)
- Built: for a segment whose registered mixture digest differs from the digest of its successor mixture, `verify_caches.shard_lengths` takes each shard of the successor mixture's snapshot in the trainer's order and reads its index file at the place the store registered for the shard (`store.disk_path`, `artifact_files.shard_paths`, `read_shard_index`), keeping `sequence_lengths`. `corrected_run_record` copies the segment's run record, sets its shard list to those shards, replaces the recorded digests of the index arrays by those of what `join.replay_arrays` derives from these lengths, and gives each shard the `ids.file_digest` of the index file it just read as `idx_sha256`. `shard_lengths` and `corrected_run_record` compare the file with no digest the store holds for the shard (`Store.disk_path` returns the registered path and says the file need not be there): the mixture digest of D-71 comes from the store's recorded content digests, the lengths come from the file as it lies at verification time, and the join's own comparison of `idx_sha256` with the file's digest (`join/_join.py`, `_shard_lengths`) meets the digest taken from the same file a moment before. A shard that is not registered, or an index file not at its registered place, raises `LookupError` with a sentence; that is a reason and never an error: `rebuilt` false, the sentence in `reason`, and the run's restart is the phase start with `step_comparison` "not possible" (`restart_exact.exact_rerun_restarts`). That half is recorded in D-69 ("any failure give `rebuilt` false with the reason and the fallback of the phase start") and in D-72 (f) ("every other failure of the build is a reason"); this entry records only where the lengths and the digests come from. No test removes or changes an index file for this branch; the table row c09.E.stale-cache.E/seg-2 expects `rebuilt` true with the files in place.
- Other option: the store holds each shard's document lengths (or the digest of its index file) from registration, and the verification reads them from the store. Result of the other option (inferred, from D-71's own reading of the disk option): the verification would not need the shard files and would rebuild the corrected order from a store copy that holds none, and a file moved after registration would not turn `rebuilt` false; a file changed after registration would be read as registered, not as it lies, so the corrected order would be that of the registered shard and not of the file the builder holds.
- What would change it: a ruling that a verification must not need the shard files, or one that the file it reads must match the digest registered for the shard before its lengths are used.
[written at the M4 check]

## D-129 Which run segments get a `caches` entry (plan 4.4; docs/interfaces.md 7.3)
- Built: `check_caches` goes over the registered runs. A run gets entries only when at least one of its segments with a mixture is on a mixture the report lists with the action rewrite. Then EVERY segment of that run that names a mixture gets one entry, in segment order, including a segment on a kept mixture (it reads `stale` false, `rebuilt` false and its own mixture as `corrected_mixture`). A segment that names no mixture gets no entry and no word. A run with no segment on a rewritten mixture gets no entry at all, whatever the report says of its other mixtures: a run whose only touched mixture is one the report keeps, or leaves unresolved, is absent. Other option: an entry for every segment of every run, with a reason for a segment that names no mixture. Result of the other option (inferred): the `caches` list would carry a line for every run of the store, mostly "not stale", and a segment with no mixture would carry a line about caches it has none of; a reader looking for what the repair touches would read the runs it does not touch. The built leaves those out. Pinned by tests/rebuild/test_verify_caches.py::test_every_segment_of_a_run_whose_mixture_is_rebuilt_has_an_entry_and_other_runs_none (the run filter and the entry of a segment on a kept mixture) and the table row c09.E.stale-cache.E/seg-1 (stale false); the omission of a segment with no mixture has no test and no row. What would change it: a reader who needs an explicit line for each run the repair leaves alone, or a plan sentence that lists the runs. [written at the M4 check]

## D-130 A segment whose mixture the report does not list is read as one the report keeps (plan 4.4; docs/interfaces.md 7.3; D-74, D-80)
- Built: `verify_caches.check_caches` finds the report's mixture entries by the report's instance id and by the instance the store holds for the name (`_mixture_entries`), gives a run `caches` entries when one of its segments sits on a mixture the report rewrites, and for each segment of that run asks `_entry`. For a segment whose mixture has no entry in the report, `_entry` reads the action as none, the same as keep: `stale` false, `corrected_mixture` the mixture's own name (the first name in the store's order), `corrected_digest` equal to `digest`, `rebuilt` false, `reason` null. There is no refusal and no reason, and the text form prints nothing for it (it prints only stale entries and entries with `stale` null, D-85). A mixture the report lists as unresolved is different: `stale` null with a reason (D-85). How the case arises at 42f3068: the report the tool makes lists every mixture the store holds when it is made (`artifacts.wanted`), and `verify` refuses a report that names an instance the store lacks (D-74 AMENDED by D-80), so the case needs a report that omits a mixture the store holds, such as a mixture registered after the report was made or a report edited by hand (inferred). The "not stale" such a segment gets has no entry of the report behind it. No test hands `check_caches` a report that omits a mixture of the store (tests/rebuild/test_verify_caches.py lists both mixtures in every report it builds).
- Other option: `stale` null with a reason that the report does not list the mixture, as for an unresolved mixture; or a refusal of the report, as D-80 refuses an instance the store lacks. Result of the other option: for the first (traced, `_entry`, D-85), the verification would say it has no answer for the mixture and print the line, and not say that the caches are current; for the second (inferred), a report older than the store's mixtures would be refused whole, with the comparisons of its other artifacts.
- What would change it: a ruling that a verification names a mixture the report does not list.
[written at the M4 check]

## D-131 The candidate sources of a "may contain" (plan 2.1; docs/coverage.md 2)
- Built: when the failing link is a set of candidate parents, `candidates` are the sources of those candidates. Otherwise they are the sorted union of the sources recorded for the artifact itself and for every artifact reached from it by following its inputs, the shards of a mixture's snapshot, and the mixtures of the run segments of a checkpoint, all the way back, each artifact visited once (`Session.candidate_sources`; `Store.sources_of_artifact` per artifact, which follows no edge). The own list of the failing link REPLACES the walked one (`failing.candidates or walk.candidate_sources(artifact)`); the two are never joined. Plan 2.1 ties the list to the inputs of the producing step and says nothing of how far back it reaches; docs/coverage.md 2 (the entry for `candidates`) says the list goes back through the artifacts those inputs were made from. Other option: only the sources recorded on the immediate inputs of the producing step. Result of the other option (inferred): for a shard or a mixture, whose immediate inputs are chunk files and shards that carry no source of their own, the list would be empty or shorter, so the "may contain" would name the failing link and say less about which sources a team has to look at. Other option: join the walked list with the failing link's own list. Result of that option (inferred): the list would name sources that are not candidates at the failing link. Pinned for the immediate case only: tests/coverage/test_coverage_proof.py::test_a_deleted_edge_fails_condition_a_at_that_record (the list ["A"] for gen/P) and the table row c06.import.parent-unresolved (candidates ["A"], an import with one source of its own, which both depths give alike); no test or row separates the depths. What would change it: a plan sentence that fixes the depth, or a row that asks the candidates of a shard or a mixture under an unresolved link. [written at the M4 check]

## D-132 The proof mint is handed to `waterwhite.coverage` and every module under it, by the module name of the calling frame, once (plan 2.2, W6, Decision 1; docs/coverage.md "How does not contain is closed off")
- Built: `answers.claim_proof_mint()` reads the module name from the calling frame's globals (`sys._getframe(1).f_globals["__name__"]`) and accepts it when it equals `waterwhite.coverage` (`_MINT_OWNER`) or begins with `waterwhite.coverage.`; any other name raises `ProofRequired` naming the module, and a second claim raises `ProofRequired` ("already claimed by"). The one real claim is made when `waterwhite.coverage` is imported (`coverage/__init__.py`, `_make_prove`, `mint = claim_proof_mint()`), and the mint stays inside `prove`. The name is a string that the calling code carries in its own globals, so code that sets its `__name__` under the prefix passes the name test and meets only the once rule: tests/coverage/test_proof_only_construction.py::test_a_second_claim_of_the_mint_from_the_coverage_module_is_refused runs a namespace named `waterwhite.coverage._second` and expects the refusal "already". The gate that closes the mint is therefore the first claim at import together with the refusal of every later one (`waterwhite/__init__.py` imports nothing, so `waterwhite.coverage` is imported when something imports it; a claim made earlier by code that names itself under the prefix would take the mint and the import of the coverage package would then be refused with "already claimed", inferred); the name test is the first gate and refuses a caller of any other name (tests/coverage/test_coverage_answers.py::test_only_the_coverage_module_can_take_the_mint). The source scan treats the whole `coverage/` folder as the makers of the negative (tests/coverage/test_proof_only_construction.py::test_no_module_outside_the_makers_builds_a_negative_or_the_verdict_clear), the same boundary as the prefix.
- Other option: accept only the exact name `waterwhite.coverage`. Result of the other option (traced): a claim from a module under the package, such as `waterwhite.coverage._walk`, would be refused; the only claim in the tree is in `coverage/__init__.py`, whose name is exactly `waterwhite.coverage`, so no answer changes today, and the test with the namespace `waterwhite.coverage._second` would end in the message "only waterwhite.coverage may make coverage proofs" and not "already". Other option: no name test, only the once rule. Result of the other option (inferred): whichever module first imported the answer type and claimed would hold the mint, and the mint would not be tied to the module that holds the walk.
- What would change it: a module under `waterwhite.coverage` that must make proofs of its own (the prefix is then needed as it is), or a ruling that one named file claims the mint.
[written at the M4 check]

## D-133 The files a copy step lists for a shard come from the layout, not from the shuffled chunk (plan 3.3 copy steps, L3; docs/replica.md 5)
- Built: the replica's summary lists, for each shard, the files the copy steps are said to have read: for a partitioned shard the files of the records of its own group, for a round-robin shard every file that any group of the layout holds a record of (`files_of` and `every_file` in `build_layout_inputs`, before any shuffle and before a line is inserted). The list is what a recovery of an unobserved copy step is told as the possible inputs. It does not follow where the shuffle put each record. Recovery treats "one candidate" as one among the files named and does not see a twin in a file left out; identical texts found in two listed files give a candidates edge, so that a withdrawal of either source leaves the record unresolved (plan 3.3 copy steps, L3). Other option: list the files of the records the shuffled chunk really holds. Result of the other option (traced for recovery's rule, inferred for a given chunk): a round-robin chunk that holds one copy of a text that two files both hold would list one file, and recovery would record a single recovered parent and answer record-level for that record, where the built lists both files and records candidates; the other option hands recovery knowledge of the dealing that an unobserved step never gave it. The fixture's no-capture rows have no round-robin case (grep of fixture/table/check06.toml for "round-robin" finds none), and the rows there give the same result under both options (no twins in those shards, partitioned layout). tests/replica/test_replica_maps.py::test_the_round_robin_store_holds_its_shards_and_mixtures pins the built listing (every round-robin shard of the fixture lists the same three files). With capture on, the build hands the registered files of the list to `replica/pipeline_links.py` (`register_sanitizer_input`), which recovers the copy step over them when there is at least one, so a test that builds the replica with capture can reach that recovery; whether a test or row holds a round-robin chunk with twins in two listed files, the case that separates the two options, was not searched for this entry. What would change it: a row for a round-robin build without capture on a layout with twins, or a ruling that recovery is told what the build did when it did not observe it. [written at the M4 check]

## D-134 An import with no selected record is neither written nor registered, and what the verification then reads (plan 4.4; docs/replica.md sections 8 and 15; docs/interfaces.md 7.3; D-82)
- Built: `replica/build.py` `build_import` returns at its first test (`if not selected: return`, line 485) when the build's changes (`select`: a record left out, or a swap) leave the import no record. Nothing is written for it: no `imports/NAME.jsonl`, no manifest beside it, and no call to the capture, so the repaired store registers no import under the repair's name. `build_generation` does the same for an output with no rows (`if not lines: continue`, line 441), a test that no input of the replica reaches at 42f3068: fixture mode makes one output of one row each (replica/plan.py:550), a generated set skips a job with no rows (replica/plan.py:689), and a leave-out acts only where records are selected, which generation does not do. The sides file then leaves the name out of the side that did not write it (docs/replica.md section 15, which says it for a shard no record reaches), so an import whose every record the repair leaves out is on the repaired side not at all, and on the reference side not at all where that side leaves the same records out (D-63). What `verify` reads for such a name follows D-82 and its AMENDED line, and is not decided here. For the fixture's text-only import of T the report gives the import the action "unresolved" (the file answers "contains" through a file-level link and none of its records has a definite action; `artifacts._artifact_action`, and the table row c06.import.withdraw-A for the record): the replica's `_side` names only keep and rewrite entries, so the sides file never names it, an "unresolved" entry is never in the `lacking` list, and D-82's clause "`unresolved_records` above 0 still gives 'not claimed' for the multisets" is the reading. For an import whose report entry is a keep or a rewrite (`verify_sides.verify`, the `lacking` loop, for kind jsonl): a file one side holds and the other does not is a `differences` entry with null counts and a note, not counted in `differing_records` (D-72 (e)), and `multisets` reads "not compared" where nothing compared differs and "differ" where a compared file differs, never "equal"; a file neither side holds, which the report rewrites with `rows_in_successor` a whole number 0 and no successor holding a record, does not hold "equal" back and is named with the note of its own; a file neither side holds that the report does not show empty (a keep, or a rewrite with a count above 0 or none) holds "equal" back and is named "named on neither side, not compared". Other option: write an empty file for the import, as a release is written when the build's changes and its dedup leave it no row (`build_release` has no such test). Result of the other option (inferred): for an import whose entry is a keep or a rewrite the sides file would name an empty file on each side and the comparison would run over it and read it equal, instead of the name being left out and the reading above; and the repaired store would register an import with no record, which a query would answer as an artifact and not as an unregistered name, where the tool accepts such a registration. What would change it: a ruling that every side writes every file its plan names, or a report that states, for an import, that it is left empty and why.
- Its checks: tests/replica/test_replica_build_fixture.py `test_leaving_out_the_affected_records_takes_x_from_b` (no `imports/T.jsonl` when T is left out; the generation output of T is still written), tests/replica/test_replica_capture.py `test_a_repaired_build_registers_under_a_prefix_beside_the_first` (no `repair-1/import/T` among the registered names). No test was found for the generation half. The reading of the verification is D-82's, with its checks (tests/rebuild/test_verify_missing_artifacts.py, tests/rebuild/test_verify_empty_artifacts.py).
[written at the M4 check]

## D-135 The name shown for an artifact with several names is the first in the store's binary order (plan 3.1; docs/interfaces.md 3, 9.2; D-42, D-64)
- Built: `Store._artifact_view` reads an artifact's names with `SELECT name FROM artifact_name WHERE artifact_id = ? ORDER BY name`; the column has no collation in `schema.sql`, so the order is SQLite's binary order (the bytes of the UTF-8 text, capital letters before small ones), and `Artifact.names` holds them in that order. Where a result shows one name it takes the first: among them a hop of a chain (`Session.name`), the answer to a query that names the artifact by its instance id, a content digest or a path (a name the caller gave that is one of the artifact's names is shown as given, `coverage.answer`), a listing (`listing._shown`), an artifact entry of the report and an entry of its `upstream_files` (`rebuild.artifacts.shown`, used by `upstream._entry`), a checkpoint name in the restarts (`restart_free._name`, with the checkpoint's own read of names at `_reads.py` `_checkpoint_view`, in the same order), the mixture of a `caches` entry (`verify_caches._entry`) the descriptions of the `show` command (`cli.describe`, `describe_trace`), the request's own artifact names (`requests.py`), the shard names of the layout count (`counts.py`), the tokenization wrapper's input and output names (`tokenmap/_wrapper.py`) and the names a recovery shows (`recovery/_files.py`). D-42 fixes only that an answer shows one of the artifact's names or its instance id; D-64 gives a kept artifact a second name (`successor of <name>`), after which the name shown is the one that sorts first: `R2` for the artifact `R2` and `successor of R2` (capital R sorts before small s), and `successor of tail` for an artifact `tail` (`s` sorts before `t`) (inferred from the order, not traced in a run). No test fixes which of two names is first by anything but this order: the tests that read two names in order (tests/store/test_store.py::test_the_same_history_again_is_the_same_instance_and_writes_nothing, ::test_mixture_reads_back_and_names_a_shard_the_store_was_never_told_of, tests/integration/test_store_capture.py::test_a_release_registered_twice_is_one_instance) use a second name that is also the later one registered, so they do not tell the two orders apart, and the test of the replica's prefix `repair-1/` compares sets.
- Other option: the first name registered is the one shown, held in a column the table does not have now (the table has no row order: it is `WITHOUT ROWID` with the name as its key). Result of the other option (inferred): a kept artifact would keep the name it was registered under in every result after a repair, whatever either name is called, and registering a second name would never change what a result shows; with the binary order it can change what a result shows (a historical name that sorts after `successor of ` stops being the name shown once the successor name is registered).
- What would change it: a ruling that a result shows the name the caller used wherever it can (as `coverage.answer` does when the query names an artifact by one of its names), or a name the team marks as the one to show.
[written at the M4 check]

## D-136 The successor shards a row asks for are all shards but the kept ones, split by the rows the count leaves (plan 5.5 Check 9, ledger row BF-8; plan 5.1; docs/replica.md section 4)
- Ruled during M4 (ledger row BF-8 of plan 5.5 Check 9; the table row's source sentence was corrected in the same part). Built: `acceptance_check09.successor_split` takes the kept-shard list, and which shard keeps a row, from the plant list through the counting script's per-shard table (`counted["shards"]`, fixture/plant/count.py), and reads neither the report, nor `verify`, nor the repaired build's own list: the names asked of are the shards the historical build registered (its own summary, chosen by the naming rule of the entry on the shards a layout registers, D-137) together with every shard the count names, minus the kept-shard list that the count gives (`expected_shard_actions`). A name of that complement keeps a row when `rows_left` is above 0, which is the count's `lines` minus its `in_scope_lines`; a shard the count has no line for keeps none. `receives`, which is the count's own field (a number, in the count's table) and not the report's, is not counted: it marks a shard the repair rewrites, and a row of the withdrawn source whose text another source holds belongs after the repair to the file of the copy that dedup keeps (docs/replica.md section 4, "a partitioned shard" and "a row dedup kept"), so it leaves the shard it was in. `check_successor` then asks of a shard that keeps a row that its successor answers as the table row says ("does not contain"), and of a shard that keeps none that no successor is registered (the query answers "not covered" with the reason "not registered") and that no file of the shard's name is in the repaired build's shards folder. The rule is the same in both layouts, and the row still needs at least one such shard (`successor shards >= 1` otherwise). Other option: ask "does not contain" of every shard but the kept ones. Result of the other option (traced: BF-8's ground and docs/replica.md, a shard with no row is not written): a correct repair that leaves a shard no row, so writes no file for it and registers no successor, would fail the row. Other option: take the shards, or which of them keep a row, from the report or the replica's summary. Result of the other option (traced: `test_the_shards_asked_for_a_successor_are_the_counts_whatever_the_report_says` builds a report that omits a shard the count empties, calls another kept and rewrites a kept one): a shard the report omitted would not be asked, and a wrong report would choose the questions that test it. What would change it: a change of the count's per-shard table or of the placement rule in docs/replica.md section 4, or a report that states where re-sourced copies land. At 42f3068 the report's shard entry holds `receives` only for a shard whose answer is not negative (a negative answer returns "keep" before `receives` is filled), and the helper reads none of it. The owner decided a change to this, not built at this commit (the entry on where a returning copy lands, D-141).
- Its checks: tests/acceptance/test_check09.py `test_a_shard_with_no_row_left_by_the_count_needs_no_successor_and_one_with_rows_needs_one` (its shard-A has `receives` 6 and no row left, and needs no successor), `test_a_successor_that_is_missing_or_present_against_the_count_fails_the_row` (four cases: rows left and no successor, no rows left and a successor, a shard file of an unregistered name, another reason), `test_the_rule_holds_in_both_layouts_and_a_file_of_another_name_is_not_a_shard_file`, `test_the_row_still_needs_one_shard_that_is_not_kept`, `test_the_shards_asked_for_a_successor_are_the_counts_whatever_the_report_says`.
[written at the M4 check]

## D-137 The shards a layout registers are named by the documents' naming rule, not by what the tool registered (plan 5.5 Check 9; docs/replica.md section 4)
- Ruled during M4 (the shard-list ruling: per layout the helper's list is exactly the shard instances docs/replica.md says that layout registers; a companion of a shard and every other instance never match; round-robin unchanged; one test over both layouts' names). Built: `acceptance_sets.layout_shards(instances, layout, set_folder)` returns, sorted, the names among those offered that match a rule written from the documents: for the round-robin layout a name `<prefix>/shard-<digits>` (`SHARD_NAME`); for the partitioned layout `set/shard-<NAME>` for each source file of the set (`source_names`, read from the `[sources.*]` tables of the set's `set.toml`, the file's stem or the table's key) and `set/shard-gen`. A companion (`<shard>.input`, `<shard>.sanitizer-input`), a mixture and every other name never match, a shard that was not written is not in the list, and any other layout name raises `SetError`. The names offered are the instances of a build (the historical build's summary) or the shard names made from the files found in the two builds' shards folders; the rule chooses among them and adds none, so a shard registered under a name outside the rule is not seen by this list. Other option: take every instance the tool registered under the layout's prefix. Result of the other option (inferred): a name the documents do not give, or a companion, would be asked as a shard of the layout, and a layout that registered a wrong name would be asked about that name and pass. Other option: one pattern for both layouts (the earlier `shard-<digits>` only). Result of the other option (traced: `SHARD_NAME` does not match `set/shard-A`): the partitioned layout would list no shard, so the rows put to it would have nothing to ask. What would change it: a change of the documents' naming of shards (docs/replica.md section 4), a third layout, or a check that a name outside the rule is itself a difference.
- Its checks: tests/acceptance/test_check09.py `test_a_partitioned_layout_lists_one_shard_per_source_and_shard_gen`, `test_a_partitioned_shard_that_is_not_written_is_absent_and_the_sets_sources_decide`, `test_a_round_robin_layout_lists_its_numbered_chunks_as_it_did`.
[written at the M4 check]

## D-138 A shard the repair writes that the historical build never wrote is asked for its successor only where the reference build holds it (plan 5.5 Check 9, ledger row BF-8 as amended; plan 4.4; docs/replica.md section 4)
- Ruled during M4 (ledger row BF-8 and its amendment at 5.5 Check 9): a successor shard that the repair writes and the historical build never wrote is put the same question, and whether such a shard holds a row is read from a source the tool does not supply (the plant list placed as the replica's documents place rows, or the reference side's own folder), and the shard list of the repaired side is not asked. Built: `acceptance_check09.new_successor_shards` takes the shard stems found in the shards folder of either build (`shard_stems`, the repaired and the reference folder), puts the historical build's name prefix before them, drops the names the historical build held or the successor split of the entry on successor shards (D-136) and the kept list already handled, keeps those that match the layout's naming rule (`layout_shards`), and divides them by whether the reference build's own folder holds a file of that shard's name (`reference_shard_files`: a file named the stem or the stem and a suffix; the sides file, the report, `verify` and the repaired side's list are not asked). A shard the reference build holds must have a successor that answers as the table row says ("does not contain"), and no file test is made for it; a shard only the repaired build holds is a mismatch (`shard file of NAME`, expected none, got the files) and its successor is not asked. A shard only the reference build holds (the repair wrote none) gets the question and the repaired store has no successor for it, so it answers "not covered" and the row fails on the answer. Other option: ask every new shard of the repaired side for its successor. Result of the other option (inferred): a shard that only the repair writes would pass whenever its successor answered "does not contain", although a from-scratch build writes none, so a repair that wrote a shard the clean sources do not hold would not be a difference. Other option: leave the new shards out of the row. Result of the other option: a shard that exists only after the repair, which in the 10k partitioned set the promoted copies make, would be asked nothing (traced: BF-8's own ground; that successor split never names it). What would change it: a report or a store that states where re-sourced copies land, which would let the helper read the placement from the tool without reading its own list, or a build that writes no shard file without its pair of `.bin` and `.idx` (today a file of either suffix counts as the reference build holding the shard). At 42f3068 the report has no entry for a shard the store does not hold, so a shard that only the repair writes is in no shard entry (`artifacts.shard_entry` is made for the shards the store holds). The owner decided a change to this, not built at this commit (the entry on where a returning copy lands, D-141).
- Its checks: tests/acceptance/test_check09.py `test_a_new_shard_the_reference_writes_is_asked_for_its_successor_and_it_must_answer_as_the_row_says` (also a stray `shard-Q` is no shard of the set and is not asked), `test_a_new_shard_the_reference_writes_whose_successor_answers_wrongly_fails_the_row`, `test_a_shard_only_the_repaired_side_writes_fails_the_row_and_is_asked_nothing`. No test was found for a shard only the reference build holds.
[written at the M4 check]

## D-139 The per-shard digest row compares every shard either build wrote under the naming rule, whether or not the sides file names it (plan 5.5 Check 9, 4.2; docs/replica.md sections 4 and 15; D-82)
- Ruled during M4 (the digest-row gap ruling, made at the read of the helper's second part: the row compares every shard either build wrote under the layout's naming rule, read from the two builds' folders; a shard only one build holds is a difference; no expected value moves). Built: `acceptance_check09.own_digest_gap` compares in two parts. The shards the sides file names on both sides are compared by their digests through `acceptance_repair.own_shard_gap`; then every shard stem found in the shards folder of either build (`shard_stems`) whose store name was not in the first part, and that matches the layout's naming rule (D-137, the shards a layout registers), is compared by `acceptance_repair.own_folder_shard_gap` (line 251): a shard both builds hold whole (`.bin` and `.idx`) by `shard_digest`, a shard one build lacks or holds in part is a difference, never skipped and never an error. A file whose name is outside the rule (a stray `shard-Q.bin`) and a companion are not compared. The row fails when no shard was compared, when any differs by those digests, and when the verification's own word `per_shard_digests` is not "equal" (`check_digests`), so its own comparison and the tool's word each stand alone. Other option: compare only the shards the sides file names, as before. Result of the other option (traced: the sides file names only the artifacts the report lists, docs/replica.md section 15, and `test_a_shard_both_builds_wrote_that_the_sides_file_does_not_name_is_compared_by_bytes` builds a shard in both folders with other bytes, named in no sides file): a shard both builds wrote that the report does not list, such as the one the promoted copies make in the 10k partitioned set, would never be compared, and the row would read equal over bytes that differ. Other option: skip a shard that only one build holds whole. Result of the other option (traced: `test_a_shard_only_one_build_wrote_is_a_difference`): a build that wrote a shard the other did not would read equal. What would change it: a sides file that names every shard either build wrote, or a ruling that a stray file outside the naming rule is a difference. At 42f3068 the tool's own half does not compare such a shard: `verify` takes the shards it compares from the names the sides file gives on both sides, and holds "equal" back only for a keep or rewrite shard of the report that a side does not give (D-82), so a shard both builds wrote that the report does not list and the sides file does not name is not compared by `verify` and does not keep `per_shard_digests` from "equal". The owner decided a change to this, not built at this commit (the entry on where a returning copy lands, D-141).
- Its checks: tests/acceptance/test_check09.py `test_a_shard_both_builds_wrote_that_the_sides_file_does_not_name_is_compared_by_bytes`, `test_the_same_shard_with_equal_bytes_on_both_sides_is_counted_and_agrees` (a stray file of another name is no shard), `test_a_shard_only_one_build_wrote_is_a_difference` (both sides), `test_a_shard_one_build_wrote_in_part_is_a_difference_and_not_an_error`; the unchanged half: `test_the_digest_row_is_held_to_the_table_by_the_own_digests_and_the_tools_word`.
[written at the M4 check]

## D-140 A shard the count names that the helper's list lacks, and a kept shard with no file, are differences and never errors (plan 5.5 Check 9, 5.1)
- Ruled during M4 (the rulings on the diagnostic run: a shard the count names but the list lacks prints as a mismatch, not a KeyError). Built: `acceptance_check09.check_kept_shards` takes the kept and rewrite lists from the count (`expected_shard_actions`) and makes a mismatch `listed shard[NAME]` (expected "listed", got "not listed") for every name of either list that is not among the historical build's shards (`hist.shards`), and goes on; its local `digest` returns no value for a shard that has no whole pair of `.bin` and `.idx`, and `check_shard_actions` reads a kept shard whose digest before or after is none, or differs, as `byte identical afterwards[NAME]`. No lookup raises, so the other parts of the row (the actions the report gives, the tool's `kept` entries through `compare_kept`) are still made and the row names every shard at fault. Other option: let the lookup fail for a name the list lacks. Result of the other option (inferred, from the ruling's words "not a KeyError"): the row would end as an error that names a missing key and not the shard that differs, and the rest of the row would not be read. Other option: leave a name the list lacks out of the row. Result of the other option (inferred): a shard the count calls kept or rewritten that the build never registered would pass the kept-shard row. What would change it: a row that is to stop at a named setup fault instead of reporting differences.
- Its checks: tests/acceptance/test_check09.py `test_a_shard_of_the_count_that_the_helpers_list_lacks_is_a_difference_and_not_an_error` (the two fields `listed shard[set/shard-Z]` and `byte identical afterwards[set/shard-Z]`), `test_a_listed_kept_shard_with_no_files_is_a_difference_and_not_an_error`.
[written at the M4 check]

## D-141 Where a re-sourced or promoted record lands when no shard the store holds receives it is decided by the owner and not built (plan 4.1, 5.5 Check 9; docs/interfaces.md 7.1)
- Built: `artifacts.shard_entry` returns keep for a shard whose answer is the negative before its `receives` list is filled, and fills `receives` only for the shards the store holds. A record that is re-sourced or promoted onto a copy that no held shard holds or receives appears in no shard entry of the report. Seen on the 10k partitioned set under "withdraw A": the report rewrites set/shard-A (500 documents, 0 in its successor, `receives` empty), and the repair writes a shard set/shard-B that the store did not hold and that the report does not name; the acceptance row of successor shards asks for it from the reference side's build (plan 5.5 Check 9, ledger row BF-8).
- Decided by the owner during M4, NOT BUILT at this commit. The result decided: "keep" keeps its meaning, the piece stays byte-identical; where the user states how the layout places records, a piece a returning (promoted or re-sourced) copy lands in reads "rewrite"; where the user does not state it, the report does not say "keep" outright: it says the piece's own contents are clean, names the returning copies and says their place is not stated, so "keep" is never silently wrong; and `verify` does not read "equal" while a shard both builds wrote goes uncompared. No expected value of the table moves.
- Other option: the report stays as built. Result of the other option: a returning copy that lands in no held shard appears in no shard entry, a new successor shard (set/shard-B in the 10k run) is named nowhere in the report, and a shard a layout fills with a returning copy can read "keep" while its bytes change (traced: the 10k run of Check 9; inferred for a layout that fills a clean shard).
- What would change it: the owner's word. The decided change is built as its own part, tests first, and this entry is amended when it lands.
[written at the M4 check]
- AMENDED (built; plan 4.1, 5.5 Check 9; docs/interfaces.md 7.1; docs/rebuild.md 2.2; docs/cli.md "report"): the decided change is built for the report. "keep" keeps its meaning (the proof's negative answer, the bytes unchanged). `rebuild.placing.apply` runs once the entries of the shards and the files are made and before the mixtures: where the caller states a layout's placement (`report --placement NAME=FILE`, D-144) a shard the placement names for a returning copy that does not hold it reads "rewrite", names the copy in `receives` and gets a successor name, and a shard the store does not hold is named in `groups.placements[NAME].landing` (D-145); where no place is stated and no shard of the group holds a returning copy (D-143, D-147), every shard of the group whose own answer is the negative reads the fourth word "clean, place not stated", names the copies it may receive and says the place is not stated, and the mixtures, counts, groups and restart points read it (D-146). The part of the decided change that concerns `verify` ("does not read equal while a shard both builds wrote goes uncompared"), the words the replica and `verify` accept, and the refusal of a shard in no layout is not in this amendment. Other option: leave the entries as they were at 42f3068. Result of the other option (traced: the entry above): a returning copy that lands in no held shard stays in no shard entry and a shard a layout fills with one can read "keep" while its bytes change. What would change it: the owner's word. [written for the keep part]
- AMENDED (built for `verify` and the replica; plan 4.2, 4.4; docs/interfaces.md 7.2, 7.3; docs/replica.md 15): the rest of the decided change is built. `verify` accepts the fourth word, never holds a shard that reads it to the byte identity of a kept artifact, requires no successor of it and reads a mixture that reads it as a mixture the report leaves unresolved (D-150). `verify` does not read "equal" while a shard both builds wrote goes uncompared: which shards each build wrote is read from the stores and the disk (D-148). The replica reads the word and builds such a shard by its layout, and it refuses a shard that lies in no layout before either build is made (D-149, D-150). For the fixture no table value moves; for the generated sets, which no row ran here, see the last results of D-148. [written for the keep part]

## D-142 A checkpoint that answers "not covered" is named in the report, with its reason (plan 4.3; docs/interfaces.md 7.1; docs/rebuild.md section 4)
- Ruled during M4: every registered checkpoint that answers "not covered" is named in the report with its reason, whether or not a run reaches it; it is not in the exposed list, and no table value moves. Built: `restart_free.checkpoints_not_covered(store, request, session)` walks `store.artifacts(kind=v.KIND_CHECKPOINT)` in registration order (no new store read), asks `coverage.answer` of each one through the report's session, and for each whose answer is "not covered" adds `{"checkpoint", "segment", "step", "run", "answer", "reason"}`: `checkpoint` is `_name` (the first name, else the instance id, as `exposed_checkpoints` has it), `segment` and `step` come from `store.checkpoint` (null for an artifact of the kind that has no checkpoint row, which the store's calls never make: it is still named), `run` from `store.segment(...).run` (null when the store has no such segment), and `answer` and `reason` are the answer's own words, never a check of the segment. `rebuild.build_report` adds the list as the last top-level key `checkpoints_not_covered`, after `exposed_checkpoints` and beside it, always present and `[]` when none, and `cli._report_text` prints `not covered: NAME (REASON)` for each entry after the `exposed:` lines. Every registered checkpoint is then negative, in `exposed_checkpoints`, or in `checkpoints_not_covered`. Other option: leave such a checkpoint out of the report. Result of the other option (traced: `Store.register_checkpoint` names its segment by value, docs/store.md, and `restart_free._runs_with_checkpoints` reaches a checkpoint only through a run's segments): a checkpoint registered through the library over an unregistered segment would be named nowhere in the report, so a reader could not tell it from a checkpoint the store never held. Other option: build the key inside `report_without_restarts`. Result of the other option (traced: the callers of `report_without_restarts` are `build_report` and the tests that read the report's parts, among them the test that compares `verify`'s judgement with the report the tool makes for the same store; `src` has no other caller): the report a user gets would be the same; every other caller of `report_without_restarts` (tests only) would also receive the key and answer every registered checkpoint. What would change it: a store that refuses a checkpoint over an unregistered segment (then the key is always empty).
- Its checks: tests/rebuild/test_report_checkpoints_not_covered.py (the library and the command name the same entries, the partition of every registered checkpoint, the text form), tests/rebuild/test_report_command.py (the key list).
[written at the M4 check]

## D-143 A shard "already holds" a returning copy only by a limited walk (plan 4.1, 5.5 Check 9; docs/rebuild.md 2.2; docs/interfaces.md 7.1; D-141)
- Built: `placing._kept_parents` takes the map parents of the documents of a shard that `records.judge_document` keeps as they are (a row that names a set of candidates, a shard whose gate has no map and a document the report changes give none), and `placing.Chains` follows from each parent the edge of the record only while the edge has one input, is not an "any" edge, is not a generation edge and carries a record-level label (`vocabulary.RECORD_LEVEL_LABELS`), memoized and safe against a cycle. A shard holds a returning copy when the copy is the parent or on the chain above a parent: the copy itself, its parent, its parent's parent. No equivalent copy is counted and no "any" edge is followed. On the fixture (main world, withdraw A) the document D16 of E/shard-1 reaches src/B:L1 in two such steps; on the hand-built world shard-1 holds src/B:L1 through R2:L1. The ground is the reviewer's ruling on the ground of the table row c09.fixture.E/shard-1.action in fixture/table/check09.toml (its source: "shard 1 already holds that record from src/B"), and not words of the owner. Other option: count a copy as held when an equivalent copy is held, by any edge or by the text. Result of the other option (inferred): a shard that holds a text equal to the copy through a candidates row or an "any" edge would read "keep" although no record-level link shows the copy is in it, which is the silent "keep" D-141 rules out. Other option: do not count "already held" at all. Result of the other option (traced: c09.fixture.E/shard-1.action, "keep"): the fixture's shard 1 would read the fourth word and the table row would not hold. What would change it: a ruling that a copy reached by an "any" edge counts as held, or a table row whose shard holds the copy only so. Its checks: tests/rebuild/test_keep_held.py (the clauses of the walk, one case each). [written for the keep part]

## D-144 The placement is a file the caller names for a layout, by source, with a root rule (plan 4.1, 5.5 Check 9; docs/cli.md "report"; docs/interfaces.md 7.1; D-115, D-141)
- Built: `report withdraw --placement NAME=FILE`, once per layout, and only for a layout that `--layout` names (a placement for another name, a name given twice, an argument not written NAME=FILE, a file that cannot be read, is not JSON or is not of the form: a usage error, exit 1). The file is `{"form": "waterwhite.placement/1", "by": "source", "shards": {source artifact: shard store name}, "otherwise": shard store name or null}`; "by source" is the only rule, the store does not keep the file, and the report states its path and SHA-256 (the bytes parsed are the bytes hashed). The root rule (`placing._source_name`, `_target`): a copy lands in the shard named for its source artifact, which is the copy's own artifact when that is a registered source file (`src/B:L1` under `src/B`), else the root of its single-parent record-level chain when that is one, and when neither is, or the file names no shard for the source, in `otherwise`; null is "not stated". Other option: keep the placement in the store, or name the layouts the replica builds ("partitioned") in the tool. Result of the other option (inferred): a stored placement would be a derived fact outliving the files it describes, which D-110 refuses for the report; the tool would carry the replica's layouts although the report is a file so that the replica is never imported (docs/rebuild.md 1). Other option: place by line or by document.
