Result of the other option (inferred): the tool has no record of where a layout puts a document, only of the records, and a rule by source is the one the partitioned layout documents (docs/replica.md). What would change it: a layout whose place depends on something other than the source of the record, which needs a second value of `by`. Its checks: tests/rebuild/test_keep_placement.py (the flag, the malformed files, the root rule, the `otherwise` cases). [written for the keep part]
- AMENDED (built; plan 4.1, 5.5 Check 9; docs/cli.md "report"; docs/interfaces.md 7.1; docs/replica.md, the repair section; D-145): a placement file that names a shard, a value of `shards` or an `otherwise` that is not null, whose store name does not begin with the prefix of its layout is a usage error (exit 1): `rebuild.placing.check_placements` reads the file against `--layout` before the report is built, from the file alone, so a target no copy resolves to is checked too; the error names the placement file, the layout, its prefix and the first such shard, and no report is printed (the store is opened read-only, so its bytes are the same). A target inside the prefix that the store does not hold stays a landing (D-145). The repair holds the other end to the same rule: `refuse_shards_in_no_layout` reads the shards the report names under `groups.placements[NAME].landing` as well as its shard entries, so, when `--sides` is given (the refusal of a shard in no layout is made only then), a landing that lies in no layout of the build is refused before either side is built, with nothing registered and no folder written, and the silent skip of such a landing in the sides file is replaced by the refusal. Other option: accept the target as a landing whatever its name. Result of the other option (traced: the report named a landing "<target>" with in_store false that no build writes, since the repair builds a copy into the shard the layout's own rule gives; `_side` skipped a landing that lies in no layout without a word; `verify` read the shard both builds wrote as uncompared and never tied the two names): the report named a place for a copy in a name the builds never wrote, while the shard that received the copy has another name, and nothing tied the two. What would change it: a layout whose shard names carry no common prefix (a prefix the caller cannot state), or a decision that a report may name a landing outside the layouts it was asked for. [written for the keep part]

## D-145 `groups.placements` and the landing of a copy in a shard the store does not hold (plan 4.1, 5.5 Check 9; docs/interfaces.md 7.1; D-141, D-144)
- Built: `groups.placements` is a sibling of `by_run`, `by_layout` and `layouts` (which do not change): for every named layout `{"placement": {"path", "sha256"} or null, "landing": [{"shard", "in_store": false, "receives": [copies]}], "place_not_stated": [shards]}`, and `{}` with no `--layout`. With a placement stated, a copy counts as held only by the shard the placement names for it: a named shard the store holds that does not hold it reads "rewrite" and receives it (a shard that is rewritten already takes it into `receives`, an unresolved one keeps its action and takes it into `receives`); a named shard the store does not hold (set/shard-B in the 10k partitioned set) is in `landing`, with no artifact entry and no count moved. The shard is looked for by name among all shards of the store, whether or not its name begins with the layout's prefix. Where a placement is stated, it decides where a shard's own re-sourced documents land too: they leave the shard's `receives` (a copy lands where the placement says, and nowhere else), and `documents_in_successor` of a shard that receives records by the placement is `documents` less the documents that leave plus the records it receives (the arithmetic of the form; without a placement it is the one it was: a re-sourced document keeps its place). The arithmetic does not subtract a shard's own re-sourced document whose copy the placement lands elsewhere, nor does it avoid counting one twice where the placement lands the copy in the shard the document is in; no test of the keep part tells the arithmetic from the exact count, and the corner is returned as an open item. Other option: list no landing and leave a shard the store does not hold out of the report. Result of the other option (traced: D-141): the new successor shard of the 10k run stays named nowhere in the report. Other option: add an artifact entry for a shard the store does not hold. Result of the other option (inferred): the entry would have no instance, no answer and no file, and every reader of `artifacts` (the replica, `verify`, the counts) would have to tell it from the rest. What would change it: the owner's word that the store registers a shard it does not hold, or a report that must name the successor shards. Its checks: tests/rebuild/test_keep_placement.py (the landing, the receiving shard, every reader of "rewrite"). [written for the keep part]
- AMENDED (built; D-144 as amended; docs/cli.md "report"; docs/interfaces.md 7.1): a placement may no longer name a shard outside its layout's prefix (the usage error of the D-144 amendment, made before the report is built), so the lookup above is unchanged but only a name that begins with the prefix reaches it, and the words "whether or not its name begins with the layout's prefix" no longer describe an input the report takes. Other option: leave the sentence as it stands. Result of the other option (traced: `rebuild.placing.check_placements` refuses such a target before `rebuild.placing.apply` looks the shard up): the entry would describe a lookup no accepted placement can reach. What would change it: the D-144 amendment withdrawn. Its checks: tests/rebuild/test_placement_outside_layout.py. [written for the keep part]

## D-146 The fourth word "clean, place not stated" and what reads it (plan 4.1, 4.3; docs/rebuild.md 2.2; docs/interfaces.md 7.1; D-141, D-112, D-74)
- Built: the word is `vocabulary.ACTION_CLEAN_PLACE_NOT_STATED`, a member of `ARTIFACT_ACTIONS`; it is not "keep" and does not begin with it. Where at least one copy of the report is placed nowhere and held by no shard of the group (D-143, D-147), every shard of the group whose own answer is the negative (action "keep" at that point) reads it; a shard already rewritten or unresolved keeps its action. The entry keeps its fields and gains `returning` (the unplaced copies, `artifact:locator`) and `place` ("not stated"); only these entries carry the two keys. `counts.place_not_stated` is `{"shards", "mixtures"}` (a sibling of `unresolved_artifacts`; `counts.shards` and `counts.mixtures` keep keep and rewrite). `mixtures` gains a fourth list, `place_not_stated`, so that every mixture is in exactly one of the four: a mixture reads the word when its own answer is the negative, no shard of its snapshot is rewritten and a shard of it reads the word (its entry gains `returning`, the union, and `place`). `groups.placements[NAME].place_not_stated` lists the shards of the layout; the shards with the word are in none of the lists of a `by_run` or `by_layout` group. The text form prints a line for each such shard (its own contents are clean, the copies it may receive, their place not stated). A run with a segment on a mixture that reads the word is given the phase start as its exact rerun, as an assumption (D-74 amended). Other option: report "keep" and name the returning copies beside it. Result of the other option (inferred): a reader who stops at the word reads a shard as unchanged while a copy may land in it, which is D-141's "silently wrong". Other option: give the word to every shard of a layout whatever any shard holds. Result of the other option (traced: c09.fixture.E/shard-1.action): the fixture's shard 1, which holds the copy, would not read "keep". What would change it: the owner's word, or a layout that is always stated. Not built in this entry: the readers of the word in `verify` (accepted action, no byte check for it, no successor required, its mixture read as an unresolved one) and in the replica (its action words), which are the other part of the decided change. Its checks: tests/rebuild/test_keep_fourth_word.py. [written for the keep part]

## D-147 With no layout named, and for the shards no named layout matches, all those shards are one group (plan 4.1; docs/interfaces.md 7.1; D-115, D-141)
- Built: `counts.membership` gives the shards of each named layout and, as one group, the shards no named layout matches (all shards, when no layout is named); a shard that two prefixes match is still a usage error. "Already held" is counted over one group at a time (D-143): a copy held by a shard of the group is held for every shard of it, a holder of one layout does not serve another, and the shards of no named layout serve each other. A placement exists only for a named layout, so these shards never have one. Other option: each shard is a group of its own. Result of the other option (inferred): a shard would count only the copies it holds itself, so with no layout named every shard but the holder would read the fourth word, although the place of a copy is as unstated for the holder as for the rest. Other option: with no layout named, say nothing about a copy's place. Result of the other option (inferred): the report would say "keep" for shards a copy may land in, as before. What would change it: a ruling that the store's shards form one layout, or that unmatched shards are a group of their own per run. Its checks: tests/rebuild/test_keep_held.py (the group cases). [written for the keep part]

## D-148 `verify` does not read "equal" while a shard both builds wrote goes uncompared, and which shards each build wrote is read from the stores and the disk (plan 4.2, 4.4, 5.5 Check 9; docs/interfaces.md 7.2, 7.3; docs/cli.md "verify"; D-82, D-120, D-139, D-141)
- Built: `verify_sides.shards_each_build_wrote` is asked when a sides file is given. A layout is the names that share a prefix: for each shard the report names, the prefix is its store name without the file name the sides file gives its files (the reference side's, and failing that the repaired side's), and failing both the folder part of the name; a name with no folder part has no layout and nothing is said of it. The repaired build's shards are the shards the store registers as `successor of <name>` under that prefix (the repair registers the whole repaired build under it, D-120); the reference build has no store, so its shards are the pairs of `.bin` and `.idx` files in the folder of the reference files the sides file names for the layout (the folder comes from the sides file, the list of names does not). A layout whose reference folder the sides file does not give is left out: what the reference build wrote there is not known. Of the shards the report does not name: one both builds wrote is named in `differences` with the note "both builds wrote the shard and the report does not name it, so it is not compared" (`repaired` and `reference` null, no locators, not in `differing_records`), and `multisets` and `per_shard_digests` read "not compared" instead of "equal" (a "differ" stays "differ", "not claimed" stays "not claimed"); one only one build wrote is named with a note that says which build, and `per_shard_digests` reads "differ" (where a shard was compared in the seeded mode; outside it the word is "not compared" as before). A shard the report names is judged as before (D-82). Without a sides file nothing is added, as for every file the sides leave out (D-82). Other option: have `verify` compare the shard both builds wrote by the digest the repaired store registers and the reference files. Result of the other option (inferred): the reading would be "equal" over a shard the report never described, which is a comparison the report does not ask for; the ruled form leaves it uncompared and says so. Other option: leave `verify` as it was and leave the comparison of such a shard to the acceptance helper (D-139). Result of the other option (traced: D-139): the tool's own word `per_shard_digests` would read "equal" over a shard no comparison of the tool's own touched. Result of the built rule for a generated set (traced on the small set of tests/replica/test_replica_repair_empty_shard.py, where the shard of B's file that both builds write reads "not compared" for both words; inferred for the 10k run, which is gated and was not made here): the shard the promoted copies make in the partitioned 10k set (set/shard-B) is written by both builds and is no artifact entry of the report, so the rows c09.10k.multiset and c09.10k.shard-digests, whose table value is "equal", would read "not compared" for the tool's word; the fixture rows are not touched (every shard of its layouts is an entry of the report). What would change it: a ruling that a shard the report names in `groups.placements[NAME].landing` counts as named, or that `verify` compares such a shard itself, or the owner's word on the two rows. Its checks: tests/rebuild/test_keep_verify.py (the sides cases). [written for the keep part]
- AMENDED (ruled: a landing shard is compared like a rewritten shard): a shard the report names under `groups.placements[NAME].landing` is a shard the report names. `replica.repair._side` names it in the sides file, on each side whose build holds it (D-120), and `verify_sides.verify` reads it as a shard of kind shard: compared where both sides name it (its two digests feed `per_shard_digests`, and its being named on both sides is what stops it holding either word back; the multisets read only the release files, as for a rewritten shard), and named in `differences` as a name on one side only where one side names it. A landing shard that the stores and the disk show both builds wrote and that the sides file does not name on both sides is a file the comparison lacks: it is named in `differences` (where the sides name it on neither side: a note that both builds wrote it, the report names it under a landing, and the sides file does not name it on both sides; where they name it on one side, the line of a name on one side only) and `multisets` and `per_shard_digests` do not read "equal" while it lacks. A landing shard only one build wrote is named as a shard one build alone wrote and the word of `per_shard_digests` reads "differ" in the seeded mode; one neither build wrote is not mentioned. `shards_each_build_wrote` takes the landing names so that they share a layout with the shards of the report; `verify` settles which of them the sides already compare or name, so that none is reported twice. Only a shard the report names nowhere reads "not compared" (the sentence above). Other option: a landing shard read as one the report names nowhere. Result of the other option (derived, traced through `verify_sides`; the measured answer is owed on the server): the partitioned 10k rows c09.10k.multiset and c09.10k.shard-digests would read "not compared" where both builds agree. What would change it: a ruling that a shard the report names only under a landing is not a shard the report describes, or the owner's word on the two rows. Its checks: tests/rebuild/test_keep_verify.py (the landing cases) and tests/replica/test_replica_repair_empty_shard.py (the sides file names the landing shard; both words read "equal"). [written for the keep part]

## D-149 The refusal of a shard that lies in no layout moves before both builds (plan 4.4, 5.8 B-7; docs/interfaces.md 9.5; docs/replica.md 15; D-118, D-120)
- Built: `replica.repair.refuse_shards_in_no_layout` reads the report and the layouts of the plan (`Layouts(plan, historical name prefix)`) and refuses an entry of kind "shard" whose action is keep, rewrite or "clean, place not stated" and whose store name begins with no layout's prefix: an input error (exit 1), one line, naming the shard and the layout prefixes. `repair` asks it right after `plan_repair` and before the other refusals and both builds, when a sides file is asked for (the same trigger as before: the sides file is where a shard is looked up by its layout). A refused repair registers no successor and writes no folder. `_side` keeps its own refusal, for a caller of `write_sides` that made the builds itself; for `repair` it is not reached. The entries refused are those refused before; an entry with no kind and an unresolved shard are left out as before. Other option: refuse such a shard whether or not a sides file is asked for. Result of the other option (inferred): a repair with no sides file for a store whose shards lie in layouts the build does not know would stop, where it builds the repaired side today. Other option: leave the refusal after both builds. Result of the other option (traced: D-120 (c)): both builds are made and the successors registered before the one-line refusal, so a refused repair leaves registered successors and two folders. What would change it: a store whose shards lie in layouts the build does not know and are to be left out (D-120 (c)), or a ruling that a repair with no sides file refuses too. Its checks: tests/replica/test_replica_repair_keep.py (no repaired folder, no reference folder, no sides file, no successor). [written for the keep part]

## D-150 A shard that reads the fourth word is named in the sides file and compared, never held to its recorded bytes (plan 4.1, 4.2, 4.4; docs/interfaces.md 7.2, 7.3; docs/rebuild.md 2.2; D-141, D-146, D-82)
- Built: the replica's `ARTIFACT_ACTIONS` and the vocabulary's gain the word together (the replica's tuple is the vocabulary's plus "unresolved", in the same order, which tests/replica/test_replica_repair_words.py pins). `_side` names a shard of the word like a kept or a rewritten one, so the shard the repaired build writes by its layout and the one the reference build writes are in the sides file and `verify` compares them. In `verify` the word is among the actions a file is compared on: a shard or file of the word that the sides do not name on both sides keeps `multisets` or `per_shard_digests` from reading "equal" (D-82). The check of kept artifacts never applies to it (it has no `kept` entry), `check_successors` requires no successor (only "rewrite" has one), and a mixture that reads it is given the `caches` entry of a mixture the report leaves unresolved, with a reason of its own: the report does not state where the records that return land, so no corrected mixture is named. This closes the part D-146 left out ("Not built in this entry"). The ruled form does not say that the word is compared; it is decided here, because the shard that reads it is the one whose bytes a returning copy may have changed. Other option: name no shard of the word in the sides file, as for an unresolved one. Result of the other option (inferred): `verify` would read "equal" over a shard that may differ and was never compared, which D-82 forbids for every other shard of the report. Other option: hold such a shard to its recorded digest as a kept one. Result of the other option (traced: `_kept_entry` compares the file the repaired side names with the registered digest): a shard that received a returning copy would read as not identical although the word says its bytes are not claimed. What would change it: a ruling that a shard that reads the word is left out of the sides file. Its checks: tests/rebuild/test_keep_verify.py, tests/replica/test_replica_repair_keep.py, tests/replica/test_replica_repair_words.py. [written for the keep part]

## D-M6-1 The open objections handed over at the close-out, recorded unchanged (plan Section 7, Decision 30, Section 12 change 2, Check 8; hand-off M6)
- Recorded, not changed: O-0dae83cf, O-3fe96fa0, O-63f03189 and O-913fb513: the six extension designs lie beyond the request's statement; the core and their off states are built, and keeping or deleting the designs is the owner's call (Decision 30; his answer of 14 Nov 2026 was to keep them). O-8b5fa0c0: the basis on every checkpoint "contains" answer (Check 8). O-d8088952#3: the generating-model rule's default changes core answers (Section 12 change 2); the docs say so where the rule is described.

- Recorded: N-1d7afddd (the Check 5 clause is core), N-256b9d47 (the A6.5d row points at its decline), N-48a8cf15 (plan 4.5: which of 2.2(a), (g), (h) fails), N-5cfcaad8 (L9 and 2.4 in one wording), N-9276955e (a test for the loosening of changes 17 to 21), N-9397163c (F6 per version), N-a815308a (3.3 scoped by 2.2(h)). ## D-M6-3 What the close-out reading of Sections 11 and 12 found (plan Sections 11 and 12; checks C1, C2)
- Read: every row of Section 11 from A1.1 to B4.2 (58 rows) and every change of Section 12 (21), each against the built tool's code, tests and docs. Result: 47 met by the tool or its docs, 19 about the plan's own text, 12 for an extension that is off and built only in its off state; one contradicted; none left unchecked.
- The one contradiction, now corrected in docs/rebuild.md: it said a round-robin layout rewrites every shard of the phase without the condition plan 4.5 and change 12 attach (the source's records must reach every chunk). Two stale sentences were corrected with it: docs/confine.md said the fixture's generation jobs run in the in-process harness (a build whose answers the table grades runs them confined), and docs/coverage.md listed the exposure join as not built (it is built, docs/join.md; the proof reads its traces and computes none).
- Observations, not changes: the checkpoint level "eligible" is defined in vocabulary.py and never reported (docs/coverage.md says so); a row ingested as a source member can reach a negative for another source, while the text-only import (capture.import_jsonl) creates no member and answers "may contain" for any source its file family does not name, so the plan's "plain import" means the member path; the plan itself says "which of the three" where it defines two (change 10, 2.4; plan item B-10 is open on it) and calls a Megatron run pending (note C-6 on A10.2) that its plan 5.3 records as done.
- C9 stays OPEN: the pass condition of 5.5 is not met while Check 10 has no run of the Dolma toolkit and of OriginBlame on the replica's output and the items of 5.6 (the 240 timed runs) are not reported with their workload.

## D-151 Four loud checks follow the five that were there (plan 2.2, 3.4; docs/store.md "The loud checks"; D-18, D-91)
- Built: check 6 holds a run record's text against its registered digest. Check 7 recomputes a mixture's content digest, its parameter digest (when the settings text is canonical) and its instance id from the snapshot entries; a mixture with no entry at all is a record that is gone and no fault. Check 8 requires the records of a binding to be numbered L1 to Ln (ordinals 0 to n-1 for a shard's map) when the binding declares n, for an artifact whose file is present. Check 9 requires one digest per blend array. Each raises a named error of the integrity family (exit 3): "stored digest mismatch", "locator domain mismatch", "cache digest conflict". They come after check 5, so every faulted fixture keeps its old name.
- Result: a record altered without the digest or count that was recorded with it is found; an altered run record, a mixture that lost an entry and a changed locator no longer answer "does not contain" or "contains" from altered history. The checks hold a record against what the store recorded of it; they do not defeat a rewrite of every commitment, and docs/store.md says so.
- Other option: no check. Result: those alterations answer from altered history.

## D-152 The step inventory is not checked against the launches (plan 2.2; docs/coverage.md condition (e); docs/join.md "unsized"; docs/store.md "States that are not faults")
- Not built. A step that a launch covers and that is not registered is an untraced step ("may contain", never a shorter history), whatever the reason. Through the real query a removed middle step answers "may contain, step untraced" and never a shorter history; the integrity checks pass on that store and two tests pin it.
- Result of a loud check: stores written through the write API (an operator's step record that lists fewer steps than the launches, a step the join cannot size) would answer with an exit-3 refusal instead of "may contain", and test_a_step_missing_from_the_step_record_is_untraced and test_steps_before_the_first_registered_one_are_untraced_when_the_launch_shows_them would flip. The store holds no count or digest of the registered steps, so a removed step and a never-listed one look the same.
- What would change it: a decision that a hole is a fault, or a commitment to the registered steps stored at registration.

## D-153 An artifact's edge digest and a trace's rows are not recomputed by the loud checks (plan 2.2; docs/store.md "States that are not faults"; D-18)
- Not built. The edge digest covers the child's locator, its used parents and the parent an "any" edge kept. Recomputing it from the stored edge rows makes an edge input moved to another record, an edited used flag and a removed edge faults. The base text of docs/store.md lists under "States that are not faults" an occurrence with no edge and an edge label edited after writing (read for conditions 2.2(f) and 2.2(g)); the digest does not cover the label. The acceptance table grades a removed edge (f.edge-deleted) and an input's used flag edited (s.in-scope-only) as states the proof reads, and an edge parent pointing at no occurrence (l.edge-parent, "edge parent missing") as a loud fault. An edge input moved to another record that exists, which is the case shown, is named by neither text; docs/store.md now lists it beside them. A trace row moved to another shard position has no recorded digest at all.
- Result of the present rule: an edge input redirected to another existing record, or a trace row redirected to another shard position, answers "does not contain" where the unaltered store answered "contains"; two tests pin that answer for an altered store. Result of recomputing the edge digest: test_a_chain_that_runs_into_itself_has_no_origin, test_a_record_in_scope_only_gives_may_contain_and_never_contains, test_a_jobs_sole_handed_record_is_a_parent_even_when_it_is_not_stated, test_a_fault_on_a_consumed_record_reaches_the_checkpoints_that_read_it and the removed-edge and used-flag states change from answers to refusals, and the document's own list changes. A check limited to a parent that is moved to another record, leaving a removed edge and a used flag as they are, cannot be told apart from a used flag edited, because the digest covers the used parents only.
- Ruled by the owner: keep as built. In his words: "Waterwhite does not defend against direct edits of its database, and the docs say so: such a store is read as stored. The signed table stays unchanged." The README and docs/store.md say it in those terms; the two pinned answers stay as pins of that rule.
- What would change it: a later decision that an edited stated edge is a loud fault; a digest over a trace's rows (a new commitment).

## D-154 Check 8 does not look at an artifact whose file is gone (docs/store.md "States that are not faults"; D-18)
- Built: the numbers of the records are compared only while the file is present; a locator changed while the file is away is found once the file is back (tested). Result of looking at gone files too: rows removed from the middle with a lowered count would stop a query while the file is away, against D-18 and test_records_removed_with_a_lowered_count_stop_a_query.
- What would change it: D-18 changing to "a gone file stops a query".

## D-155 The hand-off of a harness log keeps its order (plan 3.4; docs/store.md "The write methods", "Opening a store"; docs/capture.md and docs/interfaces.md "in hand-off order")
- Built: schema step 3 adds `harness_handed.position` (nullable, at least 0) and a unique index on (log_id, position) beside the primary key (log_id, occurrence_id). The write numbers the hand-off from 0 in the order handed (a record handed twice is one row at its first position); the read orders by position; a second registration of a stored job compares the hand-off in order.
- Result: the log reads back in the order it was handed, also after closing and opening. The same records in another order are another log and are refused when the job name is already stored (this changes an answer: before, the set was compared and the reordering was accepted). Result of keeping a set: the order the documents promise is lost at the first write.
- What would change it: a decision that the hand-off is a set, after which the documents drop "in hand-off order".

## D-156 A log written before schema version 3 has no hand-off order (D-155)
- Built: its positions stay NULL; it is read in the order of its occurrence ids, the order the store always gave, and a second registration of its job compares the hand-off as a set. Result of numbering the old rows by occurrence id: the store would invent an order the log never had, and the same job registered again with its real order would be refused. The order of an old log is not recoverable; recording it would need the log file the harness wrote, which the store does not hold.

## D-157 One array of a blend has one digest in the schema (plan 3.4; docs/store.md "The loud checks" check 9, "Opening a store")
- Built: schema step 3 adds the unique index `segment_cache_digest_one_blend` on (segment_id, kind) where the shard position is NULL, because the table's UNIQUE over (segment_id, kind, shard_position) holds nothing for a NULL position. Step 3 first refuses, with "cache digest conflict" (integrity family, exit 3, context segment, kind, held, version) and with the transaction rolled back, any store that holds more than one NULL-position row for one segment and kind, whether the digests differ or not. No row is changed or removed to make the index fit; a store from an earlier version is left at the version it reached.
- Result: a store written through the API cannot get a second row, and check 9 is reachable only in a file whose index was removed. Result of refusing only differing digests: a store with the same digest twice would fail on the index with a bare SQLite error. Result of removing the repeated row at migration: the tool would rewrite stored history, which it does nowhere else. Result of a refused-request error (exit 2): the migration would read as a refusal of the request, not of the store's contents.
- What would change it: a decision that a migration may repair stored rows, or a store format that gives the blend arrays a position of their own.

## D-158 A run record's digest is taken over the text the store keeps (D-151 check 6)
- Built: `register_run_segment` digests the canonical form of the record as read back, which is the text written to `run_record`. Result: for a record with text keys the digest is what it was; for one with number keys it now matches the stored text, so check 6 cannot fault a store written through the API. Not changed: a store already holding such a record keeps its old digest and still faults check 6.

## D-159 A handed text is compared with its record where the tool holds both (docs/confine.md section 5; docs/capture.md section 3; docs/store.md "The write methods"; plan 3.3) (the part that kept a log without texts at its bound is superseded by D-CORE-3)
- Built: `HarnessLog.handed_texts` and `EdgeRecord.handed_texts`, each a tuple of (reference, text) pairs in hand-off order, outside equality and representation (`compare=False`, `repr=False`), never stored and never part of `log_digest`. `run_job` and `run_confined_job` set the field on the log they return (`harness._run_job`, so both take one code path); a log from `read_log` carries none, because the log file holds references and never a text. `capture.generation_edge` copies the texts onto the edge it builds. When the store registers an edge that carries texts, on itself or on its log (`register_jsonl`, `record_jsonl`, a registered history handed again, `add_recovered_edges`), it takes, for the texts of both (each once), `ids.fingerprint` after the store's normaliser (the computation of an ingest, `normalise.text_fingerprint`) and compares it with the fingerprint stored for the record the reference names. A mismatch refuses the registration with `InvalidRecord`, naming the job and the reference and never the text; the check runs before any row is written, in the one transaction, so a refusal leaves the store as it was. A reference naming no record of the store is refused as it was. A record with no fingerprint (a line with no text, or one empty after the normaliser) holds no text: a handed text that is empty after the normaliser is that record, any other text is refused in the same way. An edge and a log that carry different texts under one reference are refused unless each is the record after the normaliser. An edge and a log with no texts register exactly as before. No schema change: the store stays at version 3, no column, no migration step, no stored log or old store changes.
- Routes (read in capture.py, store.py, cli.py, recovery, replica): compared = each text that an edge or its log carries, in: the result `run_job` or `run_confined_job` returned, registered in the same process through `generation_edge` or `generation_edges` and `record_jsonl` or `register_jsonl` (the replica's capture hooks take it), also a fresh result of a job whose log is already stored and the same history registered again; an `EdgeRecord` built by hand that carries texts on itself or on its log; a `HarnessLog` built by hand and given its texts; `add_recovered_edges` for an edge built by hand. Not compared = a handed reference that neither the edge nor the log carries a text for (a hand-built edge or log may give texts for only some of the handed references: those are compared, the others are not), so every reference of a registration that carries no texts anywhere: a result rebuilt from a log file with `read_log`, a `HarnessLog` built by hand without `handed_texts` on an edge with none, a log fetched from the store (`store.harness_log`), which holds no texts, and a generation edge with no log and no texts. For these the hand-off is the caller's statement that the text is the record at the reference, and the documents say so. The command line registers no generation edge, so the comparison is a matter of the Python calls. A pipeline that registers an output in a later process from a log file gets no comparison.
- Result: a job handed the reference of a record of source A with the text of source B still runs, and registering its output is refused, in process and in the sandbox, naming the job and the reference. Before, the output registered with A as its parent, withdrawing A answered "contains" with the process-evidence note, and withdrawing B answered "may contain" (upper bound not established) in process and "does not contain" for the sandbox log; those two pins (tests/capture/test_handed_and_reads_reproduction.py, tests/confine/test_confine_handed_text.py) now pin the refusal and that nothing of it is stored. With the right text nothing changes: a confined log still establishes its bound and a source outside the hand-off answers "does not contain"; an in-process log still answers "may contain" naming "upper bound not established". A log read back from its file or built by hand without its texts registers and keeps its bound exactly as today. A reference to a record that holds no text used to take any text uncompared: the output registered with that record as its parent, and withdrawing the source whose text the job really read answered "does not contain" past a confined log, a wrong negative in a new store (tests/confine/test_confine_hand_off_no_text_record.py). It is refused now, as is a wrong text that travels on the log of an edge built by hand (tests/capture/test_hand_off_text_both_carriers.py). Stores written before are read as before: nothing is stored, no mark, no schema step.
- Other option: compare and mark (the store keeps a mark per log of whether its hand-off was compared, in a new column with a schema step 4). Result: a log read from its file, a hand-built log and every stored log would be marked unchecked and lose the negative their bound gives, an old store would change at its next opening, and 52 existing tests change; the comparison would still be made only where the texts are in hand.
- What would change it: a log format that carries a fingerprint of each handed text, which would let a log read from its file be compared too (new: the file holds none today).

## D-160 `verify --require-held` exits 5 when the verification's conditions are not all held; the default stays 0 (plan 4.2, 4.4; docs/interfaces.md 2, 7.3; docs/cli.md "verify", "Gating on the verification"; D-122)
- Built: an explicit flag on `verify`. The document on standard output is the one `verify` prints without it. The exit code is 0 when every condition of the list in docs/cli.md "Gating on the verification" holds and 5 otherwise; standard error carries one line naming what is unmet. Code 5 exists only under the flag; without it every verification that is made still exits 0 and D-122 stands. Refusals (1, 2, 3) print no document, with or without the flag.
- The conditions fail closed: a sides file is given when the report names a JSONL file or a shard; multisets "equal"; per-shard digests "equal" where the report names a shard; no difference with a note except the nothing-to-compare notes; every kept file or shard identical; every successor registered and "does not contain"; every history entry instance_unchanged; every cache entry answered and, if stale, rebuilt. "not compared" is unmet only where the report names that kind. Restarts inform and do not gate.
- Consequences: a report whose only JSONL files are left no row reads multisets "not compared" and never holds under the flag. The conditions lean on `verify_sides.NOTE_EMPTIED`: a new nothing-to-compare note there reads as unmet until it is listed.
- Other option: a nonzero exit by default when the sides differ. Result (D-122 and the exit table): a difference would share the code of a refusal, or the table would change for every caller. Other option: a "held" key in the document. Result: the form of the document in docs/interfaces.md 7.3 changes. What would change it: a decision that a difference has an exit code of its own by default, or a held verdict in the document.

## D-161 What the Check 8 answers and the ancestry boundary look like in real output (plan 5.5 Check 8, 4.5; docs/interfaces.md; docs/capture.md section 4; objections O-8b5fa0c0 and O-d8088952#3 stay open)
- Evidence, not a decision. tests/acceptance/test_check08_basis_and_scope.py runs the real command on the built world for 11 Check 8 rows and five beside them (the table's and Check 5's) and asserts, in JSON and in text, what each answer carries. Output of that run: for a checkpoint's "contains" the answer carries a basis ("whole-sample read assumed", F9, L9), a level (consumed or inherited) and the first exposure (for example E/seg-2 step 7 for E@9, K/seg-1 step 8 for K@8 and K@40); the shared-token cases read "inherited" at D@3 and "consumed" at E@9. A "does not contain" carries a scope (source, members, records, runs, steps, generation edges, harness logs, walked, files not checked) and no basis. A "contains" that is not a checkpoint's carries no basis and no scope; its chain is its evidence. The documents give the scope to the negative only, and the test follows them. With a trace deleted the same rows read "may contain" at E@6 and keep their basis at E@9 and D@3.
- The ancestry boundary, as the code has it: a generating model is a field of a harness log, not an input or an ancestor of the output (capture.py module text; docs/capture.md section 4). With the generating-model rule off, a negative past a generation edge is not assessed for the model's training data and says so in its notes ("generating-model exposure not assessed"); the real output is the Check 5 row withdraw-C on E/shard-3, which answers "does not contain" with that note. With the rule on, such a negative is refused. Superseded by D-E2-1 for the generating-model clause: with the rule on, the negative is judged by the edge's model (and the ancestry of a registered model checkpoint enters the closure of the loud checks); with the rule off, the sentences above stand.
- Nothing is closed by this entry.

## D-162 A module that reads an answer's word asks the vocabulary, and does not spell it (D-160; tests/coverage/test_proof_only_construction.py)
- Built: `vocabulary.is_negative_word` is true for the word of the negative answer. `verify --require-held` asks it of each successor's answer. The scan of the source, which allows no module outside the coverage package, `answers.py` and `vocabulary.py` to spell that word or import its name, is unchanged; the first version of the gate named the word in `cli.py` and the scan stopped it.
- Result: the scan still means that only the coverage package builds the negative; a reader of the word does not build it. Other option: allow `cli.py` in the scan. Result: the scan would no longer say that no module outside the makers holds the word.

## D-170 The runner keeps every step, and a line names the steps it rests on (plan 5.5 Check 10; docs/baselines.md)
- Built: `baselines.runner.StepLog` writes every command the runner starts as one step in `steps.jsonl` (id, command, exit code, raw output of standard output and error together, seconds, the files kept with it), and copies each file a reading parsed to `kept/<step id>/`. `answer_line` and `attempt_line` carry `step_ids`: for a question, the steps shared by its store or set (the one run of the tool on it) and the steps of its own reading. Folders of the run are written as placeholders; a step that still holds a local path is kept with each path cut to its last name and says so, so a step is never lost to its own text.
- Other option: keep only a failed attempt's command, exit and output, as before, and let a reading hold only its raw value.
- Result of the other option (traced): a reading keeps no command, exit or raw output, so a later re-grade of a run cannot be repaired from the record, and a wrong parse of a companion or entry file cannot be seen after the machine is gone; it is read from `record.py` (the attempt line is the only place a command was kept).
- What would change it: a tool whose steps are too many to keep (the 100k runs), where the output of a step would be cut to its head and tail with its size and sha256 kept.

## D-171 The pre-run order is one function, and the interpreter probe is the copy's python alone (plan 5.5 Check 10; docs/baselines.md)
- Built: `pre_run` checks, in this order and before the gate of a tool opens: `compare.json` says equal true; `identity.json` is there; its own sha256 is the one handed in; the copy's python started alone inside the sandbox writes the path it resolves to and the sha256 of that binary into a file of the work folder, and that sha256 is the identity file's `interpreter_sha256`; for OriginBlame the program's sha256 is its `ob_binary_sha256`. A `GatedStarter` starts nothing, except the one permitted probe command, until the gate is released, so no code path starts a tool without the order. The runner reads `identity.json` and never writes it, and holds no hash value.
- Other option: the sandbox module's shell that records the interpreter and then starts the tool in one command.
- Result of the other option (traced): the shell makes no compare before the tool starts and the record is made in the same command as the tool, so a retargeted interpreter link would pass both sides; the runner stops before any tool command is built.
- What would change it: a probe that cannot run as a separate command (a tool that needs its first start to be the real one).

## D-172 A store run twice keeps both attempts (plan 5.5 Check 10; docs/baselines.md)
- Built: `StoreAttempt` carries `run` (1 for the first run, 2 for the second run on the same output). The record key is subject, target, layout and run, so the stopped writer's run and the second run are both in the record. The row `c04.retry` reads the second run where there is one; every other row reads the first. A line citing a failed attempt stands only with the attempt its row reads.
- Other option: one attempt per subject, target and layout, the second run replacing the first or raising an error.
- Result of the other option (traced): a failed second run, or a stop that did not land, on a store whose first run holds the key has no line to land on, and a good second run would hide a failed first.
- What would change it: a table row that reads both runs of a store as one answer.

## D-173 The faults are put to the tool's own files by the runner (plan 5.5 Check 10; docs/baselines.md)
- Built: a Dolma fault is put to a copy of the companion rows of the chunk file and written back in the file's own format; an OriginBlame fault is put to the entry files of a fresh store copy, each entry staying one JSON line in the file it was in, a line the fault does not change kept as it was. The result records whether the binary index existed and was fresh before the fault and after it. The stopped write, the second run and the changed file are put elsewhere and are no failed attempt. A faulted OriginBlame row with no putter stops the run before any start.
- Other option: put each fault through a call of the tool, or write a fixed text over the file.
- Result of the other option (inferred): a call of the tool would test the tool's own error path with a fault it made itself, and a text written over the file would not be the format the tool reads, so the tool's read could stop on the format and not on the fault.
- What would change it: a first real run that shows the tool reads the binary index and ignores a changed entry file; the putter would then also remove the binary index, and the line would say so.

## D-174 A step is stopped by the id of its process group, and every step has a cap (plan 5.5 Check 10; docs/baselines.md)
- Built: a real process is started as the leader of its own group, so the lock command, the sandbox and the tool end together; it is stopped by that id, never by a name or a pattern. `ProcessHandle.stop` gives the number of the signal sent and `wait` the exit of the stopped writer; the subjects decide whether the stop landed (the negative of the signal, or 128 plus it) and the runner records the exit in the fields both subjects read. A step past its cap is stopped and kept with its seconds as a failed attempt; the cap is an input of the run.
- Other option: stop the process the command line started, or no cap until a hang is seen.
- Result of the other option (inferred): the process started is the lock command, which forks the sandbox, so a stop of it alone can leave the tool running; without a cap a step that hangs ends no run.
- What would change it: a first real run that shows the signal does not reach the tool through the group; the stop would then be sent to the tool's own id read from the sandbox.

## D-175 A reader that fails still gives its line (plan 5.5 Check 10; docs/baselines.md)
- Built: `answer_question` gives a line for every question the tool ran on. A reader that raises an error for a row the table gives no join for is cannot answer with the table's words; after a failed tokenizer run of the store or set the line is the status of that attempt; an expected value that could not be derived is cannot answer with "expected value not derived" and its words. A store the tool did not run on gives no line, and a row read in two layouts is read and graded by layout.
- Other option: let the error end the run, or write no line for the question.
- Result of the other option (traced): the question would have no status, and a run of 2346 questions would end at its first such row; the completeness check counts a question with no line as not attempted, which would hide a run that did happen.
- What would change it: a table row that gives the join, which would be read by the subject and not by this rule.

## D-176 The runner drives two subjects from a world file (plan 5.5 Check 10; docs/baselines.md)
- Built: `python -m baselines` selects subjects by `--subjects` and takes the step cap by `--step-cap`; it drives `dolma` and `originblame` from a JSON world file that names the builds, chunk files, requests and counting documents, and refuses another subject. A question is put only on a store or set the world names for the subject.
- Other option: build every store inside the runner, and drive all six subjects.
- Result of the other option (inferred): the stores are made by the replica, which needs its own environment, and the two combinations need the trace export of each store; the runner would then start the replica as well as the tools, which the pre-run order does not cover.
- What would change it: the first real run showing that the builds can be made inside the sandboxed order, or the combinations being driven by the same options.

## D-177 The program is compared with a sha256 supplied on the command line, and the setup writes the one key of it (plan 5.5 Check 10; docs/baselines.md)
- Built: `identity.json` gains the key `ob_binary_sha256`, written by the setup's own checker (`--program FILE`, passed by the setup script for the OriginBlame environment only) from the same program file the runner is handed. The runner also compares the program's sha256 with a value supplied with `--program-sha256` before any tool starts, and stops at the stage `binary` on a difference. The supplied value is never read from `identity.json` and no value of it is in the code; a run of `originblame` without it is refused.
- Other option: compare the program only with `identity.json`.
- Result of the other option (inferred): the setup and the run would hash one path, which shows only that the file is unchanged since the setup; a program swapped before the setup passes both sides.
- What would change it: a program that is not one file (a folder of files), which would need a digest of the list and its own supplied value.

## D-178 Every step takes the lock for itself, the wait is a field of the step, and the cap counts from the acquire line (plan 5.5 Check 10; docs/baselines.md)
- Built: with `--lock-wait-bound` the runner makes a note file for each step in the step's own folder of the run record and sets `WATERWHITE_LOCK_NOTE` and `WATERWHITE_LOCK_WAIT_BOUND` on the prefix's process; the prefix writes `acquired <seconds waited>` once it holds the lock. The cap of the step counts from that line, the wait is the step's `lock_wait_seconds` and is in neither the raw output nor the seconds, and a note with no acquire line when the process has ended is a wait bound that ran out: the run stops before the step with `LockWaitStop`, writes no line for the question and keeps no step for it. A cap hit and a stop end the tool through the process group, which holds the prefix and the lock command, and the lock is free afterwards.
- Other option: start the cap clock with the process, and read a bound that ran out from the exit status 75.
- Result of the other option (traced): a step that waits for the lock longer than its cap would be killed at the cap before it began and be recorded as a failed attempt of the tool; a command that exits with 75 by itself would be read as a bound that ran out.
- What would change it: a first real run in which the process group does not end the tool behind the prefix, which would end it by the id of its own process read from the sandbox.

## D-179 A line of a record file that is not JSON holds no record, and it is counted (plan 5.5 Check 10; docs/baselines.md)
- Built: the three readers that map each record id of a build to its file (`IdMap` in the runner, `id_files` of OriginBlame, `_holders_of_ids` of Dolma) skip a line that is not JSON, as the tool's own line reader does (`waterwhite.capture.line_text`: "The text a line holds, or None when it holds none: the line is not UTF-8, is not parseable JSON, ..."), and count it for its file. They read the file as bytes and split lines at the line feed, as the tool does. The count is `IdMap.unparseable_lines` (file to number), the keyword `unparseable` of `id_files` and `_holders_of_ids` (a dict the caller hands in), and `unparseable_lines` of the Dolma and OriginBlame subjects (build name `<kind>-<name>` to the count of each file); the run keeps them in `unparseable.jsonl` of the run record, which `sha256sums.txt` lists. A parseable line that is not an object still stops with a `ValueError`, as before.
- Other option: stop the run on the line, as the reader of the build companion (`companion._holds_id`, which calls `json.loads` with no guard) does, or skip the line without a count.
- Result of the other option (traced): the generated set plants such lines in `sources/Z.jsonl` on purpose, and the build copies sources byte for byte, so every OriginBlame shard on a generated set, and every Dolma question that resolves an id on it, would end the run with a `ValueError`; a skip without a count would drop a line with no trace.
- What would change it: a decision that the build companion's `resolve_id` is to skip such a line too (no module of `baselines`, `src`, `replica` or `tools` calls it; only its own tests do), or that a parseable line that is not an object also holds no record, as `line_text` has it.

## D-180 A request word is checked against the builds of the questions that carry it (plan 5.5 Check 10; docs/baselines.md)
- Built: `check_request_files` takes the questions of the pass. A word is checked against the build of each OriginBlame question with a route whose row carries it (the `request` of the row's `given`; the build is the one named `<kind>-<name>` of the store or set the question is asked on). A word no question carries is checked against no build. A question whose build is not among the builds handed in makes its word count for every build. Called without questions it checks every word against every build, as before.
- Other option: check every word against every build of OriginBlame.
- Result of the other option (traced): the row of the stopped writer on the scratch store carries no request word and the scratch store holds only `sources/A.jsonl`, so the word "withdraw C" stopped the pass with exit 3 before any tool started.
- What would change it: a row that hands a request word to a build the row's own store does not name.

## D-181 The second review of 504bf64: a shard's files are checked, a negative that rests on an unchecked shard with its files away is not given, and the report names one artifact once (docs/cli.md "check" and "verify"; docs/store.md schema step 5)
- Built: schema 5 records for every shard what a read of its two files found (`shard_files_check`: passed or failed; none for a shard of an earlier store). A negative answer whose closure holds a shard with its files away and no passed record stops with "shard files not checked" (exit 3); with the files present the loud checks read them at every query, as before. `waterwhite check` reads the shards that are at their place, once each and outside a write transaction, writes only the records that change, and prints `shards_not_passed`. D-18 is amended for this one case: a gone file is still no fault for a shard that has a passed record, and for every other kind of artifact.
- Other option: keep giving the negative for a gone file (D-18 as it stood). Result (traced, the review's fixture): a store registered at a011cd5 whose index offsets name another document than the map does answers "does not contain" once its files are moved, and nothing in the store can say whether the offsets were ever right.
- Other option: answer "may contain" for it. Result (inferred): no condition of the proof failed, so the answer would have no failing link to name, and its word would say that a record may be there when what is missing is a reading of the files.
- Built: `waterwhite check` opens the store for writing, and for a store file it cannot write it opens it read-only, gives the same verdict and says "not recorded: the store cannot be written"; it never fails only because the write is refused. Other option: leave `check` read-only and put the re-check in a new command that opens for writing. Result (traced): a new command would also record, so it would also fail on a store it cannot write, and it would read the same shard files `check` reads; the legacy shard's negative stays refused until it has run; `check` writes nothing. Result of the built form: one command both checks and records, and a read-only archive can be checked but not recorded in (a legacy shard stays unchecked there; copy the archive, check the copy, or open it writable).
- Built (ruled): a listing, `report` and `verify` withhold only the answers that rest on an archived shard no check has passed: each is named in `withheld` with its reason, the rest is printed, and the command exits with 3 (the fault's code). A shard that answers "contains" is not judged document by document either (its entry in the report is "unresolved"). Other option: stop at the first such answer, as at a cycle. Result (traced): one archived shard of a migrated store (a common state: archived files are "a state, not a fault") ends every listing, report and verification with exit 3 and no answer, naming one shard per run.
- Built: `verify` refuses a report that names one artifact in two entries (by name, or by two entries with one instance) and an entry, other than a rewritten artifact's, that is not named by a name the store holds its instance under. A rewritten artifact's name may resolve to another instance: its `history` entry says "instance changed" and the gate does not hold on it. Other option: refuse that too. Result (traced): the documented "history ... instance changed" condition could then be reached only through the Python function, and a store whose name was moved to a newer instance after the report was made would be refused where it is now reported.
- Built, reading put to the lead (the ruling said: the integrity check, at the bar an ordinal has, no scan per query): a trace row whose stored segment is not its trace's segment reads, in the proof of a checkpoint, as a read of a document the segment's rows do not hold ("may contain", missing origin, condition a), the answer an ordinal the map does not hold (99) gets. It fires only when no row of the document is left in the segment; an ordinal edited to another valid document, as before, is not seen, and `check` sees neither edit. There is no scan of `trace_row` per query. Other option: a loud check in the integrity check over every row of the segments in the closure. Result (inferred): the cost grows with the rows of the store at every query, where the proof already visits each document once; it would catch both edits. A fault for `acceptance_faults` needs a row in the catalogue of fixture/table/README.md section 7, which this round did not add.
- Built (ruled): when a command is told of migrations (the command line), the version of each committed migration step is recorded in `meta` (`migration_unsaid`) in that step's own transaction and cleared when the last step has committed, so a migration killed in a later step still gives the earlier step's note on the next run; a process killed between the last commit and the printing of the note loses it. Other option: no record (the note of the first run only). Result (traced): a kill in step 5 loses the sentence that every harness log now reads as not compared.
- Built: a store of schema 4 must be migrated (`waterwhite migrate`) before any command but `migrate` opens it; the migration fills the segment of every trace row and takes about a second for a store of 100,000 records (1.3 s measured). Other option: read a store of schema 4 with the old statement. Result: a second statement for `trace_hits` and a store that stays slow where the index is not there, for as long as nobody migrates it.
- What would change it: a way to tell, from the store alone, that the offsets of a shard's index were read by the token reader.

## D-WORLD-1 Two builds that name one chunk file are merged in the tie record only when their results are equal (plan 5.5 Check 10; docs/baselines.md)
- Built: the tie record is keyed by the chunk file's path relative to its build, and the table's `main` and `no-capture` stores name the same paths. Each build's tie results are made in the build's own task, and `merge_tie_results` gives one result for each chunk file: results that agree on `shown`, `documents`, `lines` and `first_mismatch` are one result, and a difference raises `TieMergeError` naming the chunk file and both builds, which ends the command. The order of the builds changes nothing.
- Other option: keep the result of the first build to name a path, or write the result of each build under a key of its own.
- Result of the other option (traced): the first would hide a difference between two builds behind whichever finished first, and a row graded on the other build would read a tie it did not make; the second needs a key in the record that the reader of the record (`tie.is_shown`, which asks for the chunk file's relative path) does not have, and would change what a run reads.
- What would change it: a first world where the two builds differ on a chunk file, which would show the merge is the wrong rule for that path, or a tie record keyed by build.

## D-WORLD-2 The successor of a shard is made by the tool's report and the replica's repair on a copy of the main store, and its chunk file is copied under a folder of its own (plan 5.5 Check 10; docs/baselines.md)
- Built: the artifacts the table's rows name as `successor of ...` and the source of their request are read from the routed rows. The tool prints the report of that withdrawal on a copy of the main store made before any run is registered in it, and `python -m replica repair` builds the repaired side into the copy, with the job count of the command. The tokenizer input of the successor shard is copied into the main build as `successor/tokenizer-input/<name>.jsonl` and is the value of the key `successor of E/shard-3` of the chunk files of `main`; the tie show of it reads the shard files of the repaired build. A layout name of its own keeps the runner's mixer, which takes the chunk files of one layout, from mixing the successor with the historical shards.
- Other option: build the repair on the main store itself, or name the successor's file by its path in the repaired build.
- Result of the other option (inferred): a repair on the main store would leave successor names in the store the other rows read, and a path in another build lies outside the build the tokenizer starts in, which the runner refuses for a relative chunk file.
- What would change it: a table row that reads the successor from the main store, or a runner that takes a chunk file from another build.

## D-WORLD-3 The request words are those of the routed rows of every subject, and each is resolved from the main build and the spec (plan 5.5 Check 10; docs/baselines.md)
- Built: the words are read from the `request` of the `given` of each question the table routes to any of the six subjects, parsed by the table reader's own request table. A withdrawal names `sources/<source>.jsonl`; a request narrowed to a record names the id of the line the replica's manifest of that file gives the handle, read from the file itself; `withdraw W` takes its source and record from the spec's shared-token position, as the table's request table says. A word that is no withdrawal (the content ban) is not a request of a routed row and is left out.
- Other option: take the words of the two subjects the runner drives only.
- Result of the other option (inferred): the row of the shared token that the Dolma and trace combination routes carries a word no plain row carries, and the combination's driver would find no entry for it.
- What would change it: a decision that the combinations are driven with a world of their own.

## D-WORLD-4 The 500-line file of the scratch store is also a record file of its source (plan 5.5 Check 10; docs/baselines.md)
- Built: the store `scratch-500` ingests its 500-line file under the source `c04` and copies only `sources/A.jsonl` into its build, so no file under `sources/` or `gen/` holds the ids of the shard's lines and the plan of the shard (`track_plan`) stops on the first of them. The world copies the file the store ingested to `sources/c04.jsonl` of that build, where the readers of record files look.
- Other option: leave the build as the builder made it, and let the shard's plan stop.
- Result of the other option (traced): `originblame_shards` raises a `ValueError` for the shard of `scratch-500`, and the factory of OriginBlame stops before it tracks any shard, so no question of that subject is answered.
- What would change it: a reader of record files that also reads the tokenizer inputs a store ingests as sources, or a plan that takes the section of such a record from the store.

## D-WORLD-5 The sha256 of each kind of file lives in a named place, and independent builds run at the same time in processes of their own (plan 5.5 Check 10; docs/baselines.md)
- Built: three statements say where the sha256 of each kind of file lives. First, every file the world names has its row in `WORLD.sha256`, `world.json` included: the files of the seeded builds and the unseeded files of the store `no-capture` alike (the paths come from `_listed_paths` and each file is hashed when the command ends). The seeded files are the same with one worker and with several, and the three chunk files of `no-capture` are compared order-free between the two worlds (D-WORLD-8); the row of each is the guard of its own world. Second, the digests of the files `subject_inputs` names (the built-from documents, the trace documents, the frozen files of the repository the trace reads and the two files of the Megatron tokenizer) are inside `world.json`, each entry holding a path and the sha256 made when the world was assembled (`_entry`, `_repository_entry`, `_megatron_files`); the files of the repository and of the Megatron folder lie outside the world folder and have no row in `WORLD.sha256`, the files inside it have both. Third, `WORLD.sha256` itself is read back by the gate before any tool starts (D-GATE-1), and its own sha256 is in the start line of the run. With `--jobs N` above one, the builds of the stores and sets are tasks of a pool of N processes, longest first. Inside a task the steps that read and write files of their own go on together in threads, each thread a wait on a process of the toolkit or the replica: in a store the tie show of its chunk files, the runs and the repair; in the runs the registration in the store and the registration in each of the four starting states of Check 2 (the segments of one store are registered in order, since a segment's position is the number of segments before it); in a set the counting and the tie show. The tie show of one build uses one line tokenizer for each worker, at most one for each chunk file, the chunk files spread by size. The same N is handed to every builder that takes a job count. With one worker every step runs in order. The report and the summary give the processor seconds of the command and of every process it started, and the cores that kept busy on average.
- Other option: keep every digest in one place, all of them in `WORLD.sha256` or all of them inside `world.json`; and run the builds one after another, or whole builds in threads of one process, or leave the steps inside a build in order.
- Result of the other option (inferred): a list alone cannot name the frozen files of the repository and the Megatron files, which have no path inside the folder; digests inside `world.json` alone would make the gate trust a file that holds its own check, and a changed `world.json` would pass. One after another leaves the cores of the machine idle while one tokenizer runs; the tokenizer of the replica keeps module-level stop state, so whole builds in threads of one process could stop each other; steps in order inside a build keep the run to the length of the longest chain of one store (build, runs, four states, repair, tie show), so a machine with many cores is used by a few processes only.
- What would change it: a subject that reads a file of the repository or of the Megatron folder by a path inside the world folder, or a build that shares a file with another, which would be seen as a refusal of its builder.

## D-WORLD-6 The trace documents are made by the tool's own show command, and only a documented refusal in a starting state is kept as words (plan 5.5 Check 10; docs/baselines.md)
- Built: for each store that holds runs, the runs are built by the replica and registered through the tool, and `baselines.trace.export_segment` turns the printed `show SEGMENT --trace`, the run record, the step record and, for a segment with a parent checkpoint, the printed `show CHECKPOINT` into one document of the store. For the main store the runs are registered again in a fresh copy of the store from each starting state of Check 2, and a document is made from each. In a starting state the tool's documented refusal ("request refused", exit 2) of a segment is kept with its words (`failed` of the state, `none` where no segment has a document). Any other end, a refusal in the store as built, a run that cannot be registered in a state and a document that cannot be exported stop the command, naming the segment and the tool's words.
- Other option: keep every such end as words and still write the world.
- Result of the other option (inferred): a failed start of the tool, or an export that does not fit the printed document, would be written as "none" and read by a later driver as the tool's answer, so a world with no trace at all would pass as complete.
- What would change it: a first world in which the tool gives another end than the documented refusal in a state, and that end is the tool's answer; it would then be kept as words as the refusal is.

## D-WORLD-7 The chunk files of the sets of different sizes are named apart, and the smallest size keeps the path its build wrote (plan 5.5 Check 10; docs/baselines.md)
- Built: every size of a generated set deals its records over the same twenty chunks (the generator's default; the builds of the table pass none), so the sets of two sizes name the same chunk paths `pipeline/round-robin/tokenizer-input/shard-NN.jsonl` for different documents and lines, and the tie record is keyed by that path alone. The smallest size keeps the path of its build. Each other size gets a copy of every chunk file of its build at `set-SIZE/tokenizer-input/shard-NN.jsonl` (the same bytes; nothing the build wrote is changed), and that copy is the chunk file its Dolma entry, its OriginBlame shards and its tie results name. The runner groups the chunk files of an entry by the folder before `tokenizer-input`, so the folder name is free.
- Other option: stop at the merge for two sizes (D-WORLD-1), or key the tie record by build.
- Result of the other option (traced): stopping makes the command end after every build with no `world.json` as soon as two sizes are asked, because the results of two sizes for one path differ; a record keyed by build needs a key that `tie.is_shown`, the Dolma subject and the combination do not read, and would change what a run reads.
- What would change it: a tie record keyed by build or target, or a reader of chunk files that opens a file beside a chunk file by its path.

## D-WORLD-8 The files of two worlds are the same except the unseeded ones, which are compared order-free (plan 5.5 Check 10; docs/baselines.md)
- Built: two worlds are built, one with one worker and one with four, the count of the worlds of record (a machine under 24 GB of memory or under eight cores; on a larger machine the same tests compare one worker with four and with one for each core; that branch has not run on any machine), and every file they name has the same sha256 in both, except the three chunk files of the store `no-capture`. That build draws its shuffles from the operating system's randomness, on purpose. The read of the builder shows that the shuffle only reorders: it swaps positions in a copy of the records (`UnseededShuffler.shuffle` and `_fisher_yates`, replica/steps.py lines 70-75 and 128-129), the store is built in the partitioned layout alone (replica/fixture.py lines 95-96), which shuffles each shard on the records it is given (replica/steps.py lines 167-172), the line the spec writes into a chunk is skipped by the sanitizer, and the tokenizer input holds one row for each surviving record made from the record's own id and text (replica/steps.py lines 212-230, replica/build.py lines 626-627). So the three chunk files are compared as the lines over the shards together, in any order, between the two worlds, and every other file of that build is compared by its sha256. What guards every file, the unseeded ones included, is its sha256 in `WORLD.sha256`: a check of the list refuses a file whose sha256 is not the listed one, a listed file that is not there and a path listed twice, naming the file. A copy of a world with one changed line in a shard of `no-capture`, one dropped line, or one changed byte in any file is refused by these checks. The checks are in the tests of the world, and the pass makes the same check of the list before any tool starts (D-GATE-1: `check_world_hashes`, stage `world-hash`). Extension of 18 Nov 2026 (W9): the file a chunk file of a set was copied from (`copy_of` in the entry of the set) is listed in `WORLD.sha256` too; in a build made without capture its bytes are the copy's and follow the same unseeded line order, so it is held to its own copy within its own world and is not compared by its sha256 between two worlds (box set J21 showed the 20 originals of the small set made without capture refused when they were listed and compared by sha256).
- Other option: compare the three chunk files by their sha256 between two worlds as the seeded files are, or seed the build made without capture.
- Result of the other option (traced): the sha256 compare fails for the first two worlds built, which differed in exactly these three files and in no other; seeding the build would change what the builder writes for the store whose point is that no seed was set, and the builders are not changed here.
- What would change it: a row or a reader that depends on the order of the lines of a chunk file of that store, which would show the lines over the shards together are not the whole bar.

## D-WORLD-9 The successor of the third shard of main is the last OriginBlame shard of the world, with its index inside the build of main (plan 5.5 Check 10; docs/baselines.md)
- Built: the world holds a fourth OriginBlame shard of the store `main`, named `successor of E/shard-3`, flagged successor, of the target `main`. Its chunk file is the copy of D-WORLD-2 (`successor/tokenizer-input/shard-3.jsonl`), and its index and packed file, `successor/shards/shard-3.idx` and `successor/shards/shard-3.bin`, are copied into the build of main from the repaired side's tokenized shard (the `files` the repair prints, read in `_repair_part`), by the same route as the chunk file; the prefix of the entry is taken from there. The entry is the last of `shards`, after the shards of the set, so the shards of the stores and sets that come before it keep their places (the shards of the stores no-capture and scratch-notokens, added later, come after rr and move the numbers of the shards that follow them, and so the step ids of the questions on those shards), and `tracked_order` names it last. Its layout and its run are those the builder's own readers give its folder and its name (none), so a row that picks the shards of a layout or of a run does not reach it, and a row that does not carry `successor` in its id never picks a shard flagged successor (`OriginBlame._select`). A routed row that names the artifact picks it by name and target. The shard holds the documents the table says (`documents_in_successor` of `c09.fixture.E/shard-3.action`), its index and its chunk file agreeing.
- Other option: leave the successor out of the shards of OriginBlame, as the world did before this entry (a chunk file for Dolma only), or tokenize the successor's chunk file again inside the main build.
- Result of the other option: traced. Without the shard, the routed row `c09.fixture.successor.E/shard-3` finds no shard of that name on `main` (`_select`, kind shard) and gets no line for OriginBlame, so a routed row of the table would have none; a second tokenization would write an index the repair did not write, and the tie show of the successor and the shard of OriginBlame would read two different files for one artifact.
- What would change it: a row that reads across all the shards of `main` with an expected value that moves with a fourth shard, or a builder that registers the successor's shard files under another path of the repaired side.

## D-GATE-1 The run refuses a world whose WORLD.sha256 does not say what the folder holds, before any tool starts (plan 5.5 Check 10; docs/baselines.md)
- Built: the pass opens with `check_world_hashes`, given the world file by the configuration (`RunConfig.world_file`, set by `main` from `--world`), and stops with a `PreRunStop` of the stage `world-hash` (exit status 3, a sealed record, nothing started) when the `WORLD.sha256` beside `world.json` names a file that is changed (its sha256 is not the listed one) or missing, lists a path twice, has no row for `world.json`, or has no row for a path `world.json` names; and when the folder has no `WORLD.sha256`, because the world command always writes the list, so a folder without one is not a built world and deleting one file would otherwise switch the check off. The paths `world.json` names are those of the builder's own listing function (`_listed_paths`, from the builds the file names), not a second copy. The stop names its findings by their paths relative to the folder (the first eight in full, then their number), and the record holds `stopped.json` with the stop and no question, which the other pre-run stops do not write. The start line (`start.txt` and the line the command prints) names the sha256 of `WORLD.sha256`, or the word `none` where the folder holds no such file. It is the repository check that D-WORLD-8 names as what would change it, and it makes the checks that entry says only the tests make.
- Other option: keep the check as a helper of the tests, as before, or let a folder with no `WORLD.sha256` pass.
- Result of the other option: traced. As a helper only, a run puts a changed or half-copied world to the tools and records answers on it with nothing in the record to say so; a pass that accepts a folder with no list is switched off by deleting that one file.
- What would change it: a world folder moved to another place (the paths `world.json` names are absolute, so the listing finds them outside the folder), or a world file that the command names otherwise than `world.json`.

## D-SUBJ-1 One pass drives the six subjects in one fixed order (plan 5.5 Check 10; docs/baselines.md)
- Built: `--subjects` may name any of the six subjects. The drivers are built and run in one order whatever the option says: plain `dolma`, plain `originblame`, then the other four in the order of the table's list of subjects (the table names no order for them: only that the plain halves come first); the gates of the two tools open in that order too.
