The plain subjects' lines, kept steps with their ids and failed attempts are the same whether two or six subjects are driven, and a step a later driver logs for a store is a step of that driver's lines only. A combination named without its plain half is refused before any command starts (stage `subjects`).
- Other option: run the drivers in the order the option names them, and key the steps of a store by the store alone.
- Result of the other option (traced): the step ids are one counter and the steps of a store are shared by every question of it, so a driver that runs first renumbers the plain steps and a later driver's export of a store is cited by the plain none lines of that store; OriginBlame's copy and attempt counters are shared by every user of one object, so a combination that runs between the plain subjects changes the ids of their steps and attempts.
- What would change it: a table row that asks a combination before its plain half, or step ids that are no longer one counter.

## D-SUBJ-2 A combination starts from the runs of its plain half (plan 5.5 Check 10; docs/baselines.md)
- Built: the context of a combination starts with the targets its plain half ran on and the failed attempts of the runs that failed (the same attempt objects, also for the second run of a store). No baseline tool is run again for a combination's line, and its none lines stand on the same attempts as the plain subject's.
- Other option: the combination makes attempts of its own.
- Result of the other option (traced): a combination's context is empty, so its none lines on a store whose run failed read not attempted and no failed-attempt line is written for them; the failure is hidden behind the word the table keeps for a question never tried.
- What would change it: a table row that says what a combination's attempt on a store is when only its trace half failed (open).

## D-SUBJ-3 originblame+trace is served from the plain line for the groups the tool half reads alone (plan 5.5 Check 10; docs/baselines.md)
- Built: for the eight groups the combination reads from the tool half (`c03.spans`, `c04.interrupted-write`, `c04.count-499`, `c05.unmutated.shards`, `c05.withdraw-C.unmutated`, `c06.downstream`, `c09.artifact-actions`, `c09.successors`) and for the shard rows of `c05.f.map-deleted` and `c05.f.digest`, the combination takes the raw reading of the plain OriginBlame line of the same listed row and size (its twin, worked out from the table) and its own row grades it; its line cites the twin's steps. Old behaviour: the combination called the tool half's own read for the same row. A row whose plain row holds no record (the shard rows of `c05.f.trace-deleted` and `c05.f.trace-half`) has no twin and is read by the tool half as before. With the generating-model extension on, the group `c05.withdraw-C-on` is a ninth group of the tool half (added 18 Nov 2026 after the first dry pass with the extension stopped at its first row with no reading; its row says "as the row of the tool alone in this group").
- Other option: keep the second read of the tool half for the combination.
- Result of the other option (traced): per shard one more fresh copy, revoke and generate-set, and for the interrupted-write row a second stopped writer whose rows_showed ("while the writer ran" or "only at its end") depends on timing and can differ from the plain line's.
- What would change it: a table row that says the combination's tool half is read again, or a row with no twin that the table then settles.

## D-SUBJ-4 A pass that stops names the stop (plan 5.5 Check 10; docs/baselines.md)
- Built: the sealed record of a pass that ends with an error holds `stopped.json` (listed in `sha256sums.txt`): the error's text, the driver the pass was in and the question it was answering (none where the error came while a driver was built). The pass is stopped, never graded and never mixed with a later pass.
- Other option: leave the name of the stop to the error note only.
- Result of the other option (traced): the note is added to the exception and never written, and the question it names is the last question started, which is the wrong one for an error raised while a later driver is built.
- What would change it: a table row that reads a partly answered record.

## D-SUBJ-5 The facts each silence turns on are kept (plan 5.5 Check 10; docs/baselines.md)
- Built: `subject-facts.jsonl` holds one line per fact a driver reports: each export with its outcome, each half's failure with its own id, each shard's failure; it is sealed and is written when the pass stops. No bar is set for a silence of the table; a bar set later is a re-grade of these lines.
- Other option: keep only the first failed attempt of a store, as the attempts file does.
- Result of the other option (traced): two shards that fail on one store leave one attempt in the record, and the failure of the second is lost to a later re-grade.
- What would change it: a table row that names the one attempt of a store whose halves failed differently.

## D-SUBJ-6 The section hashes of a store are kept with its entry files (plan 5.5 Check 10; docs/baselines.md)
- Built: a step on a store keeps the store's `driver_meta.json` beside the entry files, because the reading of `c08.step-8-holds-no-T` takes the section registered for `gen/T.jsonl` from it and the line's raw does not hold it.
- Other option: leave the file out of the kept files.
- Result of the other option (traced): the reading cannot be repeated from the sealed record, so a later re-grade of that row could not repair a wrong reading.
- What would change it: the reading carrying the hash it used in its own raw.

## D-SUBJ-7 The manifest reads the groups whose arguments the listed row names (plan 5.5 Check 10; docs/baselines.md)
- Built: the manifest driver reads the walks of one artifact (the artifact is the listed row's own), the two mixture lists (the mixtures are the rows of the manifest whose name holds `/mix-`), the exposure-free restart (the checkpoints of the run are the ones the trace of the store holds for it, in step order) and, since 17 Nov 2026, `c09.artifact-actions` at fixture size, `c09.successors` and `c09.history`, by the manifest subject's own walk rule in their rows of check10.toml. An action is the walk read by the plan's own definition (contains is rewrite, does not contain is keep); the fixture's three shards of a run are the rows of the manifest named RUN/shard-N; the successors are read in the manifest of the repaired side (the subject's own run on that build); the historical R1 is read in the rows of both builds joined, the targets taken from the joined rows. The artifacts a request names are the names of the rows whose `files` hold a file the request names, so no table of request words to artifact names is kept; a request no row holds a file of is not read. The listing (`c05.listing.map-deleted`) and the file family of an import (`c06.import.file-family`) are read from the rule text of their rows: the listing is every name the manifest holds after the fault, by kind, with the names that have no row of their own as `not_covered` (the manifest holds no run, so none is listed and none is made up); the file family is the files the `built_from` list of the row of `import/T` names. The grader compares the listing by membership, with no order and no reason, and the file family by the files it names against the file of the member the listed row gives (the file comes from the world's own request entries and the fixture spec's member of each source; a member with no derived file is not graded). Not read: the rows of the 10k set (the rule text does not name the artifacts of their lists: the shards read keep, the number of chunks, each successor shard). A question not read gives no line and a fact in `subject-facts.jsonl`; it stays not attempted. Extension of 18 Nov 2026 (the table gives each reading of the 10k lists): the 10k set's lists are read from the rows the manifest names (a shard: a row whose files are its `.bin` and `.idx`; a chunk: the row `<shard>.input`, docs/replica.md section 16), `c09.10k.partitioned.kept-shards` as the shards read keep in the manifest of the partitioned build (key `set-10k-partitioned`) and `c09.10k.round-robin.all-rewrite` as the number of chunks read rewrite; `c09.10k.successor-shards` is the manifest's cannot answer, as for every subject (no fold of many shards into one answer, D-SUBJ-11); `c09.10k.R1-history` waits for the repaired side. The byte identity of the kept shards afterwards is not read (the rule text names the list of shards read keep only): the "right" of kept-shards shows that the manifest's walk reaches no file of A from each listed shard, not that the shards are byte-identical.
- Disclosure for the verdict walk (kept-shards): the shards the manifest keeps are named by the file stem of their chunk file, as the plant list's counting names a shard, and the registered names are kept beside in the raw record (`registered_names`); that a stem and a registered name stand for the same shard is read from the manifest's own rows, not from the bytes, and no row compares the kept shards' bytes with the historical ones. A box row that shows the two name forms differ for a shard would move the reading, not the record (a re-grade reads either).
- Other option: read the three groups with a list the driver makes up (the rows of one kind, the repaired side taken as the same build).
- Result of the other option (traced): `manifest._walk` with an empty set of names answers does not contain for every artifact that has a row, so a request with no matching file would read as a result; and a list made up by the driver is a reading rule the table does not hold.
- What would change it: a table row that names the artifacts of each 10k list (the successor shards come from the plant list, the others could be enumerated from the manifest's rows), the shape of the reading of the listing and of the import's file family, and a world that holds the import store and the 10k set.

## D-SUBJ-8 A routed row of the trace on a store with no registered run is not attempted (plan 5.5 Check 10; docs/baselines.md)
- Built: where the trace of a store is `{"none": reason}` and a routed row of the trace is asked on it, the driver gives no line and keeps a fact. The table puts no routed row of the trace on such a store (the routed groups are asked on `main` and `rr`, which hold runs); the none lines of the store are the table's own text.
- Other option: read the question from a document with no segment.
- Result of the other option (inferred): `c05.unregistered-checkpoint` would read not covered from nothing, an answer the store did not give.
- What would change it: a table row that asks a routed row of the trace on a store with no run.

## D-SUBJ-9 A none row on a store with no chunk file and no shard is put with the attempts of the pass (plan 5.5 Check 10; docs/baselines.md)
- Built: a none line (a row whose record is none and whose rule is cannot answer) is written after the attempt of its subject on the store or set its listed row names. A store the world names for the manifest or the trace that holds no Dolma entry and no shard of OriginBlame (no chunk file and no shard: the table's stores `import` and `swap`) can be no target of a tool's own run, so for it the attempts of the pass stand: where the tool completed a run in the pass, its none rows and those of its combination read cannot answer with the row's own missing text (the tool ran, and its records hold nothing for the store); where it completed none, the line is its failed attempt; where it has no run and no failure at all, the line stays not attempted. The rule is the narrow part of the earlier one for these stores only: every other none line keeps the attempt on its own store. A pass whose world names no such store (the first real pass) changes no line.
- Other option: leave these none rows not attempted, or give each tool a run on a store that holds record files only.
- Result of the other option (traced): left not attempted, 12 questions of each tool stay open and Check 10 cannot complete (README: every row of every group, six times); a run on record files has no text in the table to say what it is.
- What would change it: a table row that says what a tool runs on a store with no chunk file and no shard, or a README sentence that a tool which did not run on a store cannot earn cannot answer there.

## D-WORLD-10 A generated set made without capture is a target of its own (plan 5.5 Check 10; docs/baselines.md)
- Built: the rows of Check 6 whose `given` names a set and the store `no-capture` ask the build made without capture, so their target is `("set", "no-capture-<size>")`, taken from the row's own `given` by `record.attempt_target` (the store the row names, then the size); every other row keeps the target it had (a test over the whole table shows it). The name is no folder of a size (`set-<size>`). A question of that target is graded against the counting document and the index of its own build: the runner reads the world keys `counted_<store>` and `index_<store>` (`counted_no_capture`, `index_no_capture`; "-" read as "_"), and refuses a layout reading of such a build. The world builder makes these sets by Check 6's route (`replica.fixture.build_set_store`, builders unchanged), counts them with the counting script with the pair kinds not required, as Check 6 counts its build, and names the world keys (built; shown on a world of small size, not yet on a pass).
- Other option: drop the capture sets of the world so that the generated rows read one set of each size.
- Result of the other option (traced): the rows of Check 3 and the others that name a set would read a build made without capture, which the table does not say for them.
- What would change it: a table row that names the build made without capture for a row that today names a set alone.

## D-WORLD-11 The oracle's log is kept by the world and the expected sources by the record (plan 5.5 Check 10; docs/baselines.md)
- Built: the world builder runs the oracle over each store that holds runs (`oracle/fixture_runs.py` through the acceptance helper, the oracle and the replica unchanged) and keeps one file of log lines for each run, named by the world key `oracle_logs` and hashed with the world; the runner grades `c01.E.diff`, `c01.E-rr.diff` and `c01.K.diff` with it by `grade_whole_run`. The run record keeps, sealed, copies of the counting documents, the shard indexes and the oracle's logs the grader read (`expected-sources/`, with the sha256 of each), because a build made without capture cannot be built again.
- Other option: leave the three rows not gradable, or keep the world folder only.
- Result of the other option (traced): the nine lines of the trace and the two combinations on those rows stay open as "expected value not derived"; a world folder is deleted with its machine, and a re-grade of the rows made from the unseeded set's counting document then has no expected value.
- What would change it: an oracle log the replica writes itself, or a seeded build made without capture.


## D-CORE-1 A switch that cannot be read stops every answer, not only the answers that read it (plan 1.4; docs/coverage.md; docs/store.md; docs/interfaces.md)
- Built: the switch of each of the six extensions is checked before anything else is done for a request, a proof, a listing, a report or a `verify` (`extensions.check_switches`, called first in `requests.withdraw`, `ban` and `flagged`, in `coverage.answer`, `coverage.prove` and `coverage.check_store`). A switch whose value is neither "on" nor "off" refuses with the existing `Refused` and its existing sentence ("the switch of the extension 'X' holds 'ON'; a switch is 'on' or 'off'"), so a store with one broken switch gives no answer at all, including an answer that never reads that switch. `Store.set_setting` does not pass through the check: writing the switch again is how the store is mended. `show`, `check`, `migrate`, `recover` and `register-run` are not answers and keep working. The check is not part of `check_integrity`: the loud faults stay the four of plan 3.4, and the `check` command is unchanged. A missing switch row is off, not broken. The owner chose this on 18 Nov 2026 over the other option.
- Other option: check a switch only where an answer reads it, as before; a "contains" at a shard that passes no generation edge, and the listing of such answers, were then given from a store whose switch file could not be read.
- Result of the other option (traced): `test_a_switch_that_is_neither_on_nor_off_is_refused_and_not_read_as_off` pinned it (a negative past a generation edge refused, a "contains" given); that test now pins the new behaviour. No row of the table has a broken switch (no table store writes a value other than on or off), so no answer of the table changes.
- What would change it: an extension that needs more states than two; a switch kept somewhere other than the store's setting table. A setting under the prefix `extension.` that names no extension of this build (a store file edited by hand) stops every answer too, with a named error (`the setting 'extension.x' names no extension of this build`): a switch the tool cannot read would be taken for off. `Store.set_setting` still refuses to write such a key or a value other than "on" or "off"; `Store.remove_setting` clears one (removing a switch row means off).

## D-CORE-2 A store that holds a published model is listed while its extension is off, and the model is named "not covered" (plan 2.1, 1.4; docs/coverage.md; docs/interfaces.md; docs/cli.md; the reason word and the query are changed by D-CORE-5)
- Built: with the published-models extension off, `listing.listing` no longer refuses a store that holds a published model. The model answers "not covered" with the new reason "extension off" (`vocabulary.REASON_EXTENSION_OFF`, one more word of `NOT_COVERED_REASONS`) and stands in `not_covered` with the detail "the published-models extension is off" and the records that name it. Every other answer of the listing is what it was. A query that names the model is refused "extension off" as before, and with the switch on the listing is refused as before (this build holds the off state only). The owner chose this on 18 Nov 2026 over the other options. The report of a store that holds a model is unchanged (it asks no model).
- Other option: refuse the whole listing (the earlier behaviour); or pass the model over, so that the listing reads as if no model were stored.
- Result of the other option (traced): refusing: one stored model made every listing of its store impossible, whatever else the store held. Passing over: the listing would not say a stored artifact exists that it does not cover, and `covers` and `not_covered` would differ from the store's own inventory with no sign.
- What would change it: the extension built, when a model has a record that is covered; no table store holds a model, so no answer of the table changes.

## D-CORE-3 A log whose handed texts were not shown and compared establishes no bound, until they are (plan 3.3, F10; docs/store.md; docs/capture.md; docs/confine.md; docs/cli.md; README.md)
- Built: schema version 4 (`schema_4.sql`): a harness log carries `hand_off_checked`, set by the store and by nothing else. A registration marks a log compared when the texts of its edges and of its log cover every reference of its hand-off and each matched its record (the comparison of D-159, both carriers, a record that holds no text taking only a text empty after the normaliser); a log read back from its file, one registered with some or none of its texts, one with an empty hand-off, and every log of a store migrated from version 3 are not compared. `HarnessLog.bound_stated()` is what the log says (the confined harness wrote it, no read outside its hand-off) and still writes the edge's own bound field; `bound_established()` also needs the mark, so a negative past an unmarked log reads "may contain", naming "upper bound not established" and the job, with its own reason sentence. `Store.verify_hand_off(job, texts)` and `waterwhite compare-hand-off --job J --texts FILE` take the text of every reference of the stored hand-off and of no other, compare each as at registration, and mark the log; any refusal changes nothing and names the job and the reference, never a text. Registering a job again never changes the stored mark, and a value handed in for it is not read. A store of version 3 is refused read-only until migrated (the rule for any older version); a command that migrates a store says so in its output, with a note that clean answers past a generation step read "may contain" until the texts are shown. The owner chose this on 18 Nov 2026 over the other options.
- Other option: keep the bound of a log whose texts were never shown (the state of D-159: no mark, no schema step, old stores unchanged); or mark such a log permanently as "may contain", with no way back.
- Result of the other option (traced): keeping: a "does not contain" past a log registered without its texts rested on the caller's word that each text was the record, with nothing in the store to say so, old stores included. Permanent mark: the same answer, with no command to bring the clean answer back where the original texts still exist.
- What a later comparison shows: that each text given is the record its reference names. That the job was handed those texts stays the team's statement; the store never saw the run. The log of a job with an empty hand-off is never marked, and `verify_hand_off` refuses it.
- A returned log: `HarnessLog.bound_established()` on the log a job run returns (in memory, never marked) is false, because only the store marks a log; code that asks what the log itself says asks `bound_stated()` (four landed tests that asserted `bound_established()` on a fresh result now assert `bound_stated()`).
- What would change it: a store that keeps the handed texts itself, or a harness the tool runs that writes the texts on the log it returns. Where the original texts were never kept, an old store stays at "may contain" (nothing in a version-3 store says whether a log's texts were compared at registration).

## D-CORE-5 A published model the store holds, while its extension is off, answers "not covered", reason "not registered", in a query and in a listing alike (plan 1.4, 3.1, Section 7; docs/coverage.md; docs/interfaces.md; docs/cli.md)
- Built: one finding (`coverage._model_off_finding`) is the answer for a stored published model while the published-models extension is off: "not covered", reason "not registered" (lines 156 and 208 give both words: "not covered", "not registered"; line 378 gives the answer "not covered"), and the note "the published-models extension is off" in `notes` (the owner's answer on the listing: say the extension is off; the sentence is `vocabulary.NOTE_PUBLISHED_MODELS_OFF`). `coverage.prove` returns it for a query that names the model (after the integrity check of that one artifact, as for any artifact), and the listing takes its answer and its `not_covered` entry (the note as the detail) from the same words. `vocabulary.REASON_EXTENSION_OFF` is gone from `NOT_COVERED_REASONS`; D-CORE-2 had added it. With the switch on, the query and the listing are refused as before (this build holds the off state only).
- Moved: the query that names a stored published model gave the refusal "extension off" (exit 2) and now answers "not covered" (exit 0); the listing's reason word of that model was "extension off" and is "not registered". `ban` and `flagged` stay refused "extension off". The lead ruled this on 18 Nov 2026 after reading the plan lines; the owner's answer had fixed the listing only.
- Other option: keep the refusal for the query and the reason "extension off" in the listing (D-CORE-2 as it stood). Result (traced): the plan's three lines say "not covered", "not registered" for a query naming a published model while the extension is off; the refusal contradicts them, and one model got two different sets of words in one store.
- What would change it: the published-models extension built (a model that is registered and answers from its parent); no table store holds a model, so no answer of the table moves.

## D-CORE-6 A switch that cannot be read is named before `report` interprets a layout or opens a placement file and before `verify` reads its documents (plan 1.4; docs/coverage.md; the owner's answer of 18 Nov 2026, "checked first, everything stops")
- Built: `report` (withdraw) and `verify` open the store and run `extensions.check_switches` first; only then is `--layout` or `--placement` interpreted (a placement file opened) and the report and sides files of `verify` read. `rebuild.verify_sides.verify` (the library call) checks the switches before the mode, the report's shape and the request kind of the report. D-CORE-1 said "before anything else"; as built it was true for requests, proofs, listings and answers and false for these two commands. The parser still refuses a missing value or an unknown option first (grammar), and the library refusals of a call that is not a request (a request given that is not a mapping, a proof asked of something that is not a withdrawal) are the call's grammar and stay first.
- Moved (every refusal that changes for a valid configuration, listed because the comparison of the core rows does not show it): only the order between the store and an argument. A command line with no `--store` gave the refusal of the argument it also had wrong and now gives "the command needs --store FILE" (usage, exit 1): for `report` with a bad `--layout` or an unreadable placement file both are exit 1; for `verify` with a file that is not a report (refused, exit 2) the exit moves to 1. `report SRC --layout BAD` (or a bad `--placement`) with a store that does not exist, is not a store, or has a schema version this build cannot read read-only, gave the usage error (exit 1) and now gives the store's refusal (exit 2); `verify` with a report or sides file that cannot be read or is not JSON (usage, exit 1) or is not a report (refused, exit 2), and a store that cannot be opened, now gives the store's refusal (exit 2); a placement file that cannot be read or is not a placement (usage, exit 1) is behind the store's refusal in the same way. With a store that opens and a switch that can be read, no refusal, word or exit code changes (pinned by `test_a_switch_that_can_be_read_changes_no_refusal_of_these_commands`).
- Other option: leave the order and correct the sentence of D-CORE-1. Result (traced): a store with a switch that holds "ON" named a bad `--layout` or an unreadable report file and not the switch, so "checked first, everything stops" held for the requests only.
- What would change it: an extension whose request has its own arguments (the order of D-CORE-6 is the order for them too: parse, then the switch, then the arguments' meaning). No table store has a broken switch and no row of the table expects a usage error or an unreadable document, so no row of the table moves.

## D-CORE-4 What the README's "Core result" means as a file: records of every core row, one file, compared by the table's rows (plan 1.4, 5.5, W5; tests/acceptance/core_result/README.md)
- Built: the core result is `tests/acceptance/core_result/core_result.jsonl.gz`, made by `core_record.py` (what the tool gave to each core row: how the row ended, the reason of a skip, the programs the helpers started with the tool's whole output) and `core_result_file.py` (the one file from the first run and the two long-run runs; the gzip form; the comparison). The comparison reads the core rows only, treats a core row missing on either side as a difference, lists the rows of extensions and does not compare them, and says FULL only when every core row ran and none differs. A run that skipped the long-run rows says PARTIAL, with the rows not run by id, and never the words of the full verdict. Twelve rows asked of a store built unseeded are compared by how they ended, the reason of a skip, whether a program started and the distinct exit codes; the other 379 in full.
- Other option: the file as the words of the answer only (answer, names, failing link), or as the test id and its outcome. Result (traced): the words of the answer leave out the exit code, the stream the tool wrote to and everything a row asks of a program that is not an answer (a refusal, a map, a drop), so a changed exit code or a changed `show` would not be seen; the test id and outcome tells only that the row's own test passed, which the table's fields already say, and nothing about what the tool gave when a field the table does not pin changed.
- What would change it: a row whose question starts no program (21 rows hold the way they ended only); the extension rows entering the comparison when an extension is built (plan line 370); a store written the same way in every build, which would let the twelve unseeded rows be compared in full.
## D-WORLD-12 The 10k set is also built in the partitioned layout, and the world holds a counting document for each layout (plan 5.5 Check 10; docs/baselines.md)
- Built: with `--sizes` holding `10k`, the world builds the generated set of that size a second time in the partitioned layout (`LAYOUT_BUILDS`: the replica's build-set of the same generated set, capture on, `--layout partitioned`; the round-robin build was shown equal to Check 9's route in every file a question reads, box job J23, except the harness logs and the preprocess logs, which differ in the harness word and in paths and timings). The build keeps its own chunk-file paths (`pipeline/partitioned/tokenizer-input/<shard>.jsonl`, no copy, `copy_of` naming the same path), is counted by the counting script on that layout, and its chunk files get their tie show. The world file gets, beside the entry of the set (unchanged: its target and key stay), a Dolma entry that names its layout (`layout`: the key of a Dolma entry is its target and its layout), OriginBlame shard entries with that layout, the key `counted_by_layout` (the layout to the counting document; the set's own round-robin document is the same file as `counted` holds for the size, and the map holds only while one size has two builds, which the builder refuses otherwise), and the manifest of the build under the key `set-10k-partitioned`. The runner grades a row that names one layout of such a set against the document counted on that layout (`_graders`), and refuses a world that holds two builds under one key (`check_distinct_builds`).
- Other option: read the partitioned rows of the 10k set from the round-robin build, or leave them out of the pass.
- Result of the other option (traced): `expected._shard_lists` refuses a counting document counted on another layout than the row names, so a row read from the round-robin build has no expected value; leaving the rows out is the layout filter, and it leaves Check 10 open for them (the tools never ran on that layout).
- What would change it: a table row of the 10k set in a third layout, a second size built in two layouts (the map by layout would need the size in its key), or a build of the partitioned layout that Check 9 makes with another route than the replica's build-set.

## D-WORLD-13 The 10k set has a repaired side for each of its builds, made by Check 9's report and repair commands without the reference side (plan 5.5 Check 10; docs/baselines.md)
- Built: for each build of the 10k set (its own round-robin build and the partitioned one of D-WORLD-12) the world runs the tool's `report withdraw A --layout NAME=PREFIX` (the partitioned layout with a placement written from the documented rule, as Check 9 does) and then the replica's `repair --set --layout` on a copy of that build's store, taken with SQLite's backup beside the store (the paths of a store are relative to its folder). The shards of the layout are read from the instances the replica's build-set printed, by the documents' rule for the layout. The repair follows the harness the build's own logs name (the world builds in process; Check 9's historical build is confined: J23 found the two builds of the small set equal in every file). The repaired side's `built-from.jsonl` is named in `subject_inputs.manifest` as `repaired-set-10k` and `repaired-set-10k-partitioned`, the names `subjects.repaired_key` reads. No reference side is built.
- Other option: Check 9's full command, with `--reference-set`, `--reference-out` and `--sides` (docs/interfaces.md 9.5).
- Result of the other option (traced): a reference folder and a sides file that no subject and no grader reads; the repaired side's `built-from.jsonl` is the same file with or without them (9.5: the reference side "is built into its own folder").
- What would change it: a subject or grader that reads the reference side or the sides file, or a table row that asks about the repaired side of a size other than 10k.
- Scheduling: the repair of each layout is one build of the 10k set after the layout's own; it runs in the set's task, after the count and the tie show.

## D-WORLD-14 With an extension on, the main store of the world is the replica's build of that extension, and the repair of main is made on a plain build of main (plan 5.5 Check 10; docs/baselines.md)
- Built: `python -m baselines.world --extensions NAME` names the extensions the world is built for. The extensions the builder has a build for are `BUILT_EXTENSIONS`, which follows `builds.MAIN_WITH_EXTENSION` (today `generating-model` to the replica's key `main+generating-model`: the main build, then release R3 by `build_extension_release`); a name with no build is refused with exit 2 before anything is built. With an extension on, the main store is made by that keyed build, with the same worker count as the other builds, and `world.json` records `"extensions": [...]` (an empty list with none). The repair of the main store is made on a second, plain build of main in the folder of the copy, because `replica repair` knows only the releases of the spec's `[releases.*]` tables and the report of a store that holds R3 names R3 (the first box row with the extension died there with "no release named ['R3']"); the repaired side of main is therefore the one of the world without the extension. The rows of the extension are routed, graded and expected like any row (`expected.py` reads them; the question list adds them only while the extension is on). The world is compared with the plain world of the same worker count by the bars of D-WORLD-8 (the world folder read as a word, the files of the build made without capture and the tie record by their own bars, `world.json` left out of the byte compare and its `stores`, `shards` and `requests` equal): every file the plain world lists is equal, except main's `built-from.jsonl`, which holds the plain rows in the same order with the rows `gen/R3-models` and `R3` added after the rows of the build and before the rows of the checkpoints (the build writes R3's rows inside the build; the runs append the checkpoint rows after it, docs/replica.md section 16); and the paths the plain world does not list are R3's only.
- Other option: serve the rows of the extension from the plain build of main, with no R3.
- Result of the other option (inferred from the rule of the row `c10.c07.R3-release.manifest`, which says "no record: the artifact has no row"; the dry pass with the extension, not of record, read the real cell): the five rows of `c05.withdraw-C-on` ask about `E/shard-3`, which the plain build holds, but `c07.R3.release` for the manifest would find no row for R3 and answer "cannot answer", and so hide the cell the dry pass wrote as wrong: the manifest's walk from R3 reaches no file of a model and reads "does not contain" against the outcome "may contain" of the listed row `c07.R3.release`.
- What would change it: a second extension that needs a build of its own (it is added to `builds.MAIN_WITH_EXTENSION` and `BUILT_EXTENSIONS` follows), a replica repair that knows the extension releases (the plain build of main then goes), or the extension's rows moving into the core table.
- Scheduling: the world with an extension builds main by the keyed build and the repair builds main once more on a plain copy; the test file builds one more world. Box, small set, four workers, dry pass and tests not of record: the test of the extension world took 105 s alone, and the file with all its worlds 688 s alone under a watchdog of 13,000 MB (whole-box peak 7,627 MB); both seen once.

## D-WORLD-15 A pass that asks an extension reads only a world built with it (plan 5.5 Check 10; docs/baselines.md)
- Built: `runner.check_extensions_built(world, extensions)` compares the extensions a pass names (`--extensions`, kept in `extensions.txt` of the run record) with the `extensions` of `world.json` (a world with no such key was built with none). A pass that asks an extension the world was not built with is refused in `main` before a run record is made: it names the extensions and says to build the world with `--extensions`. It is a refusal by `SystemExit`, not exit 3, and it writes no record.
- Other option: ask the rows anyway and let the questions of the extension stay without a line.
- Result of the other option (inferred from the runner's own comments and docstrings, which say a question with no line "stays not attempted"; not run): the questions of the extension would be in the pass's question list and no line would be written for them, so a pass of six subjects would count 54 questions that were never put to a store that holds R3 as not attempted.
- What would change it: a world that holds the files of an extension without the key `extensions` (none is built), or an extension whose rows need no build of their own.
- Scheduling: the check is one comparison at the start of the pass.

## D-WORLD-16 The successor shards of the 10k set's repaired sides are OriginBlame shards of the world, last of all (plan 5.5 Check 10; docs/baselines.md)
- Built: after each repair of the 10k set (the set's own round-robin build and the build added beside it in the partitioned layout, D-WORLD-13) the world reads the successor shards of the repair from what `replica repair` printed (`repaired.files`, the files of the repaired build by registered name, and `kept`): the shards registered under `successor of <name>` that the repair did not keep (a landing shard counts as one). It COPIES the tokenizer input and the two shard files of each into the folder `successor` of the historical build (the route of D-WORLD-9 for the third shard of main), under a name that holds the layout, and writes one shard entry for each (no tie show: the tie record is read for the chunk files of a Dolma run, and these are read by OriginBlame alone), `successor` true, in the layout of its build. The entries come after every other shard of the world, those of the successor of main first, so that no shard number moves and the tracking steps of every earlier shard keep their ids (the steps after the tracking phase move by two for each new shard, a track and a clean: 44 for the twenty-two). The repaired folders are not touched: the files the repair-equality gate compares stay as they are. The rows `c09.10k.successor-shards` of OriginBlame and of the combination are then attempted and read cannot answer with the table's words (D-SUBJ-11), where they stayed not attempted for want of a shard.
- Other option: leave the two questions not attempted and report Check 10 open on them.
- Result of the other option (traced on pass 15, 18 Nov 2026, not of record): world.json held 136 OriginBlame shards and none flagged successor for the 10k set, so the tool was given no shard for the row and its line stayed not attempted; a question never attempted leaves Check 10 open.
- What would change it: a replica repair that prints the successor shards of a set under another key, or a table row that reads a successor shard of the 10k set with a fold the table gives.
- Scheduling: the copies of about twenty-two successor shards of the 10k set add seconds to its task (inferred, NOT MEASURED: the first world with them is the next ungraded pass). Step ids: pass 15 holds 726 steps with the tracking from step 29; the tracking of the new shards adds 44 steps after those of the earlier shards, and every step after the tracking phase moves by 44.

## D-SUBJ-4 AMENDED (18 Nov 2026) Which stops are named (plan 5.5 Check 10; docs/baselines.md)
- Built: `stopped.json` names every stop of the pre-run (every `PreRunStop`, which `run_pass` writes) and every error inside the question loop (the error, the driver, the question). It does NOT name an error that is not a `PreRunStop` and is raised before the question loop: an OSError of the step starter at the probe of a tool's pre-run (box pass 1: the lock prefix could not be executed) leaves a traceback, no `stopped.json` and no summary. The void check (`void_check.py`, start and end facts only) names it: "the runner did not end the pass".
- Other option: wrap every error before the question loop into a named stop.
- Result of the other option (traced): the stop is named in the record; it needs a change of the pre-run and one test per error class, and the void check would still be the check that no pass of record is taken from a run that did not end.
- What would change it: a pass of record whose void check is not applied.

## D-SUBJ-10 A question is read from a build in the layout it names (plan 5.5 Check 10; docs/baselines.md)
- Built: the runner leaves out every question whose listed row names a layout the world does not build for the target (see the section "Layouts the world does not build" of docs/baselines.md) and names it in `layouts-not-built.json` (a new file of the run record); a routed row of two layouts of a subject that reads per layout is read per half (a layout with no build reads None). A row that names ONE layout is read from the run of that layout by every subject, the Dolma combination included (it read the run of the target until pass 15 on the cut tree, 18 Nov 2026, found it at c09.10k.partitioned.kept-shards), and the grader derives a row from the counting document by layout only for the set built in two layouts (the 10k set), never for a row of another size.
- Other option: let a driver read the target's run whatever the layout.
- Result of the other option (traced on box pass 4): the Dolma driver read the round-robin chunk files as the answer to a partitioned question; only the grader's layout guard on one row caught it.
- What would change it: a world that builds both layouts of every set (W1 of the 10k amendment).

## D-SUBJ-11 No fold of several shards that the table does not give (plan 5.5 Check 10; docs/baselines.md)
- Built: the rows `c06.generated.downstream-of-translation` and `c09.10k.successor-shards` give cannot answer for every subject that reads them, with the table's words; OriginBlame's own "any shard contains" is dropped (ruled class 3 by rule on 17 Nov 2026: the table reads the store that tracked THE shard, and the driver tracks each shard into its own store).
- Other option: keep the join.
- Result of the other option (traced on box pass 2 and 3): a line from a fold the table does not give, graded wrong against "may contain".
- What would change it: a table row that gives the fold.

## D-RECORD-1 The kept readings are graded again by a command (plan 5.5 Check 10; docs/baselines.md)
- Built: `python -m baselines.regrade` (see the section of docs/baselines.md): the graders from the expected-value sources the record kept and from the fixture spec and table files of the repository, whose sha256 the record now keeps (`frozen-files.json`, a new file of the run record; a differing file refuses the re-grade, a record without it is labelled "frozen files not recorded"), the record never changed, a stopped or torn record refused, each changed line naming the commit that graded it. With it, the manifest reading "no record" is graded as the answer "not covered" (README, "Readings"); on the record of box pass 3 it changes exactly the two lines that were graded wrong for it.
- Other option: re-run the pass.
- Result of the other option (traced): the tools run again for a defect that is only in a grader.
- What would change it: a reading the record does not keep.

## D-SUBJ-12 A question reads exactly one build (plan 5.5 Check 10; docs/baselines.md)
- Built (scratch, the first part of W8): the Dolma subject can hold a run under the target, or under the target and the layout where a target holds a build for each layout (three words), and a question reads the run of the layout it names (the factory does not build runs per layout yet: no world carries two layouts of a set until W1); the world carries the plant list's split pairs in the entry of EACH set and the Dolma subject reads the list of the question's target (a target with no list is an error, never an empty list); the request files are checked in the builds the questions still in the pass read, for every subject (the helper that maps a request word to its builds counted the questions of OriginBlame alone until 18 Nov 2026; a request file missing from a build the world's entry says holds it stops the pass before any step, named in `stopped.json`; a build the layout filter left out, or a store no question puts the word to, is not checked). The reading of `c03.pairs.split` stays `record` alone, and a note on the line of plain Dolma (not of the combination, whose subject has no hook for it) says whether the id the tool's own row gives at each listed position is the listed member.
- Other option: one list and one run per target for every size. Result of the other option (traced on box pass 9, the 100k world): the world kept the smallest set's list, so the 100k questions were put against the small set's pairs and the reading was the small one, shard and line for shard and line (the 100k positions hold other records).
- What would change it: a stronger reading that compares the tool's row ids with the pair's member ids (the table's reading of this row is the chunk file and line of the tied row; moving it is the owner's). The owner's yes of 14 Nov 2026 to the tie by src and loc is recorded at WATERWHITE_BUILD_PLAN.md line 305 and line 370 (decision 5); it covers the tie, not what the `right` of this row proves.
- Disclosure for the verdict walk: by the table's own tie (the document with ordinal n is the row whose src is the shard's chunk file and whose loc is n + 1) the line a Dolma reading names equals the listed ordinal plus one whenever the row exists, and the list is the plant list the expected value comes from; the "right" of `c03.pairs.split` shows only that the toolkit wrote a tied row at each listed position.
- Disclosure for the verdict walk (second pin of test_world): the 100k case of the per-set split_pairs pin (each set's own list against its own counting document) rests on box pass 10 alone (5,052 pairs at 100k, both right), because the default test world builds the small size only; the 10k case runs on the box rows of the world builds.

## D-CELL-1 The harness of the cell runs: in-process for the 240, the confined harness measured once beside them (plan 5.6, 5.4; docs/replica.md section 17; D-30)
- Built: every capture-on run of the 240 runs `build-set --generation-harness in-process`, and its record carries the word "in-process" and the statement that an in-process log establishes no bound. The confined harness (`--generation-harness confined`) is measured once, for the middle cell, at both worker settings, three runs each, as a separate figure with its own label, outside the 240.
- Other option: run the capture-on halves of the 240 with the confined harness.
- Result of the other option (traced from `replica/capture_hooks.py`, `_generate_confined`: one sandboxed program per generation job): every capture-on figure would carry the cost of starting a sandbox for each job, and the figures of the common route, where a team calls the wrappers in its own process, would not be measured.
- What would change it: the confined figure showing a cost small enough to fold into the 240, or a requirement that confinement is the route every capture-on run takes.

## D-CELL-2 What "workers" means in a cell: `build-set --jobs`, with Megatron's own value stated beside it (plan 5.6; docs/replica.md sections 9 and 17)
- Built: the worker setting of a cell is `python -m replica build-set --jobs 1` and `--jobs 4`, the number of tokenizer runs at a time. Every record also states Megatron's own workers value, read from the run's settings: the `[megatron]` table's `workers` of the tokenizer spec, read as `tokenizer.workers` by `replica.plan.load_tokenizer_settings` (1 in the fixture spec), and names that place in `megatron_workers_from`.
- Other option: take Megatron's own workers value as the worker setting, by writing it into the tokenizer spec before each run.
- Result of the other option (traced: `tokenizer_digest` hashes the whole spec file, and the matched values of a pair include that digest): the spec file would differ between the settings, so the matched tokenizer value would differ between the runs of {1, 4} and a pair's two halves could only be matched within one setting, and the number of tokenizer runs at a time would stay at the build's own default.
- What would change it: the project owner naming Megatron's own value as the axis of plan 5.6.

## D-CELL-3 The 240 runs are the grid's runs and no more (plan 5.6; docs/replica.md section 17)
- Built: the grid is 20 cells (18 at 10k records in the partitioned layout, the middle cell in the round-robin layout, the middle cell at 100k records), each with 12 runs (workers {1, 4} x capture {off, on} x three paired runs). The run count 240 is derived from the grid by `run_plan()`, and `grid` prints it.
- Other option: cross every cell with both layouts and both sizes (54 cells, 648 runs, by arithmetic from the same factors).
- Result of the other option (inferred): figures for the effect of the layout and of the size at every cell, where the plan's grid gives them at the middle cell only; nothing in the 240 would be left out.
- What would change it: a first result in which the layout or the size changes a figure outside the middle cell.

## D-CELL-4 The measurement runs on the server, which departs from risk RK3 (plan 5.6; docs/replica.md section 17)
- Built: every record names the machine: role "server", the type as `--host-type` gives it, cores, memory and operating system read from the machine when the batch starts (never typed), and the tree id. Plan risk RK3 expects a laptop workload only; the project owner decided on 17 Nov 2026 to measure on the server, and the section of the documents and this entry say so.
- Other option: measure on the laptop, as RK3 reads.
- Result of the other option (inferred): figures from a machine that its owner's other programs share at the same time, with timed runs and the owner's work as neighbours, and at most a few runs at once.
- What would change it: the project owner returning to the laptop, or a comparison of the two machines that the plan asks for.
- On 19 Nov 2026 the first batch ran on a server with 16 virtual CPUs visible to the operating system. The caller identified them as shared. Activity from other tenants on the physical host was unknown (docs/measurements.md, "Conditions").

## D-CELL-5 The runner yields between runs, and a start the hook refuses is not a failed run (plan 5.6; docs/replica.md section 17)
- Built: `--before-run COMMAND` is called before each run (each group of `--at-once` runs) and its exit status and the first 200 characters of its output go into the run's `hold` field; status 0 lets the run start. Any other status stops the batch cleanly: the runs of that group get a file in `held/` and no record in `records/`, so a resumed batch starts them. `--after-run COMMAND` is called after each run or group, also after a failure or an interrupt. The commands are the caller's; the box protocol is not in the repository.
- Other option: write the refused start as a failed run record.
- Result of the other option (traced from the resume rule: a record with a non-zero exit is never run again without `rerun`): every refusal of the hook would turn a run that never started into a failed run that has to be named again by hand.
- What would change it: a hook that can refuse for a reason that belongs to the run itself.

## D-CELL-6 The level test judges by the alone runs' own band (plan 5.6; docs/replica.md section 17)
- Built: `level` takes three alone runs for each of `--jobs 1` and `--jobs 4` and the runs at the rising levels, and reports for each setting the highest level whose runs all keep throughput, query time and walk time inside the minimum to maximum of the alone runs, both ends included. It lists every level with the figures that left the band, answers nothing with fewer than three alone runs, and names the heavier cells as not covered.
- Other option: judge each level by a tolerance around the alone runs' median.
- Result of the other option (inferred): the width of the band would be a number chosen in advance and not what the alone runs show, so a noisy machine and a quiet one would be held to the same width.
- What would change it: alone runs so close together that the band is narrower than the resolution of the figures.

## D-CELL-7 Wall times come from runs with no sampler (plan 5.6; docs/replica.md section 17)
- Built: the memory sampler reads the process tree every 0.1 s. A run made with `--sampler` states `sampler: true`, holds the peak memory, and is left out of the median throughput; a run with no sampler reads peak memory "not measured".
- Other option: sample every run.
- Result of the other option (inferred): the sampling reads /proc ten times a second beside the build, so the wall times and the throughput of every run would carry the reading's own cost.
- What would change it: a measured difference between runs with and without the sampler that is inside the spread of the alone runs.

## D-E2-1 The generating-model rule: condition (h) per record, the temporal ancestry cycle, the closure with the models' ancestry, release R3 in the build (plan 1.4, 2.1, 2.2(h), 2.4, 3.3, Decision 28, L11, F10, Section 7, D-11)

Supersedes the generating-model clause of D-95 (the refusal of a negative past a generation edge while the switch is on) and touches D-11, D-161, D-CORE-1 and D-CORE-3 only by reference: the switch is still the store setting `extension.generating-model`, off when missing; a switch that cannot be read still stops every answer first; a stored log is still marked compared before it establishes a bound. Built as a switch, off by default. With the switch off every printed answer, refusal, note, scope, exit code and help line is the core's.

- Built (the rule). Condition (h) is applied in `coverage._walk.Session._edge`, per record, at the branch where a generation edge does not name the requested source and its bound has just been shown established (condition (g) first, so a log whose texts were not shown and compared answers "upper bound not established" and never reaches (h)). The model is read from the edge's STORED harness log, never from the job's own statement. "none" lets the record stand. A registered checkpoint (the artifact the text names among its own names has the kind checkpoint; an instance id, a digest or a path does not find a model) is answered for the same request, in the same session, by `Session.outcome`; its answer "does not contain" lets the record stand, "contains" or "may contain" gives "may contain", `generating model exposed`, condition h, the checkpoint's name in `failing.artifact`, the job in `failing.job`. A checkpoint with no record of its own gives `upstream record missing` (reason "record missing", no condition, as the core gives a missing record). Any other text, a release, a source, a shard or a mixture name included, gives `generating model not registered`, condition h, with the text exactly as stored in `failing.artifact`. A log with no model entry is "not established", never "no model": `generating-model entry missing`, condition h, the job in `failing.job`. The list form the harness writes (a JSON list of two or more distinct non-empty identifiers, none of them "none") is judged element by element in listed order and the first that fails gives the link; any other text that starts with "[" is one text and is not registered. A recorded "contains" keeps its place: (h) is asked only where the walk would otherwise give a negative for the edge. The refusal of today is removed from `coverage._conclude`; the note "generating-model exposure not assessed" is made only when the query's switch snapshot is off.
- Built (the record level). The verdict is made where the walk meets the edge, so the artifact's answer, the listing and the rebuild report's per-record entries agree: the report lists a record that fails (h) as unresolved and names the cause, and no successor is clean while one remains. `rebuild/` is not changed.
- Built (a publisher's pointer). With the switch on, a record whose edge is a publisher's pointer and whose parents do not name the requested source answers "may contain", `generating model not registered`, condition h, the job (the name of the edge's step); the link is the record's own because a pointer names no model. With the switch off the answer is condition g as before.
- Built (the switch). The switch is compared with "on" once when a `Session` begins and that one value serves the closure, condition (h) and the note. Every entry point has already refused a switch it cannot read, so the comparison adds no refusal. A write to the store after the query began already ends the query, so no answer mixes two switch states.
- Built (the cycle). A registered model checkpoint whose own history reads a record that the model's own output feeds is a temporal ancestry cycle. The session keeps the set of checkpoints whose answer is being made (released in a `finally`) and a list of the cycles it met. Condition (h) that finds its model in the set does not ask again and does not raise: it returns the failed link "generating model exposed" and notes the cycle. The error `temporal ancestry cycle` (a refused request, exit code 2, no answer printed; `errors.TemporalAncestryCycle`, a name and a detail sentence) is raised by `coverage.prove` at the outermost exit of an artifact's answer, when the finished verdict is not "contains" and a record met the cycle, and never inside a nested answer: a recorded "contains" of the same artifact wins and is answered. A verdict computed while a cycle was met enters neither the record memo nor the outcome cache (a "contains" does, it does not depend on the cycle). The listing, the report's artifact entries and `verify`'s artifact answers reach the error through `coverage.answer` and stop at the first artifact whose answer rests on it; a record entry of the report that rests on it reads "unresolved".
- Built (the closure). With the switch on, the ancestor closure of the asked artifact for the loud checks and for `files_not_checked` holds every registered checkpoint that a generation log in the closure names as its model, with the ancestry of each, to a fixpoint (`Store.check_integrity(..., models=True)`, `Store.missing_files(..., models=True)`, `_closure`); the one function that reads a model text is `Store.resolve_model` (`store/_reads.py`), used by the walk and by the closure. A pass made before the switch was written no longer holds (the store's mark changed). With the switch off the closure and every call are the core's.
- Built (release R3 in the build). `replica.fixture.build_table_store(folder, "main+generating-model")` is the main build followed by the R3 step (`replica.capture_hooks.build_extension_release`): the four jobs of fixture/sources/generation_r3.jsonl run in the confined harness with a model client that reports the row's model, are registered with the texts they were handed (so each log is marked compared), the log of the job `model-entry-removed` has its model entry line removed from the harness's own file and the texts re-attached, and release R3 copies the four lines. The key is outside `TABLE_STORES`; the main build is made exactly as before and writes no file of R3's.
- Not built in this round: the write-time refusal of a record-level cycle, the pairing of (h) with a content ban or flagged records, the published-model branch, and the finished-verdict method of those extensions. The cycle is a limit of the build until the write-time refusal exists: a store can hold one, written by any path; every query that rests on it is refused with the named error; a listing or a report of a store whose reachable artifacts rest on a cycle stops at the first such artifact; a record entry of the report that rests on it reads "unresolved".

### The owner's answers applied
- The extension cases are in the frozen table and are subjects of Check 10 (the owner's first answer of 18 Nov 2026).
- A log whose texts were not shown and compared establishes no bound, so condition (g) comes before (h) and the registration of every log the rule builds carries its texts (his answer on compared texts).
- A switch file that cannot be read stops every answer, one that passes no generation edge included (his answer on the broken switch).

### Taken under the owner's delegation of 18 Nov 2026
The rule of the delegation: no "does not contain" without a proof that covers it, no refusal dropped; where it does not decide, the reading that names more records; the other option's result stands beside each point.
- A log's model text is the checkpoint's own registered name (looked up by name, kind checkpoint). Other option: a registry in the store that maps a model identifier to a checkpoint. Result: a log naming a hosted model identifier would find a checkpoint if the registry says so, a store with no registry rows would read every model as not registered even a log naming a registered checkpoint, and it needs a schema step and a write method. No alias is read: a hosted name never follows the newest checkpoint silently.
- The list form is judged element by element; the first failing model gives the link. Other options: the log counts as not established (the answer is "may contain" for the source even when every listed model is clean, with a cause word the plan does not fix); or the request is refused with a named error and no answer is printed past that edge.
- A text naming an artifact that is not a checkpoint is not a model. Other option: any registered artifact counts. Result: a release or a shard could stand in for the model that made a record, and the negative would rest on an artifact that holds no history of a model.
- The loud checks run over the models' ancestry on every query (the closure above). Other option: the closure of today. Result: a file missing from a model's history is not named in `files_not_checked`, and a changed byte in a shard that only a model's history reads is not met by the loud checks of the asked artifact; the answer then rests on the generation edge and on the model's own answer alone.
- A cycle is a refused request, not a failed link. Other option: a failed link ("may contain" naming the model). Result: no query is stopped, no error class and no exit code exist, and a store holding the cycle answers every artifact; the delegation took the refusal because a cycle is invalid evidence.

### Decided on 18 Nov 2026 by the lead, after the reviewer's answer; the rule, with the other option's result beside it
- The cycle is raised at the finish of an artifact's answer and a recorded "contains" wins. Other options: a loud integrity fault (exit code 3): the artifact holding both a cycle record and a recorded "contains" would print the error and no answer, and a store holding a reachable cycle would stop every listing, every report and every `verify` that reads the artifact. Walk once and take the first answer: the output can answer "does not contain" or "contains" depending on the order of the walk.
- A publisher's pointer with the switch on answers "may contain", `generating model not registered`, condition h (plan 3.3: the model is not registered, so (h) cannot be met). Other option: condition g first. Result: the answer names "upper bound not established" for a record whose cause is the unregistered model.
- The verdict of a registered checkpoint that (h) reads is its finished request-specific verdict. For a withdrawal the finished verdict is the walk's verdict. Other options: the raw verdict (a record past a raw-clear checkpoint could read "keep" in the report while its artifact answers "may contain"); the finished verdict named as the model (every output past a registered checkpoint would answer "generating model exposed" for a request that is incomplete in itself).
- A record-level answer of R3 is read from the rebuild report's record entries (action "keep" as "does not contain", "unresolved" as "may contain", with the entry's `names` and `failing`), valid for R3's four independently identified records only. Other option: a new command that prints a record's answer. Result: `waterwhite --help` and the usage line would gain a command with every extension off, and its refusal order would fall under the broken-switch rule.
- The table holds one more row than the eight of this extension: the standing negative with the switch on (the same question with the switch off is a core row). The model-history loud stop has no table row (dropped by decision); it is pinned only by the lane's own test E2-W18 (tests/extensions/test_e2_walk_rules.py). The cycle row waits for the printed name of the error.

### Signed by text
- Plan line 181: the model is read from the stored log; "none" or a registered checkpoint that answers "does not contain". Line 228: a registered model is an ancestor of its outputs, one that is not registered is "not covered" and its outputs answer "may contain" naming it; a log with no model entry is "not established" and the answer names the job and the missing entry; where a publisher's pointer is the only evidence the model is not registered. Line 183: a recorded chain showing "contains" wins over a failed condition elsewhere; the loud faults stop the query. Line 168: the words "generating-model exposure not assessed" belong to the extension being off. Line 189 and plan 1.4: no combination is refused by name; each rule applies as written.

### Forms decided
The cause words, the condition letter and the `names` words are the vocabulary's; no word, command, field or schema step is added. The printed name of the cycle error, its detail sentence and every sentence a person reads that this change adds were shown to the owner and stand as written. The switch is turned on only by writing the store setting. The model's resolution lives in `store/_reads.py`; the closure argument is `models=True` and is passed only with the switch on. The cause words are not asserted by their sentence, only by name, condition, job and, for a model that is not registered, the artifact.

### Other option
Keep the refusal (D-95) until all the extensions that interact with it are built. Result: the five rows of release R3 and the three rows of the removed model entry could not answer, and every negative past a generation edge with the switch on would be refused whatever its model.

### What would change it
The write-time refusal of a record-level cycle; the content ban and flagged records built (the pairing of (h) with a ban: a ban's negative past a generation edge needs the model to answer "does not contain" for the same ban, and an unmatched ban or an incomplete flagged request must name its own link, never the model); the published-model branch (a model that is a published model, never clean alone); a registry of models if the owner asks for one.

### Checks
`tests/extensions/test_e2_walk_rules.py`, `test_e2_r3_library.py`, `test_e2_table_rows.py` and `test_e2_off_state.py`; the replaced test of `tests/coverage/test_coverage_proof.py`, which pinned "switching on is refused because (h) is not built" and now pins that with every model "none" the standing negative stands with no exposure note; the rows `c05.g.model-entry-removed.on.E/shard-3` and the five `c07.R3` rows of the table. The pins of the off state stay unedited.
